## Summary

In-World Instruments & Inspect View draws every reading the ship itself produces (wall and panel dials, tank sight-glasses, state glyphs, housing glow marks and a module's own state frames) as lit sprites that can be read only where the player's chest beam, a fixture or a thrown glowstick reaches them, plus a precise, verdict-free inspect card for one lit instrument at a time. It asks the Perception Filter for the value the local player is shown and draws it with three honest or dishonest motions (a step, then rest for a real change and a heavier damped settle for a real jump; a tremor under real stress; a never-resting glide for a lie), carrying the lie's violet edge and nothing else; it simulates, persists and decides nothing. Read it if you are building anything that draws a gauge or the card, authoring instrument content or a lie target, or checking that no schematic, status screen or condition word has crept into the game.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Presentation` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Life-Support Graph (designed)`, `2D Lighting & Occlusion (designed in this run)`, `Perception Filter (designed in this run)`, `Interaction System (designed in this run)`, `Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query (designed)`, `Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera (designed)`, `Player Controller & Input (designed; reply owed)`, `HUD & Suit Layer (designed in this run)`, `Debug Overlay (designed in this run)`, `Accessibility Settings (undesigned)`

## Overview

**What it is, in one sentence.** In-World Instruments & Inspect View is the presentation system that draws every reading the ship itself produces — dials, tank sight-glasses, state glyphs, housing glow marks and a module's own state frames — as lit sprites on the walls and machines of the world (layer 0), and draws the same instrument again at HUD size as the inspect card (layer 2) when the player stands in its light and asks for a precise reading.

**The data layer.** It simulates nothing and persists nothing. Each frame, for each instrument on screen, it asks the Perception Filter for the value the local player is shown (`perceived(ref, viewer)`, PF-7), maps the returned per-mille reading to a needle angle, a meniscus row or a digit string through a per-instrument calibration record (the answer to PF-45), and draws it with one of the three motion signatures of art bible 7.3: **settle** (a damped step for an honest jump, drawn on the bus-load dial only at MVP, D.4) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, **tremor** (an irregular shake around a true value while the ship is really under stress) or **glide** (the Filter's uncaused, never-resting drift of a lie). It reads the Life-Support Graph's `read_view` only for non-value inputs (which honest frame a module shows, whether the bound subject is under physical stress, whether an alarm is eligible, whether the binding still exists), never calls `true_value`, and never decides whether, when, for whom or by how much anything lies: that is the Filter's and Sanity's. It registers **zero** sim blocks, hooks, mutators, commands or persisted fields; it is a `PRESENTATION` subscriber (KRN:106-110; PIPE PR-8).

**The player-facing effect.** The player meets it as the ship's own voice, read by their own light. A gauge on a wall is readable exactly as far as the beam, a fixture, a lantern pool or a Chemlight glowstick reaches it ("What You Light, You Can Read"); the needle sits in a zone band coded by pattern as well as hue, with no numerals at game-camera distance; the player reads cause, mass and rest at a glance (a needle that steps to a new reading and comes to rest, and eases heavily when the change is a real jump; a needle that shakes under real stress) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* and learns the one motion that has no cause, no mass and no rest: the lie. When precision matters they stand still in their own light and press `inspect`: a card appears beside the instrument carrying its label, the same needle and zone band, and one numeral with its unit — never a state word or a verdict. The card is the same instrument, not a second one: it lies exactly when its wall gauge lies (advisor ruling R13), so inspecting is a precision tool and never a free falsifier. The real check is always a different reading elsewhere or the consequence channel the Graph and Failure & Trail draw (frost, drip, draught, hiss, breath, a rotor's sound).

**What the game loses without it.** The core hypothesis — a tense 30-plus-minute solo session over a visible life-support graph — has no surface on which the graph can be read or lied through. The MVP hallucination is class (b) only (CON "MVP Definition" item 5; SN-16), and class (b) lives on exactly the surfaces owned here (gauge needle, the card's numeral as a mode of the needle's lie, state glyph, lamp-housing glow frame, machine state frame, inspect card; PF-5 row 20). Without it the falsification economy cannot run, the Graph's third diegetic surface (LSG:996-1003) does not exist, and the gamepad has no HR-14 mirror card.

**Scope — "module-body reading surfaces" (advisor ruling R7, R24).** This GDD owns (a) every gauge, dial, level sight-glass and state glyph produced by the ship or a derelict, (b) the inspect card, and (c) the bounded **Module-Body Presenter**: the *selection* among a module sheet's honest state frames (running/stopped, whole/cracked, lamp lit/unlit, WORN wear) and the drawing of the state glyph. It owns no module art (Item DB `sprite_family`), no placement (Layout and Derelict Authoring), no module behaviour, and nothing suit-produced or hand-held (Suit & Equipment and HUD & Suit Layer own those, including the field-check reading, advisor ruling R15). It draws **no schematic, overlay, minimap, ship-status or loop-summary screen, ever** (advisor ruling R16; LSG:51 anti-pillar 3: "Precision exists only as the lit inspect card of a specific instrument").

**MVP scope.** MVP-live: DIAL_L, DIAL_S and the LINEAR tank sight-glass with honest settle, tremor and the stress notch; the inspect card (label, dial, numeral); the gleam on wall and card; the in-world alarm mark on DIAL_L, panel and tank housings; honest WORN wear and the DEAD frame; the stage-2 state glyph and housing pip; the stage-3 machine-frame selection for lie-capable modules (SN-16 reach); the 18-instrument indicative manifest on the LSG slice (home 9, A 4, B 5 for a seat with a usable scanner; the per-site range with and without one is in IW-29) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; BLOCKING 1)*; single seat. Later: co-op seat behaviour (E-IW-57, E-IW-58, AC-IW-85 exercised at the co-op tier), `strain` dials (OQ-IW-5), conditional `CARD_MAG` 2 (OQ-IW-15), powered gauges (OQ-IW-11), card navigation, the channelled INSPECT verb (VS-A), and the Accessibility Settings screen-reader text (Alpha).

**Index wording, reconciled.** IDX:39 says: "Owns the gauge, sight-glass and dial nodes as lit sprites and the inspect view (the same instrument magnified); asks the Perception Filter for the displayed value and draws it with the settle/tremor/glide motion signatures. *Why this tier:* Where class (b) lies live; pairs with the Failure & Trail System as the truth check." Reconciled with the rulings: "sight-glass" means the fill-level reading of a tank (the flow pattern and moving medium on pipes are world-state witnesses drawn by Lighting and Failure & Trail, advisor ruling R8); "the same instrument magnified" means the same ref, the same drawn value and the same leak, shown at HUD scale `H` with a label and a numeral (advisor ruling R2, R13); "pairs with Failure & Trail as the truth check" means the two never share a node (FT:314). The index's dependency list (Life-Support Graph, 2D Lighting & Occlusion, Perception Filter, Interaction System) is accurate but incomplete; the missing edges are listed in Dependencies and flagged, not edited (advisor ruling R26).

**Dependency status at authoring (Glob of `design/gdd/`, 2026-10-27).** Life-Support Graph — designed (locked). 2D Lighting & Occlusion, Perception Filter, Interaction System — designed in this run. Player Controller & Input, Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera, Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query, Sanity & Hallucination Rules, Item & Module Database, Failure & Trail, Derelict Authoring, Ship Section & Layout, Boarding & Salvage, Web Map & Transit, Suit & Equipment, Adaptive Audio & Tells, Attention, Horror — on disk and read where they name this system. Chart Table & Menus — designed (boundary only). **HUD & Suit Layer and Debug Overlay (True Values) — designed in this run** (header Status Designed; re-read 2026-10-27 for HS-3, HS-14, HS-33, HS-47, HS-52, HS-56, HS-57, DO-12, DO-22: they agree with IW-14, IW-22, IW-23, IW-35 and IW-38 apart from the HS-52 cursor, OQ-IW-21, and the HUD-owned counting unit, OQ-IW-9). Accessibility Settings — **has no file** (`(provisional — Accessibility Settings undesigned)`). No ADRs and no `design/ux/instruments.md` exist yet (LSG:1003 asks for the latter before epics).

## Player Fantasy

### Framing (direct, per Category UI)

**You read a dying ship by its own needles, in your own light, and you learn which needles to trust.** The player is not a captain at a status board; there is no board. They are the person who walks up to a wall in the dark, lets the chest lamp fall across a scratched bezel, watches a needle step to its new reading and come to rest, and knows from angle and zone alone that the reservoir is low. When precision matters they stand still in their own light and bring the instrument close. And at some point the needle starts to slide with nothing in the ship to explain it, a thin violet edge sits on the glass, and the player has to decide whether to believe the ship or the gauge. The answer the game teaches is always the same: **believe the ship** (P1). This system is where that sentence becomes something the player holds.

*Framing choice (logged).* Three candidate framings were weighed: (a) **direct — "the ship's own voice, read by your light"**, (b) the mechanic's glance (fluency), (c) the doubting reader (epistemics). (a) is chosen because it is the only framing whose design pressure points the same way as every locked guard rail (LSG:51 no schematic, ART 3.1 no numerals at distance, EM-22 no dwell, PF-4 default truth, the card-is-the-gauge rule). (b) and (c) are real feelings that appear *as the session arc* — (b) mid-session, as reading becomes fluent, (c) late and deep, as the glance stops being enough — but neither is a design driver: (b) would push toward numerals on world gauges and a status glance (P3 and P1 strain), (c) toward a lie detector and a "lies caught" score (P1 and P5 strain). Fantasy-as-role ("be a starship engineer") is deliberately not first: it would push toward more instruments and a console to sit at. The fantasy is the act of *reading under doubt*, which needs few instruments, each one meaningful.

**MDA rank, local to this system.** (1) Discovery: finding out what the ship is doing, and whether the reading is lying. (2) Challenge delivered as Competence: reading cause, mass and rest at a glance and choosing the cheapest truth check. (3) Fellowship *(co-op tier; the MVP is single-seat, so solo the cross-check is a second dial, the scanner or the witness, *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*)*: the cross-check. P4's "Crewmates Are Instruments" lives here: a teammate's body is the honest second reading ("you're fogging up"), a teammate's beam keeping my gauge lit (IW-25) is a shared act of reading, and, in co-op, the cheapest truth check is often a question asked across a room. (4) Sensation: the settle, the tremor, the tick of a digit, the gleam on glass — instruments are the most tactile UI in the game, but Sensation is deliberately ranked fourth so the presentation layer never drifts toward the "juicy UI" that ART 7.3 rejected. **SDT:** Autonomy — the player chooses where to spend light, whether to inspect, which truth check to pay for; Competence — the motion grammar is learnable and consistent; Relatedness — in co-op the other player's body is the honest cross-check ("you look cold" costs only light, ART:738), and no instrument ever reads a crewmate: the crewmate is the instrument.

### Pillar lines this system embodies (verbatim)

| Pillar | Verbatim line | What the player physically does |
|---|---|---|
| P1 The World Never Lies | "Only instruments and perception can be wrong." Design test: "a hidden stat vs. a visibly leaking pipe -> the pipe" | reads a gauge, then confirms it against frost, drip, breath, draught or a rotor's sound; the instrument is the one thing allowed to be wrong |
| P4 Sanity Rewrites Reality | "(b) False system states. Broken reads as fixed, fixed as broken; the lie extends from the gauge to the sprite." / "No lie is unfalsifiable ... checking costs time, risk or trust." Secondary test: "-10% move speed vs. a gauge showing a false value -> the false gauge" | catches a reading that glides, falsifies it by walking to the thing the gauge is about, by a second instrument or by a crewmate |
| P3 The Web Watches (Visual Identity Anchor) | "What You Light, You Can Read." "Light is a resource you spend; inside your light, every pipe is a sentence; outside it, the dark is honest." | spends light and stillness to read; a dark gauge is unreadable, never "unknown" |
| P2 Dread Before Chaos (serves) | "Every breach is preceded by a tell" | sees an honest needle shake under real stress; the shake is the instrument's share of the tell |

Art bible 7.3 already wrote the lesson in one line and it is quoted here as the player-facing test: **"A player who learns 'needles that wander are lying; needles that shake are warning' has learned Pillar 2 and Pillar 4 from the same gauge."**

### Design tests (this system's own, pillar format)

> *A reading the player can trust by looking at the screen vs. a reading the player must spend light, stillness or a walk to trust -> the second.*

1. **Card vs gauge.** A card more trustworthy than its wall gauge vs. a card that is exactly the gauge, magnified -> exactly the gauge (R1, R13; ART:747; SN-16 AD-C1).
2. **Cause of a doubt.** A UI element that tells the player a reading is lying vs. a world the player can walk to that disagrees with the reading -> the world. Any feature that signals a lie without a world cause or the HR-5 leak (a "suspect" icon, a confidence colour, a stability readout) fails (P1; PF-38).
3. **Motion grammar.** A needle that moves for a reason the player cannot find vs. a needle whose every honest motion has a cause, has mass and rests -> the second; and the one motion with no cause, no mass and no rest is the lie (ART 7.3).
4. **Precision vs exposure.** Precision at a distance vs. precision that needs the instrument inside your own light -> the second (P3; R4, R20). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* Under the ratified rules the card itself is free to open and to close (D94, no Attention stimulus, IW-24), so the cost belongs to reading, not to the card: the player must stand with the gauge lit, in a dark room, for as long as they read. Opening the card is harmless to spam because of card parity (it is never a falsifier, IW-26); the exposure the player feels comes from the dark room and from standing lit.

### Anchor moments (one ladder, three beats)

Ids are real MVP ids from Derelict Authoring and the Graph slice; times are illustrative; every number quoted is an owner value.

**Beat A, the anchor: the honest read in the dark (minute 2 of a fresh wake, sanity stage 0).** `cryo_bay` is in the FROST band (authored T -2000 mC, DA:197; LS-7 FROST is -15000..-1 mC, LSG:76); the ship is dark except the player's chest beam.

| t (s) | What happens | Rule it shows |
|---|---|---|
| 0.0 | The player turns; the chest beam (from the per-facing chest point, PC) slides across the north wall. A dial housing is visible as geometry; its face is unreadable. | P3: the dark is honest, not hidden; the housing is physical (ART 7.0) |
| 0.3 | The beam crosses `LIT_ON_PM` 150 on the gauge (EM-22). The face is readable; the prompt plate shows the keybind and a state-neutral verb. | What You Light, You Can Read |
| 0.3-0.6 | The needle is simply where the reading is. No settle on becoming lit: a settle is an honest *change*, not a reveal. | ART 7.3; IW-12 |
| 1.0 | The needle sits in the cold zone (a hatched band, readable without hue). The player looks down: frost on the pod rims, the drip trail (consequence channel). Reading and witness agree; the needle does not tremble: a cold band is a state, not a stress. | P1 design test: the pipe |
| 2.0 | The player presses `inspect`. The card opens beside the gauge: a label, the same needle and zones, one numeral with its unit. No word such as "cold" or "FROST". | precision needs the gauge in the beam *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| 2.0-6.0 | Movement intent is zeroed on entry and the first step past the dead-zone closes the card and moves in the same frame (IW-25); the sim keeps ticking. The player stands still, lit, in a dark room. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | P3: reading spends light and restraint |
| 6.0 | The player turns away. The light leaves the gauge, `lit` falls below `LIT_OFF_PM` 120 and the card closes at once; the face goes dark. | EM-22: no dwell |

*What the player should feel:* competent and careful. Any exposure they feel comes from the dark room and from standing lit while they read, not from the card, which costs nothing to open or close *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. They learned three things without a tutorial line: light makes it readable; the reading agrees with the frost; reading precisely means keeping the gauge in the beam.

**Beat B: the first lie (stage 1, `engine_room`, the home loop running fault-free).** The first lie in any world is FALSE_FAIL or GLIDE on a fault-free lit subject with all three leak modes (SN-16). The oxygen-partial-pressure dial `inst_engine_room_p_o2` reads NORMAL; the home loop is running, fault-free. About ten seconds after the player enters, the needle begins to slide toward the alarm side with nothing in the room to explain it, never resting (at an 18 art px needle the Filter's `rate_floor` is 1.59 deg/s, PF-17, so about 16 degrees in 10 s); a thin violet fringe (`#B07BE6`, never the core `#8A3FD1`) sits on the glass edge; a faint wrong-pitched tick sounds (`FA_LIE_SIG_GAUGE`, PF-20). Each mode alone is sufficient (HR-5). The player opens the card and **the card glides too**, with the same gleam: precision did not save them (R13). They check the cheapest truth: their own breath, the room's witness, a second reading (`suit.o2` for the room they stand in, which counts because the first lie is ALARM-direction, or another compartment's gauge, SN-15 L-2I) or a `field_check` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; BLOCKING 1)*. The lie returns to truth on the glide path, unannounced, identically whether it expired or was debunked; the game never says "that was a hallucination". *Feeling:* a cold prickle, then the satisfaction of catching it with the world. P4 test: "a hallucination that can never be disproved vs. one that costs time, risk or trust to disprove -> the costly one."

**Beat C: the lie that can kill (stage 2 or later, a remote reading).** L-G forbids a FALSE_OK on the quantity of the compartment the seat stands in while that band is outside OK (SN-15 L-G; the earlier SN:260 line cite is stale *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*), and L-S keeps lethal faults out of any comfort-direction lie (not only FALSE_OK) below `LETHAL_FAULT_MIN_STAGE` 2 (SN-15 L-S and Sanity's Tuning Knobs table; the earlier SN:1054 cite is stale), so the lethal lie is always a **remote** reading. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Worked fixtures** (proposed placements; the hop counts are verified against the Derelict Authoring room trees, DA C.2 and C.3; placement itself stays Derelict Authoring's and Layout's). **Derelict B.** `inst_b_dock_p_o2`, a DIAL_L on the `b_spine` wall beside `door_b_dock_spine` (`mount_room` `b_spine`), reads the `p_o2` of `b_dock` (`subject_room`): `hops` = 1 (tree `b_dock - b_spine`), inside `WITNESS_HOPS_MAX` 2. After the weld the O2-ROOM seam leak at the `airlock` / `b_dock` seam is on the `lethal_fault_modes` list (Sanity SN-15 L-S) and the gauge shows NORMAL (FALSE_OK). **Derelict A.** `inst_a_machine_T`, a DIAL_L in `a_spine` beside the CLOSED `door_a_spine_machine`, reads the `T` of `a_machine` (-22000 mC, the one lethal-cold room, DA-28): `hops` = 1 (tree `a_dock - a_spine - {a_life, a_machine}`), and its witness is the frost fan reaching the door sill. That lie is a cold-band lie and not a `lethal_fault_modes` fault; whether Sanity gates it as lethal is OQ-IW-35. Both gauges are the Beat C fixtures of AC-IW-109. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; BLOCKING 1)* Both are REMOTE and take a COMFORT-direction lie, for which the suit is never a second reading (SN-15 L-2I) and a REMOTE row gets no suit credit at all, so each fixture is **scanner-dependent**: the lie is drawn only for a seat that carries `tool_scanner` and is not hauling a module (SE-11: `equip_tool` is `BUSY` while carrying). A seat that walks toward the gauge with a module in hand is not lied to on it. A fixed second `p_o2` dial in `b_spine` would make fixture B seat-independent (the IW-29 ask); fixture A stays scanner-dependent (OQ-IW-35). The home ship has no lethal-class remote fixture in this manifest: `inst_cryo_bay_p_o2` hangs in the room it reads and so cannot produce Beat C. A witness is active in the fault's compartment within `WITNESS_HOPS_MAX` 2 (Sanity-owned, L-W): a hiss at the seam, a draught at the door, the strain sound. The player who trusts the gauge walks in without topping up and the room is DECOMP; the body counts honestly. Per the binding user decision ("death is a bitch. It happens."), a player who ignores the hiss can die, and at MVP restarts from the last autosave (Save/Load). The player who listens at the door, walks to the witness, scans the gauge or reads a second fixed dial lives; in the co-op tier a crewmate inside ("you're fogging up") is one more honest reading *(co-op tier)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. **That is the whole fantasy in one moment: the gauge said fine, and it was leaking; the ship said otherwise; the ship was right.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The comfort lie wears the same HR-5 tell the player has already learned (the never-resting needle, the violet fringe, the wrong-pitched tick, SN L-L), so the gauge's own leak is something the player could have caught: the death reads as "I ignored the leak and the hiss", not "the game cheated" (the Beat C attribution criterion below). The lethal remote lie is never contradicted by a mark on its own housing: the alarm mark (IW-19) is drawn only where the alarm-eligible subject physically is, and a gauge whose subject is in another compartment (`hops` >= 1, the derived REMOTE flag of IW-8) never carries it, so the FALSE_OK needle in a normal zone has no lit Alarm Red beside it to falsify it by glance. Falsifying it still costs a walk, a second reading or, in the co-op tier, a crewmate (P4). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*

### How the fantasy deepens, not breaks, under hallucination

The rule that keeps the ladder fair is ART 7.3's: **"The mechanism is always honest; only the value lies."** The needle still pivots, the digit still ticks in steps, the housing is still geometry, the alarm mark still means the real alarm. The lie is only ever *where the needle points* (or which honest frame is shown); the player never has to doubt the physics of an instrument, only its testimony.

| Sanity stage (SN-16 reach; `LIES_MAX` 1/2/4) | Surfaces that may lie (drawn here) | What the player learns |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | none | Read cause, mass and rest; match every reading to a witness. Tremor is a real warning (P2). |
| 1 | gauge needle, and the card (its needle and its numeral are the same ref's `Perceived`; the numeral is a card-visible mode of the needle's lie, not a second surface) | "Wandering needles lie; shaking needles warn." Precision (the card) is not proof. The first lie is cheap and fault-free, so the lesson lands without punishment. |
| 2 | + state glyph, + lamp-housing glow frame (including the housing pip, R21) | Finding a gauge by its glow is not proof its loop runs. Remote readings can now hide lethal faults (Beat C). |
| 3 | + whole machine sprite state frames (SPRITE_SWAP, both honest frames required) | "The lie extends from the gauge to the sprite." A rotor may spin on a stopped machine; only the consequence channel and other people remain. |

**What must survive every stage (the fantasy's floor).** At least one channel of truth (P4): the consequence channel (LSG:168) is never drawn or corrupted by this GDD, and L-2I keeps one honest second reading per loop per seat (SN-15 L-2I; the suit counts only against an ALARM-direction lie). The HR-5 leak on every lie (gleam and motion mode mandatory, only SOUND droppable, PF-8; never below two modes at stage 2-3, SN L-L). The in-world alarm mark is TRUTH, untagged, and can never be added, suppressed or faked by a lie (R14; SN L-A): it is the one surface where the fantasy promises "if this shows, believe it". The promise is one-directional: its presence is the ship speaking and outranks the needle; its absence proves nothing. It appears only where the alarm-eligible subject itself is (IW-19), so believing it still costs the walk to get there. Honest tremor and the reduced-motion stress notch appear only on an honest reading (R9, R19), so "shake = real" is a rule the game never breaks. Under reduced motion the lie stays distinguishable: never-rest and the continuous digit tick remain, glide scaling is the Filter's (PF-39), settle becomes a step, tremor becomes a held notch that is never shown on a lying reading.

**What must never happen (named as rule violations).** An honest gauge that glides; a lying gauge that settles or trembles; a card that disagrees with its gauge in either direction; a lie that ends with a snap or a cue (the end is never announced, SN-16); any surface that lets a player read their sanity off an instrument (a gauge that always lies near a stage threshold, a colour correlated with stage; PF-38).

### Tone and language

Quiet, mechanical, intimate. Instruments are the calmest thing on screen until they are not: the honest instrument is reassurance with weight (settle, rest), the lying one is an itch (it never rests). Dread lives in the gap between the two, never in UI energy (ART 7.3 rejected "juicy UI"). The register used in this GDD and in player-facing keys: *reading, needle, zone, glass, bezel, housing, face, sight-glass, meniscus, digit, settle, tremor, glide, tick, hold stale, rest, witness, second instrument, second opinion, falsify, check, chest lamp, beam, Chemlight glowstick, fixture, lantern pool.* Not used outside a REJECT or guard-rail line: status, health, condition, indicator, meter, bar (except "linear bar" quoted from ART 3.4), animate, tween, bounce, real/fake, true/false icon, "detected", "spotted", "hallucination" in player-facing text, "hand lamp", and the words OK, FAILED, DEGRADED, WORN, LOW, CRITICAL on any card or gauge. The card label is a stencilled ship label — the reading's name on its fitting, caps, Hull Grey on Ground — closer to a part number painted on a pipe than a tooltip; exact strings are the narrative-director's and the localisation lead's, and every string is a `tr()` StringName key (IA-39).

### Reference techniques

| Reference | Technique | Verdict | What and why |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pandorum | chest-mounted, hands-free light as the reading tool | **ADOPT** | The beam from the per-facing chest point makes a gauge readable; hands stay free. Binding user decision. |
| Beyond the Aquila Rift | the calm, plausible lie of a ship that talks to the waking crew | **ADOPT (tone only)** | A FALSE_OK on a remote gauge is the Aquila Rift beat; no authored reveal or story text on any instrument. |
| Project Zomboid | unannounced, lethal consequence; zoom ladder | **ADOPT** | Trusting a lying remote gauge can kill and the game explains nothing; legibility must hold at every zoom rung. |
| Project Zomboid | moodles, condition words, item condition bars | **REJECT** | No condition word, rating or bar on any instrument or card (R2; BS-6; IA-39). |
| Satisfactory | machine state in the moving part (rotor, fluid in pipe) | **ADOPT** | Flow pattern and moving medium on pipes are TRUTH witnesses (R8); the machine frame is corruptible only at stage 3. |
| Satisfactory, Barotrauma, FTL | map overlay, ship status screen, room schematic, system-power panel | **REJECT** | No schematic, overlay, minimap, ship-status or loop-summary screen, ever (LSG:51; R16). |
| Iron Lung | knowledge only through crude instruments | **ADOPT (partly)** | Information arrives through a physical instrument of limited precision. Rejected half: there the instrument is the only truth; here the world outranks the instrument (P1). |
| Dead Space | diegetic readouts on the world and body | **ADOPT** | Ship-produced readings live on the walls (layer 0, lit); only suit readings and the card are screen-space (ART 7.0). Rejected: holographic menus in the world. |
| Alien: Isolation | motion tracker with threat direction | **REJECT** | No instrument shows a horror, a threat, a proximity or a direction (AT:381; HE:377; AA:1503). Attention never moves a needle (AT-17, AT:1415 C1). |
| Amnesia, Call of Cthulhu: DCoTE | sanity meter, sanity-correlated screen effects | **REJECT** | No sanity meter or stage readout anywhere (ART:741; PF-38). The only sanity on an instrument is the violet edge on a reading that is lying. |
| Eternal Darkness | fourth-wall UI tricks | **REJECT** | ART 7.3 rejected scanline/glitch lies: "it says 'the HUD is broken' when the pillar says 'the gauge is wrong'". The frame, menus and pause never lie; only a reading does. |
| Signalis | restrained, mechanical, low-colour UI | **ADOPT (register)** | Supports ART 3.4: frames solid and static, readings thin and moving. Restraint only. |

### Anti-pillar and guard-rail statements this system carries

| Guard rail | Protects |
|---|---|
| No schematic, overlay, minimap, ship-status, loop-summary or "lit chart-table console" (rejected explicitly, R16) | LSG:51 anti-pillar 3; P1; P3 |
| No numerals on any in-world gauge at game-camera distance; numerals only on the card (ART 3.1, ART:747) | P3: precision costs light and stillness |
| No condition word, verdict, rating or bar on any instrument or card; no OK/FAILED text; Alarm Red is a mark, never text (R2; ART 4.6) | P1 design test; BS-6 |
| No instrument reads a crewmate, a horror, Attention or sanity; no completion ping, score, mastery, "lies spotted" or progression surface | not a combat game; no intentional PvP; no win state; P3 "never a meter"; PF-38 |
| No closed ring or circular dial over a square console; dials are open 240-degree arcs on chamfered housings (R25; ART:714) | crewmate silhouette protection |
| No violet or flesh pixel in any authored sheet; the card frame never carries violet; the gleam is runtime, fringe only (R5; ART:1207) | the sane palette (P1) |
| No authored story text, log or message on an instrument or card | no authored story |
| No freeform instrument placement by the player; instruments come with pre-shaped modules and authored placements | not a freeform builder |
| No pod dash (WM AB-2; R26) | scope |
| Zero simulation state: no blocks, hooks, mutators or persisted data; the card is seat-local, never replicated, never a command (R20, R22) | kernel boundary (KRN H10) |

### We will know this was right if (playtest validation; feeds (manual) acceptance criteria)

- In the MVP kill/continue playtest, unprompted players confirm a doubted reading by walking to its witness or using a second instrument at least once per session (the falsification economy works).
- Players describe a glide in their own words as "wandering", "drifting" or "won't settle" and a tremor as "shaking" (the motion grammar is legible without colour).
- No player asks for a ship map or status screen after the first session; if they do, the witness layout is too sparse (a Derelict Authoring content signal, not a reason to add a screen).
- *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The card is used for decisions, and its frequency is only logged: (a) in at least 4 of 6 sessions (sample size a D95 default pending confirmation) a tester opens the card on an instrument at one of the three IW-22 decisions; (b) the playtest log reports card opens per minute of lit-instrument time, with no pass line, because spamming the card is harmless under card parity (it is never a falsifier); a high value is read as evidence for the Attention stationary-in-beam option of OQ-IW-40, not as a defect here. No cooldown is proposed (it would be an unexplained rule).
- *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Beat C attribution.** After a lethal remote-lie death, at least 4 of 6 testers (a D95 default) attribute it to ignoring the leak (the gauge's wander, fringe or tick) or the witness (the hiss, the draught), and at most 1 of 6 call it unfair. Coding rule: the tester's first unprompted explanation is coded as LEAK/WITNESS (own fault), GAUGE-ONLY (the lie alone, no leak or witness named) or OTHER; the protocol is a post-death one-question prompt, "what happened?", asked before any hint. A failure of this criterion is a Sanity/witness-clarity signal, not a reason to remove the lethal lie.

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

*Conventions.* Rules are named `IW-n` (In-World); each states a **Rule** (what the player sees and the contract with neighbours), a **Why** and **Edge notes**. Formulas are `D.n` (Formulas section: D.1 needle angle, D.2 zone intervals, D.3 honest numeral, D.4 settle, D.5 tremor, D.6 card layout, D.7 legibility table, D.8 instrument budget, D.9 gleam edge, D.10 reduced-motion substitutes, D.11 linear instruments, D.12 `lsb` visibility lint). Conflicts are `K-n` (K-1..K-14 from the brief, K-15 and K-16 raised by the ux-designer, K-17 and K-18 raised in this stage); open questions `OQ-IW-n`. "(advisor ruling Rn)", "(checkpoint ruling Cn)" and "(Xn)" are provenance labels from the authoring run: the `rulings.md` they cite is not on disk and **no rulings digest exists in this document**; the rule each label governs is stated in the clause or rule that cites it, and a label is never the only statement of a rule *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (the earlier claim that a digest at the end of Open Questions restates every ruling was false and is withdrawn); "(orchestrator ruling: ...)" marks a place where two specialists disagreed and this GDD picked one. Values that are this GDD's own and not yet registered are `[PLACEHOLDER]`; values quoted from a neighbour carry their owner and are never changed here. Implementation choices are tagged "-> ADR candidate" and kept out of the rules. **Chest-lamp sweep:** the beam origin is the per-facing chest point (PC); the lamp is hands-free; thrown Chemlight glowsticks (`#86B896`) light a gauge exactly as the beam does; no "hand lamp" wording appears anywhere in this document.

**Rule index.** Group A, what this system is: IW-1 ownership, IW-2 presentation-only, IW-3 lit-only readability, IW-4 housing pip, IW-5 reading source. Group B, the instrument family: IW-6 taxonomy, IW-7 dial template, IW-8 data and calibration record, IW-9 quantity set, IW-10 the needle element. Group C, honest motion: IW-11 continuous change and settle, IW-12 tremor, IW-13 motion-mode dispatch, IW-14 readouts, IW-15 zone bands. Group D, lying surfaces and state frames: IW-16 the gleam, IW-17 Module-Body Presenter, IW-18 WORN, IW-19 alarm mark, IW-20 sight-glass and linear instruments. Group E, the inspect view: IW-21 targeting, IW-22 card content, IW-23 placement, IW-24 lifecycle, IW-25 input and falsification, IW-26 parity. Group F, boundaries: IW-27 reduced motion, IW-28 never-do list, IW-29 manifest floors, IW-30 DEAD binding, IW-31 placement record, IW-32 sound, IW-33 pause and restore, IW-34 fallbacks, IW-35 flagged not edited, IW-36 legibility contract, IW-37 strings, IW-38 diagnostics.

#### Group A. What this system is

**IW-1. Ownership: module-body reading surfaces.**
*Rule.* In-World Instruments owns (a) every gauge, dial, level sight-glass and state glyph produced by the ship or a derelict, drawn as lit sprites on layer 0; (b) the inspect card on layer 2, the same instrument at HUD size; (c) the **Module-Body Presenter**: the selection among a module sheet's *honest* state frames and the drawing of the state glyph (IW-17). It owns no module art (Item DB `sprite_family` names the sheet and state tokens), no placement (Layout and Derelict Authoring), no behaviour of the module, and no decision about whether, when, for whom or by how much anything lies (Filter and Sanity). It never calls `true_value`. Every reading produced by the ship or a derelict is this GDD's; Suit & Equipment and HUD & Suit Layer own only suit-produced and hand-tool readings (advisor ruling R7, R24).
*Why.* IDX:39 and ART 7.0 ("the ship is an instrument": who produced the number decides where it is drawn). No sibling claims the presenter (DA:1340, FT:232, IA:920, PF:1461). DA:1571's row assigning "gauges and instruments reading authored systems" to "the suit and HUD" is a stale assignment, refreshed by its owner (K-4).
*Edge notes.* The index has no row naming the module presenter (K-11): flagged to the producer, not edited here (IW-35).

**IW-2. Presentation-only, zero registrations.**
*Rule.* Instruments are `PRESENTATION` subscribers (KRN:106-110; PIPE PR-8). They read `SIM` and `PERCEPTION_OUT` views and write only CanvasItem, Material, Light2D, particle and audio-request state; never a block, a command, a hook or a mutator. A notification is a dirty mark; reading and drawing happen in the render callback; placement is integer base px; nothing here can cause a tick. The card is **not** an `INPUT_SURFACE`: it issues no commands. The Controller's Adapter (an `INPUT_SURFACE`) owns the `inspect_card` context and publishes a presentation-local signal `{context, inspect_ref}` (registry: `inspect_card_context_signal`) that Instruments subscribe to (IW-25). Instruments register no block, hook, mutator, dirty key or persisted field (advisor ruling R22; K-1).
*Why.* PR-8 rule 1 (PIPE:188): `INPUT_SURFACE` only "if it also issues commands — the HUD's prompts do not; the chart table does" (PIPE's example only: per Chart Table CT-27, the chart table holds the `INPUT_SURFACE` class for VS-B and submits nothing at MVP). Instruments submit nothing.
*Edge notes.* Refresh owed by the Controller: PC-16 (PC:147: "an `INPUT_SURFACE` of In-World Instruments"), PC:77's purpose column ("Interaction's inspect verb", contradicting IA-38), PC:210 row 10 (K-1, K-12).

**IW-3. Lit-only readability: "What You Light, You Can Read".**
*Rule.* A world gauge is readable exactly as far as the world lighting makes its pixels visible: Instruments do **not** gate the drawing of a world gauge on `lit(ref)`; the lit renderer and the HullSurface material make an unlit face dark. Two things are gated on the Emitter State bit `lit(ref)` (hysteresis `LIT_ON_PM` 150 / `LIT_OFF_PM` 120, no dwell; owner technical-artist; EM-22): the **inspect prompt** (Interaction's, IW-21) and the **inspect card** (IW-24). No instrument is an emitter and no gauge is a lantern. Mechanical gauges need no power to be read at MVP, so a dead loop's gauge shows its reading as soon as light reaches it. Thrown Chemlight glowsticks and fixtures light a gauge as the beam does.
*Why.* P3: reading spends light. Gating the card by the sim bit and the face by the renderer keeps one source of light truth each (ART 7.0 "unreadable until lit"; LO:436; EM:864).
*Edge notes.* Between `LIT_OFF_PM` and `LIT_ON_PM` the face may be dimly visible while the card is refused: intended (precision costs more light than a glance). Gauge motion is updated whether or not the face is lit (cheap, and a face entering the beam shows its current needle on its first lit frame); the gleam is "lit with its host" and needs no lit query (PF-16). The face is never shown or hidden by `lit(ref)` in code, so the 150/120 hysteresis cannot make a face flash at a pool edge (HR-13); only the prompt and the card are lit-gated, and the card needs a fresh press to reopen. Whether a gauge needs power at a later tier is OQ-IW-11.

**IW-4. Self-emission is a non-readable housing pip, and the pip is corruptible.**
*Rule.* "A running loop's gauge is faintly lit by its own emission" (ART 7.0) is realised as a small loop-hue **housing pip** (size `[PLACEHOLDER]`, 4x2 art px proposed by the art-director, 2x2 by the ux-designer; OQ-IW-12) on the emissive layer (mask bit 2, casts no light, exposes nothing; LO-14, LO:225). It exists only while the bound subject's `served_pm` > 0, lets the player *find* a running gauge in the dark, and does **not** make the face readable and does **not** make `lit(ref)` true. It is drawn through the `HOUSING_GLOW(ref)` ref, so it is stage-2 corruptible: finding a gauge by its pip is not proof its loop runs. Its emission is capped at or below fixture glow and well below the beam (`PIP_EMIT_MAX`, set by the Lighting spike, ART 8.12); the hull/integrity loop has no pip (it has no running medium) (advisor ruling R21; K-6).
*Why.* Keeps `lit(ref)`, the card, Sanity's lit-entry and P3 consistent without adding emitters or an Emitter State change (LO-14's channel rule: "a lamp housing's own emissive sprite ... read the Filter channel and may lie").
*Edge notes.* The pip carries **presence or absence only** ("something here is running"); loop identity is the housing's fitting silhouette (IW-7), so the hue-removed test is met by silhouette and by the pip's on/off state. The fixture lamp's brightness from `served_pm` is consequence channel and is never confused with the pip, which casts no pool.

**IW-5. Reading source: one pull per instrument per frame; never cache; never read truth.**
*Rule.* In the render callback each on-screen instrument in a loaded section obtains its displayed value with exactly one `perceived(ref, viewer)` call (PF-7), `viewer` being the local seat's `PlayerId`; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* one **`InstrumentPresenter` per instrument** is the sole caller of `perceived()` for its ref and the sole owner of the settle, tremor, dwell and notch scalars, and the wall node and the card are views of the frame index and digits it publishes once per frame (the Filter's `(ref, viewer, frame)` memo is the backstop; ADR candidate, X-6). An instrument never stores a `Perceived` record across frames, never addresses the Filter by `peer_id` (PF-10), and never calls `true_value`, `summary()` (a ship-status read), `power_use()` (Attention's stimulus) or `can_join()`. It may read `read_view` only for **non-value** inputs: the tremor predicate (IW-12), module frame selection and WORN (IW-17, IW-18), the alarm predicate (IW-19), and binding validity (IW-30). Those reads are refreshed once per Graph step (`GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6), not per frame, and never feed a displayed value. The needle target is `displayed_pm`. An instrument that is off-screen or in an unloaded section pulls nothing; on its first pull after being culled it **snaps** to `displayed_pm` with no settle and no interpolation from stale history (history older than one Graph step is meaningless, and a settle on re-entry would be a motion with no world cause, P1) (advisor ruling R18).
*Why.* PF-8: the record never contains the truth while a lie is active; caching would let a stale record outlive a lie's end. R18 and PF-3: only the Filter wraps a true value.
*Edge notes.* Presentation-local scalars (the settle triple, `prev_honest_pm` and the rest, window and reversal-dwell scalars of IW-11, tremor onset and decay timestamps, the numeral throttle timestamp) are derived drawing state, not cached `Perceived` records: each is a pure function of values the player was already shown, none is read by another system, all are discarded on restore, cull and camera cut. **CONFLICT K-17 (new): LSG:998 and LS-20 (LSG:201) (locked) say "gauges read `read_view` at render rate for smooth needles" (LS-20: "Instruments and Audio poll `read_view` at render rate for smooth needles")**, which contradicts R18/PF-3 (a gauge never reads a value from `read_view`). This GDD follows R18 and PF-3: value reads go only through `perceived`; the "smooth needles" of LSG:998 and LS-20 is delivered by IW-11's interpolation, not by a value read. The Graph's own sentences are routed to its owner as a refresh. Related: the honest path carries no previous value, which IW-11 answers with the local scalar `prev_honest_pm` (K-17, checkpoint ruling C14).

#### Group B. The instrument family

**IW-6. Taxonomy: wall instrument kinds and the card's numeral; one definition at two scales.**
*Rule.* Every instrument is one data definition (`assets/data/instruments/<instrument_id>`, IW-8) rendered by one scene definition at two scales: the wall or panel node (layer 0, art px x `k`) and the card (layer 2, HUD art px x `H`). The kinds:

| Kind | `gauge_kind` | World form (no numerals, ART 3.1) | Card form | Motion signatures | Lie reach (SN-16) | Sizes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Dial | DIAL | open 240-degree arc strip on a chamfered rectangular housing; separate pivoted needle; zone bands; hub | same dial at HUD px, label, numeral | settle, tremor, glide (with wander) | needle: stage 1 | DIAL_L, DIAL_S (IW-7) |
| Level sight-glass | LINEAR | vertical glass tube, fluid column, meniscus bead, side zone strip and ticks | same glass, label, numeral | settle, tremor, glide (linear branch, D.11) | meniscus: stage 1 | one size at MVP, `travel_px` 50 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (IW-20) |
| Numeral | READOUT | **none in the world** | digit cells in a glass-edged well | honest step per real change; lie: continuous tick or stale hold (PF-18) | numeral: stage 1 | card only |
| State glyph | GLYPH | 8x8 lens mark on a module housing; two shape-coded frames (run, stop); no flow mark, no loop hue | not carried | frame swap (no needle motion) | stage 2 | 8x8 |
| Machine frame | FRAME | the module's own state frames (IW-17) | not carried | frame cut, rotor cadence | stage 3 (SPRITE_SWAP) | module cell (96x96) |

Housing glow frames (the lamp lit/unlit pair) and the pip (IW-4) ride the `HOUSING_GLOW` ref (stage 2). The stage-1 "readout" of SN-16 is the card's numeral, which is `displayed_value` of the `.needle` ref's `Perceived` (the digit tick of PF-18 acts there; TICK and STALE govern the numeral only, IW-13); it is not a second lie surface. A wall gauge carries no digit. The SN-16 "readout" wording is routed to Sanity and the `motion_mode` field description and PF-18 to the Filter as a wording refresh (checkpoint ruling C2).
*(orchestrator ruling: the ux-designer proposed an 8-pip drum ladder as an in-world "digital readout"; the art-director and ui-programmer proposed card-only. Card-only is adopted: a pip ladder is a numeral in disguise against ART 3.1, doubles the asset set for a solo developer and adds a lie surface with no witness value beyond the needle. The battery-stock instruments become DIAL_S `stock` dials in the manifest, IW-29.)*
*Why.* One calibration for the Filter (PF-45), one test set, one art load; the card is "the same instrument magnified" by construction.
*Edge notes.* A decorative gauge that carries no remote, magnitude or trend information beyond its local witness is forbidden (IW-29). The loop identity of an instrument is never carried by dial geometry (IW-7).

**IW-7. Dial template family: two authored sizes, one arc.**
*Rule.* All dials use `arc_deg` 240 (open sector at the bottom; the arc runs -120 to +120 degrees clockwise from 12 o'clock). Two authored sizes (registry: `dial_size_class`): **DIAL_L** `needle_len_px` 24 in a 96x48 prop cell and **DIAL_S** `needle_len_px` 18 in a 48x48 cell. Both are at or above `NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX` 16 (owner: this GDD; value unchanged, range 14.3-32) and glide-eligible: `rate_floor` = max(0.5, 28.6 / `needle_len_px`) = 1.19 deg/s at 24 and 1.59 deg/s at 18, inside the 0.5-2.0 band (PF-13, PF-17). Loop identity is the housing's fitting silhouette (ART 3.4, 7.2 table) plus the running-only pip, never dial geometry or face hue. Geometry (art px; pivot at the arc centre; band inner radius = needle length + 1; one geometry set, fixed by checkpoint ruling C5; the pixel mock S-A1 confirms the DIAL_S bezel only):

| Size | Needle | Band inner r | Band thickness | Outer r | Arc width (2 x outer r) | Arc height (1.5 x outer r) | Housing (DIAL_L bezel 2 px, DIAL_S bezel 1 px, each side) | Cell | Cell margin |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DIAL_L | 24 | 25 | 3 | 28 | 56 | 42 | 60 x 46 | 96x48 | 36 px horizontal, 2 px vertical |
| DIAL_S | 18 | 19 | 3 | 22 | 44 | 33 | 46 x 35 (bezel 1 px each side) | 48x48 | 1 px each side horizontally, 13 px vertically in total |

*Arithmetic.* A 240-degree arc centred on 12 o'clock reaches its full width at the 3 and 9 o'clock points (|x| = r) and its lowest point at the end stops (y = r cos 120 = -0.5 r), so height = r (above the pivot) + 0.5 r (below) = 1.5 r. Needle stroke is 2 art px (HR-12: a stroke is at least 2 screen px at every `k`). The outer radius is the band's inner radius plus its thickness: DIAL_L 25 + 3 = 28 (arc 2 x 28 = 56 wide, 1.5 x 28 = 42 high, housing 56 + 4 = 60 by 42 + 4 = 46 with a 2 px bezel), DIAL_S 19 + 3 = 22 (arc 2 x 22 = 44 wide, 1.5 x 22 = 33 high, housing 44 + 2 = 46 by 33 + 2 = 35 with a 1 px bezel). The DIAL_S band is **3 rows** because IW-15 needs three patterns (solid, hatched, notched) that can be told apart; a 2-row band could not carry them. In the 48x48 cell the DIAL_S housing leaves 48 - 46 = 2 px horizontally (1 px each side) and 48 - 35 = 13 px vertically in total; DIAL_L leaves 96 - 60 = 36 px horizontally and 48 - 46 = 2 px vertically. The 1-px DIAL_S bezel is a minimum-feature exception routed to the art-director under BR-9 (ART 5.6); S-A1 confirms the bezel only, the radii are fixed. **Helmet-scale test (ART:714; the crewmate helmet is a 16 px circle; a round feature must be < 5.3 or > 32 art px):** sweep diameter 2L = 48 (DIAL_L) and 36 (DIAL_S), outer arc diameter 56 (DIAL_L) and **44** (DIAL_S, > 32), hub 4 (< 5.3): all pass. A dial at exactly `NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX` 16 has a 32 px sweep, on the boundary, so 16 is the Filter's eligibility floor and **not an authored size** (advisor ruling R25). The dial is never a closed circle; the 3.1 silhouette test is run on both housings (AC-IW-9). **Mounting (orchestrator ruling: art-director's terminal-fitting rule adopted over the ux-designer's housing-over-fitting-cell proposal).** Neither dial fits inside the pipe band (rows 29-58 of the 72 px face; ART 6.3 rule 7): DIAL_L (46 high) is a cap-less wall-hung panel or machine-panel instrument and is never mounted on a pipe run; DIAL_S (35 high) attaches at a pipe's **terminal fitting** (pipes never dead-end without one, ART 6.3 rule 1), centred on the band's mid-row (43.5, spanning about rows 26-61), so "pipe -> gauge" reads as a sentence; the exception to ART 6.3 rule 7 ("except an instrument seated on a terminal fitting") is a bible refresh owed by the art-director (BR-2). A MACHINE_PANEL dial of either size sits inside the module's 96x96 node. Wall gauges take the host face's sort origin (PIPE:129, :137). (advisor ruling R6, R25; K-7)
*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Lower-sector arithmetic (the fitting plate).** The open sector below the pivot is bounded by the rays 60 degrees either side of straight down, so at depth `y` below the pivot its half-width is `y x tan 60` = 1.732 `y`; its floor is the arc's lowest points, at `y` = 0.5 x outer r (14 on DIAL_L, 11 on DIAL_S), and the housing's outer bottom edge is that plus the bezel (16 and 12). A plate of half-width `w` needs its top row at `y` >= `w` / 1.732. DIAL_L: the 16-px plate (`w` 8) starts at row 5 (4.62 rounded up), and the earlier 12-px height would end at row 17, 2.62 px past the arc floor and 1 px past the outer edge; the plate is therefore **16x11**, seated on the bottom bezel and ending exactly on the outer edge at row 16. DIAL_S: the 12-px plate (`w` 6) starts at row 4 (3.46 rounded up) and the 8-px height ends at row 12, exactly the outer edge (1-px bezel), so **12x8 stands**, also seated on the bezel. Both plates overlay the bottom bezel and overflow nothing; S-A1 confirms the silhouettes at 16x11 and 12x8. The LINEAR bead is **6 wide x 2 tall** (the width of the 6-px tube); the earlier "2x6" read as 2 wide x 6 tall, which fits neither the tube nor the 4-px caps. With a 54-px interior and `travel_px` 50 the bead spans rows 0-1 at empty and 50-51 at full, clear of both caps. Plate and bead extents are in the S-A1 scope.

*Why.* Concept: solo-developer art load; PF-45: one calibration per kind.
*Edge notes.* Fallback if the silhouette test reads "gauge on a console" as the crewmate-reserved composition (ART 6.6 rule 2): a squarer, octagonal-chamfered arc authored once for both sizes (R-A2). If the terminal-fitting mount is rejected: dials only on MACHINE_PANEL mounts and the wall carries sight-glasses only. DIAL_L consumes 46 of 48 rows (a 1-px margin each side): it cannot be a console-face detail inside a cap-plus-face prop; this is a placement constraint routed to Layout and Derelict Authoring (IW-31).

**IW-8. Instrument data and the calibration record (the answer to PF-45).**
*Rule.* Per instrument surface, a record in `assets/data/instruments/`: `instrument_id`; `gauge_kind` (DIAL | LINEAR | READOUT | GLYPH | FRAME); `size_class`; `quantity` (IW-9); `arc_deg` (DIAL; 0 for LINEAR); `needle_len_px` (DIAL) or `travel_px` (LINEAR meniscus travel); `value_lo`, `value_hi`, `lsb`, `unit_key`, `name_key` (the card label); `zones[]` as LS-7 band **names** mapped to a pattern class (D.2; the thresholds are derived at build time and never written into this data); `lie_surface_id` per corruptible surface (`<instrument_id>.needle`, `.glyph`, `.housing_glow`, `.machine_frame`; there is no `.readout` surface and the card has no id of its own: one ref carries one lie and one `Perceived`, so the card numeral is `displayed_value` of the `.needle` ref's `Perceived`, and `motion_mode` TICK and STALE apply to that numeral, checkpoint ruling C2); and `beyond_witness`, the flag set defined below, which is **derived from placement and quantity** and must equal the authored value (lint `BEYOND_WITNESS_MISMATCH`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The binding (`node_ref` or `room_key`, `mount`, `host_face` and, since the design review, `mount_room`, `subject_room`, `host_node`) is **placement data** authored in Layout and Derelict Authoring (IW-31). A missing or malformed record yields the honest path plus `lie_unrenderable` (PF-44), a **build failure** for any kind listed in `lie_targets.json`. Assets owed exactly as PF V.11: `mach_gauge_<loop>_alb.png` plus `_msk.png` with slices `glass`, `needle_edge`, `needle`; a `ui_` sheet with a `glass` slice for digit cells and card glass; `mach_<name>_<state>_alb.png`; `mach_lamp_<name>_lit` / `_unlit`; both honest state frames of every pair on **one sheet page** (advisor ruling R11; PF-45).
*Definitions: mount, subject and `beyond_witness`.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier text declared `beyond_witness` in {REMOTE, MAGNITUDE, TREND} without defining any value, and the manifest tagged instruments REMOTE that hang in the room they read.)* Placement splits the two places an instrument has. `mount_room` is the compartment in which the housing physically hangs; `subject_room` is the compartment holding what the reading is about (for a compartment ref the named `cid`; for a node ref the compartment that holds the node, found through `nodes_in(cid)`; for a POWER `loop_component` ref the compartment of its anchor node). `host_node` is the node whose own panel or prop carries the housing (none for a WALL_FACE mount) and `subject_node` is the bound node (none for a compartment ref). `hops(a, b)` is the shortest path over `neighbors(cid)` (LS-19) and `hops(a, a)` = 0. `beyond_witness` is a **set of flags computed by a lint from placement and quantity; nobody judges it, and the authored field must equal the computed set**:

| Flag | Predicate (testable) | Reading for the player | Manifest example |
|---|---|---|---|
| REMOTE | `hops(mount_room, subject_room)` >= 1. A REMOTE instrument is lie-eligible only if also `hops` <= `WITNESS_HOPS_MAX` 2: SN L-W measures the witness from the lying surface's compartment (the mount) to the fault's compartment (the subject), so the same pair of rooms decides it | The dial is about a room the player is not standing in, and its witness (frost, hiss, draught) is not at hand. It is the only flag that can produce Beat C, because L-G forbids a FALSE_OK only on the quantity of the compartment the seat stands in | `inst_a_machine_T` (1 hop) |
| MAGNITUDE | `hops` = 0 and the quantity's LS-22 witness is band-stepped (O2: the THIN and HYPOXIC body cues; pressure: draught present or strong; coolant and fuel: the drip and the puddle), so the dial adds where the value sits inside a band. A quantity whose LS-22 witness is continuous (`T`: frost reach; `load`: lamp brightness and hum pitch) is never MAGNITUDE | The witness says that something is wrong; the dial says how much | `inst_airlock_P` |
| TREND | the quantity is `T` or `stock`: inertial quantities that move over minutes, whose witness shows a state and not a direction or a rate (frost does not say that the room is warming) | The dial shows which way the value is going | `inst_cryo_bay_T` |
| STATE | `gauge_kind` is GLYPH or FRAME (no flag above can apply to a two-frame surface) | A state surface whose witness is the machine's own consequence (hum, rotor, lamp). Exempt from IW-29 (iv); floor (iii) still applies (OQ-IW-37) | `inst_scr_a_glyph` |

A record whose computed set is empty is a decorative gauge and fails the build (IW-29 (iv)); a local `load` dial, whose witness is continuous and whose quantity is not inertial, is the example, which is why the bus-load dial hangs outside the room it reads. Flags combine (`inst_a_machine_T` is REMOTE and TREND).

*Why.* The Filter "cannot invent geometry" (PF-45 rationale); gameplay values are data-driven (coding standards); one calibration per kind.
*Edge notes.* No violet or flesh is ever baked in a `ui_` or `_alb` sheet (reserved-hue scan, ART:1207); the gleam is produced at runtime (IW-16). The face art with its zone arcs is generated at build time from this data and the LS-7 band names -> ADR candidate (build tool). A record whose `arc_deg` is not 240 or whose `needle_len_px` is below `NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX` fails a build lint (one dial family; glide eligibility). The zone-band lint, the `lsb` visibility lint and the quantity-collision lint (IW-9) are specified in D.2, D.12 and AC-IW-12, AC-IW-23. All records are lint-checked against `lie_targets.json` coverage (AC-SN-45): every module kind needs at least one tagged corruptible surface.

**IW-9. The quantity set and ref addressing.**
*Rule.* The closed MVP quantity set (registry: `instrument_quantity`); each row names the Graph source, unit, span, `lsb` and band family. Bands are LS-7 and LSG D.9, consumed by name and never redefined; spans are this GDD's gauge spans `[PLACEHOLDER]` pending the Graph's LS-26 nominals:

| `quantity` | Graph source (`read_view`) | Unit shown | `value_lo` .. `value_hi` | `lsb` | Zone family | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `p_o2` | `compartment(cid).p_o2` | kPa (value / 10), one decimal | 0 .. 300 dkPa | 1 dkPa | LS-7 `p_o2` bands | DIAL |
| `P` | `compartment(cid).P` | kPa, whole | 0 .. 1100 dkPa | 10 dkPa | LS-7 `P` bands | DIAL |
| `T` | `compartment(cid).T` | whole degrees C (mC / 1000) | -60000 .. 90000 mC | 1000 mC | LS-7 `T` bands (7 bands) | DIAL_L only |
| `stock` | `node(id).stock_pm` (the Graph's `⌊1000 × stock / capacity⌋`, capacity = the def's `cap_ml`, `BATT_CELL` or the O2_TANK template maximum; `true_value` kind `stock_pm`, LS-19 additions) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | percent | 0 .. 1000 permille | 10 permille | none (an empty-end tick only) | LINEAR (tank, reservoir) or DIAL_S (battery) |
| `load` | the Graph's `load_pm` `true_value` kind for a POWER `loop_component` (**requested**, specified below; this is not LSG `rho`, which is per edge and built from served-power sums) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | percent of supply | 0 .. 1250 permille | 10 permille | high edge at 1000 pm is this GDD's calibration (the brownout, D.6 `sat` < 1000), not an LS-7 band | DIAL |
| `strain` (held out of the MVP manifest, provisional) | `node(id).hp` inverted | an engineering-unit key, never a percent | 0 .. 10000 bp | 250 bp (the dirty quantum) | `HP_OK` 7000 / `HP_FAIL` 3000 edges | DIAL |

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **The `load` reading is a Graph-published quantity, not an Instruments derivation.** LS-19 publishes `supply` and `demand` as `true_value` kinds and calls their ratio "Instruments' own reading", but the Filter can only wrap a `true_value` and IW-5 forbids Instruments deriving a value outside the Filter, so a derived `load` had no lie path and no dirty-key quantum. The chosen path is **(a): the Graph publishes `load_pm` as a `true_value` kind** (request OQ-IW-20; cross-GDD note to the Life-Support Graph). Specification: `load_pm` = 0 when `supply` = 0, and `min(1250, floor(1000 x demand / supply))` otherwise; the clamp to the span belongs to the publisher, so `displayed_pm` is always inside 0..1000 and `diag.instr_pm_clamped` stays a defect counter. **Three bus cases, no longer folded into one pegged reading.** *Dead* (`supply` 0, `demand` > 0): the needle rests at the low stop, there is no housing pip (the pip needs `served_pm` > 0), the lamps are dark and the hum is silent, so the player is told that nothing is carried and not that the bus is overloaded. *Idle* (`supply` > 0, `demand` 0): the same low stop with the pip lit, so dead and idle are told apart by the pip and the lamps and not by the needle. *Loaded or overloaded* (`supply` > 0): 1..1250, with the NOTCHED stop from 1000. **Dirty cadence:** `load_pm` rides the loop key `L/<loop>/<anchor>` with a quantum of `LOAD_QUANTUM_PERMILLE` 20 (proposed; Graph-owned like the other LS-20 quanta; 16 pm of the 0..1250 span, 1.6 px on DIAL_L). The Filter wraps `load_pm` like any other kind: a lie moves the needle on the Filter's glide band and carries the full tri-modal leak. Until the Graph grants `load_pm`, `inst_engine_bus_load` is not buildable as a lie target (it is TRUTH-default by floor (i) in any case, so no manifest count moves). **Interim path (b), if the Graph declines:** the Filter's wrap derives the identical expression from `true_value(supply)` and `true_value(demand)` of the same component inside its one wrap point and states how lie and leak apply to the derived value; Instruments never derive.

`strain` is held out because a hull-condition dial risks becoming a condition bar in disguise (BS-6, FT-4, IDB): it ships only if the Graph confirms a physical stress quantity that is not `hp` (OQ-IW-5). Coolant **temperature** is not a quantity: no `read_view` field carries it (`T` is a compartment atmosphere value), so coolant instruments are level (`stock`) readings only until the Graph publishes one. **Ref addressing.** A node-bound instrument uses `GRAPH_NODE(kind, id)`; a room instrument uses `GRAPH_COMPARTMENT(cid, quantity)` (registry `perceived_query` `ref_union`; the Filter adopted the `quantity` term at the 2026-10-27 consistency pass, PF-9), so two lie-eligible compartment instruments in one room are distinct in PF-7's `(ref, viewer, frame)` memo. **Adopted by the Filter (PF-9, OQ-PF-29):** the `quantity` term and the LINEAR branch. **Confirmed by the Graph (LS-19 additions, 2026-10-27):** the `true_value(kind, id)` kinds are `p_o2`, `P`, `T` (compartment), `stock_pm` (node), `supply` and `demand` (POWER component), plus `hp` for diagnostics, and the former fallback of one lie-eligible compartment instrument per room is retired. Two instruments with the same `(subject, quantity)` still share one ref and so one lie, which would not be a second opinion; the lint `INSTR_QUANTITY_COLLISION` therefore fails the build for a duplicated `(subject_room or subject_node, quantity)` pair. `load_pm` is requested (above). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (advisor ruling R12; K-5, resolved)
*Why.* The registry formula `atmosphere_derive` lists this GDD in its `referenced_by`; the Graph does not enumerate `true_value` kinds anywhere (grep of LSG and PF found none).
*Edge notes.* The `T` gauge in `cryo_bay` is lie-eligible beside the `p_o2` dial now that the fallback is retired (IW-29) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The ui-programmer's alternative ids (`p_total`, `temp`, `bus_load`, `store_level`) are not used; the ids mirror the Graph's field names (orchestrator ruling).

**IW-10. The needle element.**
*Rule.* A needle is a **separate pivoted element** (never part of the face art) whose tip is placed on whole art pixels at integer base-px positions with nearest-neighbour sampling, 2 art px thick; the tip moves in whole art pixels: one pixel step corresponds to 9.95 pm on DIAL_L and 13.3 pm on DIAL_S (D.1). No instrument draws any element with a drawn line or an anti-aliased edge: the pinned engine's `CanvasItem` line drawing carries no antialiasing feather in 4.7 (`[REF]` breaking-changes.md), so a drawn needle would be thinner than authored and PF-45's separate-needle requirement unmet. Zone bands, ticks and bezel arcs are authored sprites baked into the face at build time, never drawn at runtime. The needle's wander (Filter-supplied) and tremor (IW-12) are real whole-pixel tip flips, not shimmer.
*Why.* 4.7 engine fact; PF-45; ART 7.3.
*Edge notes.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Decision (baseline): pre-rendered needle frames indexed by the rounded tip pixel, drawn with NEAREST filtering and no runtime rotation.** The build tool enumerates the distinct rounded tip positions along the arc (about 100 on DIAL_L and 76 on DIAL_S: the arc length `needle_len_px` x 4.19) and bakes one 2-px needle frame per tip pixel; a frame index is the only thing the presenter changes. The wall needle and the card needle read the **same frame index, computed once per frame by the instrument's presenter**, in a fixed order (read `perceived()`, advance the scalars, compute the tip pixel, publish one index), so wall and card parity holds by construction. A runtime-rotated `Sprite2D` stays the fallback only if spike S-A3 (OQ-IW-7, `[UNV]`: no engine-reference file covers rotation under NEAREST at `k` 1..8) shows the frame strip over budget. The Filter-facing contract is identical either way (pivot at the dial centre, `needle_len_px` the authored tip radius). A card needle is the same angle set as the wall needle, not a finer rotation (IW-26). The ADR is X-6 item 7.

#### Group C. Honest motion

**IW-11. Continuous change and the honest settle (closed form).**
*Rule.* Everything in this rule applies **only when `motion_mode` is HONEST**. (a) **Continuous change.** Honest samples of `displayed_pm` arrive when the Filter's honest memo is invalidated (an LS-20 quantum crossing, at most once per Graph step, `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6); while the Graph value sits inside one quantum there is no sample. **For MVP the honest needle's resolution is the LS-20 quantum** (checkpoint ruling C14): each instrument keeps one presentation-local scalar `prev_honest_pm` (the scalar that extends the triple of (c), beside the native-unit `prev_honest_native` that D.3 formats the card numeral from *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*); a new honest sample is drawn as a ramp from `prev_honest_pm` to the new accepted `target_pm` (the sample after the reversal dwell of (b); equal to `displayed_pm` unless a one-quantum reversal is still held) over one Graph step (100 ms) through the single PR-8 rule-5 interpolator (`tick_alpha`), with no overshoot and no extrapolation, and the needle then rests. The drawn value therefore lags the true value by at most one Graph step plus the quantum, except for a genuine one-quantum reversal, which the reversal dwell of (b) holds for `INSTR_REVERSAL_DWELL_STEPS` more steps (at most 500 ms in all) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; the consequence channel tolerates both (AAV-17 requires direction agreement, not phase). A slow honest ramp reads as a mechanical detent (step, then rest), which strengthens "honest rests, the lie never rests"; a fast ramp (a decompression) crosses a quantum every step and reads as continuous. The quanta in needle terms (D.1, D.4): `p_o2` 5 dkPa over a 300 dkPa span is 16.7 pm, 1.7 px on DIAL_L and 1.25 px on DIAL_S; `P` 10 dkPa over 1100 is 9.1 pm; `T` 1000 mC over 150000 is 6.7 pm; `stock` 20 pm is 1 px at `travel_px` 50. `INSTR_SETTLE_JUMP_PM` 40 exceeds every single-quantum step, so one quantum crossing never fires a settle; three `p_o2` quanta accumulated inside the jump window (50 pm) do, see (b). (b) **Rest, jump and settle: rest is defined in time.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier text defined rest per Graph step here and per sample in D.4 and AC-IW-17, a definition under which the settle could never fire on `p_o2` or `stock`.)* An instrument is **at rest** iff no honest sample has arrived for `INSTR_REST_STEPS` consecutive Graph steps (3 steps = 300 ms) and no settle is active; IW-11, D.4, E-IW-8, AC-IW-17 and the REST state of S2 all use this one definition. The first honest sample after rest opens a **jump window** of `INSTR_SETTLE_WINDOW_STEPS` Graph steps (4 steps = 400 ms) and records `rest_pm`, the value the needle held at rest. A *jump*, on a `settle_eligible` quantity only (D.4; `load` at MVP) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, is any sample inside that window with |`target_pm` - `rest_pm`| >= `INSTR_SETTLE_JUMP_PM` (the honest change accumulated since leaving rest, not the change of one step): a valve flick, a repair, an `apply_damage`, a breaker closing. The jump converts the in-flight ramp into a settle: the drawn value is the closed-form underdamped step response of D.4 as a pure function of `(from_pm, to_pm, t_jump)` and `present_time`, overshoot at most 5 % of the jump, settling to rest within 250-400 ms, then exactly still. A run that does not reach the threshold inside its window is a **sweep** (a continuing decompression crossing a quantum every step): it never settles until rest is re-entered, so a sustained ramp does not stutter. **The honest signature is therefore "step, then rest"**: every honest change is a detent-like step that ends at rest, and a real jump of at least 40 pm is a heavier damped ease; the overshoot shows as a 1-px tip flip only for jumps of 216 pm (DIAL_L) or 288 pm (DIAL_S) or more (D.4), and on the verified manifest actions only the `load` dial settles (D.4 reachability table). **Reversal dwell.** The LS-20 buckets have no hysteresis, so an honest value hovering on a quantum edge would flip the needle 1-2 px at up to 10 Hz with no stress descriptor, which reads as a tremor on a calm reading and teaches the lesson backwards. A sample that reverses the direction of the last accepted change by no more than one quantum is held as a candidate and accepted only after it has been the current sample for `INSTR_REVERSAL_DWELL_STEPS` Graph steps (4 = 400 ms); a candidate that equals the held value, or is superseded, is dropped. The needle, the card and the card numeral all use the accepted `target_pm`, so a 10 Hz dither across a quantum edge produces at most one move (AC-IW-110). (c) **State.** Exactly one `(from_pm, to_pm, t_jump)` triple per instrument plus the presentation-local scalars `prev_honest_pm` of (a) and `rest_pm`, the window start step and the dwell candidate (`target_pm`, `candidate_pm`, `t_candidate`) of (b), the triple discarded after `INSTR_SETTLE_MS`, neither ever persisted, never replicated, never read by any other *system*; the instrument's wall node and its card are two views of one instrument object and both draw from its single drawn value, which is what makes card parity (IW-26) hold. The triple, `prev_honest_pm` and the scalars of (b) are cleared by any non-HONEST frame and snapped by `hard_start` (the Filter's term, PF-33, raised by a kernel restore or `force_stage`), restore, camera cut and cull (IW-5, IW-33). The HUD's suit-arc settle (HS-14, `HUD_SETTLE_MS` 300 `[PH]`, HUD-owned; renamed there from `SETTLE_MS` after the collision, CF-HS-20) is a peer: it may cite `settle_response` (D.4) instead of restating a second curve, and this GDD's constants carry the `INSTR_` prefix so the two names never collide. (d) **Re-basing (orchestrator ruling: the ui-programmer's re-base adopted over the ux-designer's per-frame retarget, which would make the drawn value jump).** While a settle is active, any further change of the honest displayed value re-bases the triple: `from_pm` := the value currently drawn, `to_pm` := the new displayed value, `t_jump` := now; velocity is discarded (a small kink, accepted). So one triple always suffices, nothing accumulates, and a continuous ramp steeper than `INSTR_SETTLE_JUMP_PM` per step degrades into a first-order-like follower whose overshoot of each tiny delta is sub-pixel (no rubbery bounce). (e) Any `motion_mode` other than HONEST clears the triple at once and bypasses settle entirely: a lie never borrows settle (PF-17). Reduced motion: settle becomes a step (IW-27). (advisor ruling R10; K-2)
*Why.* ART 7.3 mandates a damped settle ("the ship changed; the instrument caught up"). PR-8's rationale (PIPE:198) rejected "a gauge's needle lag" as presentation-owned state because it threatened the truth channel; a gauge is module-body, not truth channel, and the closed form is a pure function of three scalars derived from values the player was already shown, with PF-11's "modulo presentation-local state" as precedent. This is the one bounded exemption from PR-8's rationale and is **carried as K-2 for the PIPE owner to acknowledge**, not silently overridden. The continuous path uses the one PR-8 rule-5 interpolator, so rule 5 itself is not violated.
*Edge notes.* **CONFLICT K-17 (resolved by checkpoint ruling C14; the Filter and Graph refreshes are routed).** PF-7 memoises an honest ref "per sanity run and invalidated by the notification that dirtied it", the honest `Perceived` carries no previous value, PF UI Requirements forbid caching a `Perceived`, and LS-20 fires dirty keys only on quantum crossings (`p_o2` 5 dkPa, `P` 10 dkPa, `T` 1000 mC, store 20 permille). So even a Filter-supplied previous value could buy at best a one-step ramp between quanta, and the honest needle's resolution is the LS-20 quantum (rule (a)). **Refresh landed in the Filter (PF UI Requirements, 2026-10-27; OQ-IW-1 closed)**: the no-cache sentence now reads "never cache a `Perceived` record; a presentation-local scalar derived from an honest displayed value and cleared on any non-HONEST frame, cull, cut and restore is not a cache". The earlier request for a Filter-supplied `displayed_prev_pm` is withdrawn. Option (B), the Filter refreshing the honest memo every Graph step for refs that have an on-screen instrument (about 18 refs at 10 Hz), would give a smooth ramp at a per-step cost and is carried as OQ-IW-18, not MVP. LSG:998 ("gauges read `read_view` at render rate for smooth needles") is still routed to the Graph as a refresh (R18 wins). A lie onset during an active settle clears the triple and the drawn needle snaps to `displayed_pm` by at most the settle's current deviation (the lie already shows its gleam floor and sound from its first frame, PF-15; the first lie is on a fault-free subject at rest, SN-16); letting the tail decay as an additive offset is **not** adopted (it brushes R8 and PF-17; OQ-IW-6). When the mode returns to HONEST (CLEAR, PF-12) no jump is detected because the displayed value tracked the true value during the return; a RETURN is a glide to truth, not a jump.

**IW-12. Honest tremor: driven only by physical stress, only when HONEST.**
*Rule.* A DIAL or LINEAR instrument whose `motion_mode` is HONEST **and** whose stress predicate holds draws tremor: an irregular positional offset of at most `TREMOR_AMP_PX` (+-1 art px, ART 7.3), at 6-10 Hz, around the displayed value, with no gleam, onset ramp `TREMOR_ONSET_MS`, ceasing with decay `TREMOR_DECAY_MS` when the predicate ends, and resting on a pixel. At zoom rung `k` 1 the amplitude is raised to keep at least `TREMOR_MIN_SCREEN_PX` (2) screen px (D.5; a refinement of ART 7.3's +-1, OQ-IW-4). The **stress predicate** (MVP) is any of: (i) an active hazard-mode FailureDescriptor on the bound node, mode in {1 LEAK, 2 SHORT, 3 OVERHEAT, 4 FREEZE, 5 BREACH, 7 OVERLOAD} (LS-11), with `sev` >= `TREMOR_SEV_MIN` (1), read through `failures(filter)`; never mode 10 WORN, 6 STARVE, 8 SEVERED, 9 SCRAM or **27 PATCH_WEEP** (a patched subject's steady weep, LS-11 row 27: `hp_cap < HP_FULL` with no mode 1-9 live, no `intensity_pm`; it is a consequence-channel witness of the patch, not a stress) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and never a compartment band descriptor (ATMO_COLD / HOT / THIN / DECOMP 20-23, AIRFLOW 24, PUDDLE 25): a band is a state, not a stress (advisor final-review amendment to R9: `warn_elapsed` is > 0 for every sev-1 subject, LSG D.11, so it is not a tremor input); (ii) an active OVERLOAD (mode 7) descriptor on a CABLE of the bound loop component, through `failures(filter)` (LSG:127); (iii) an active `SEAL_STRAIN` descriptor on an OPENING of the bound compartment, or a `LEAK` or `BREACH` descriptor on a HULL_SEGMENT or SEAM bounding it (subjects found through `nodes_in(cid)` and `neighbors(cid)`; read through `failures(filter)`); the subject lists of (i)-(iii) are precomputed at section load and recomputed only on a topology dirty key (`T`, `SEC/<id>`), never per frame. (iv) **`stress_active(section_id)`, off by default** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the MVP predicate is (i)-(iii) only, and (iv) is read only if the knob `TREMOR_USE_SECTION_STRESS` is true (default **false**, registered in Tuning Knobs); (K-13 resolved: the Graph now publishes the pure query, LS-19 additions, 2026-10-27; section stress is `stress_pm` = `BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT` 2000 while `step_no < stress_until_step`, set by M17 STRESS for `STRESS_HOLD_STEPS` 600) for an instrument in that section. **(iv) is provisional and off:** M17 STRESS has one caller, Attention, which this rule forbids as a tremor driver (a tremor driven by it would make an instrument show Attention, which the header and IW-28 forbid, AT-17), and AT C1 reads a needle moved by real STRESS damage as a consequence and not a tell; the creative-director's position is that (iv) as written is a pillar-level conflict, so it stays off until the Attention owner and the creative-director confirm the contrary reading (OQ-IW-38); with the knob false the predicate is (i)-(iii) and `stress_active` is not read by Instruments. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The predicate is refreshed once per Graph step (IW-5). Tremor is never driven by Attention, sanity, a timer or a lie. It is positional, not luminous (HR-13 exemption; shown on the card as well, suppressed under `reduced_motion`, D96) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. It is evaluated only when `motion_mode` is HONEST (advisor ruling R9; AT-17, AT:1415 C1).
