**On the card (Default applied 2026-10-27, pending user confirmation: D96 — the card shows the honest tremor, suppressed under `reduced_motion`; K-18 resolved) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.** The card shows the same honest tremor as its wall gauge (parity, IW-26; the amplitude is +-`TREMOR_AMP_PX` HUD art px, never scaled by the card multiple). With `reduced_motion` on, the tremor is suppressed on the card exactly as on the wall gauge and the held stress notch pair of IW-27 and D.10 shows in its place (two Hull Grey squares on the Ground plate above the dial) while the predicate holds and the reading is HONEST, so the stress tell never depends on motion for a player who has asked for less of it. The earlier objection (HR-13 says "no HUD shake"; ART:984 flags tremor's positional-not-luminous status as unconfirmed) is answered by this suppression and by the card frame, plate and keycaps never moving; the former fallback (the held notch on the card in all modes) is retired unless the user declines this default. Further accessibility-specialist advice is welcome when Accessibility Settings is designed, but nothing here waits on it.
*Why.* P2: the honest tremor is the instrument's share of the 10-30 s tell and is a real consequence the player can trace. "A needle moved by STRESS damage is a consequence, not a tell Attention drives" (AT C1). Because a tremor exists only on an honest reading, "shake = real" is a rule the game never breaks.
*Edge notes.* A patched or WORN subject carries only mode 10 WORN (LS-11, LS-23), so it does not tremble; its `warn_elapsed` is > 0 (LSG D.11), which is why `warn_elapsed` is not a tremor input. `alarm_eligible` (`sev` >= 3, LS-13) is not a tremor input; tremor begins at the first physical stress, always before the alarm class (`W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10 is the Graph's). If the predicate flaps at Graph-step rate, tremor flaps with it (10 Hz worst case); `TREMOR_HOLD_S` (default 0) is the playtest remedy. Tremor's perceptibility at `k` 1 on the largest field is marginal (D.7); the design accepts it, since the tell is for a player standing at the instrument. **Instrument tremor is never the only tell (stage-12 CD-GDD-ALIGN concern 6).** It is this system's share of the P2 10-30 s tell and P2 is carried by several channels: every stress source in predicates (i)-(iii) above must also have a sound tell or a world-witness tell owned by Adaptive Audio or Failure & Trail (strain creak, hiss, drip, spark or frost, per the consequence channel), so zooming out to `k` 1, or standing outside the light, never removes the warning. This is a cross-owner requirement checked by AC-IW-106 and routed to Adaptive Audio and Failure & Trail.

**IW-13. Motion-mode dispatch.**
*Rule.* The Filter's `motion_mode` is the only switch:

| `motion_mode` | Needle / meniscus | Numeral (card) | Settle | Tremor | Stress notch (RM) | Drawn value from |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| HONEST | `displayed_pm` through IW-11 and IW-12 | steps once per real change (throttled, IW-14) | yes | yes if predicate | yes if predicate | Instruments |
| GLIDE | `displayed_pm` verbatim (the Filter's trajectory, wander included) | continuous tick follows | no | no | no | the Filter's value; Instruments draw it |
| TICK | `displayed_pm` verbatim (the Filter's trajectory, wander included) | continuous tick, `T_tick` (PF-18) | no | no | no | same |
| STALE | `displayed_pm` verbatim (the Filter's trajectory, wander included) | held digits | no | no | no | same |
| RETURN | `displayed_pm` verbatim (the Filter's trajectory: the rate-limited follower to truth) | follows | no | no | no | same |

An honest gauge never glides; a lie never settles or trembles; the instrument never infers a lie, it only obeys the mode. (PF-7 edge note, PF-17; orchestrator ruling R8 of the Filter run)
*Why.* If Instruments could decide, the player could read the truth from which animation applies: a P4 oracle.
*Edge notes.* The card has the identical dispatch (IW-26). **TICK and STALE are modes of the card numeral only** (checkpoint ruling C2): a wall gauge carries no digit (IW-6), and a `lie_displayed_value` lie moves a FALSE_OK or FALSE_FAIL needle toward its zone and never holds it. No lying needle rests: whatever the numeral's mode, the needle draws the Filter's trajectory, which never rests (PF-17), so the wall gauge keeps its motion mode while the card is closed (PF-8, HR-5). The numeral's STALE hold is a card-visible mode of the same lie, not a second one; the gleam and the motion mode remain mandatory and only the sound is droppable (PF-8).

**IW-14. The numeral: honest ticking, the throttle, and who owns the scheduler.**
*Rule.* Numerals exist only on the card (IW-6). An honest numeral steps once per real change of `displayed_value` at the quantity's `lsb`, never tweens and never counts up; a transition is a digit-glyph substitution in tabular cells (fixed width, slashed zero) of only the cells whose glyph changed, and never inverts a plate (PF-18; HR-13 is then checkable per cell). Honest transitions are throttled to the same ceiling as lying ones: the card's numeral updates on the HUD's shared **hold grid** `HONEST_TICK_MS` (owner HUD & Suit Layer, HS-14, pinned 500 ms and ratified 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions, D91 iii) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; the 400-500 range is withdrawn, the HUD's window-feasibility check leaves 30 ticks as the only feasible grid, HS-47; PF-18's 334 is the bound without the beacon share and is not used here), at most `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` 3 transitions per rolling second; no second constant is registered here. At each grid boundary the numeral shows the **then-current** step (it may skip steps, never queued, never faded; it never shows a value the room did not have). The card's honest numeral changes only on the HUD's shared `HONEST_TICK_MS` grid boundary and adds no luminance event of its own (HS-47); a boundary at which no card digit changes is not an event; when the HUD's grid-skip rule skips a boundary the card shows the then-latest step at the next one, so honest numeral lag is at most `2 x HONEST_TICK_MS`. A lying numeral is the Filter's: a continuous tick or a stale hold under the shared scheduler `T_tick` = max(`LIE_TICK_MS` 400, N_r x 334) (400 / 668 / 1002 / 1336 ms for N_r 1..4; the 334 ms stagger floor keeps any closed 1 s window at or under `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` 3) (PF-18, registry `lying_digit_tick_schedule`); Instruments only draw the received digits. Formatting is integer-only: the displayed value is `displayed_value` converted by the quantity table (IW-9) with no float formatting; a temperature that rounds to -0 shows "0"; a value beyond the span shows the clamped bound with no "<" mark (a "<" would be a second reading); the format never reveals more precision than `lsb`. **`lsb` visibility lint:** for a lie-eligible dial, `lsb` must be fine enough that the digit changes at least every `T_VIS_MAX_S` during a glide at the floor rate (otherwise a lying numeral looks stale and the tri-modal rule loses a mode on the card): `p_o2` is therefore shown in kPa with one decimal (`lsb` 1 dkPa), not in whole kPa (D.12). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*
*Why.* ART 7.3 (honest numerals tick); HR-13 (no luminance change faster than 3 Hz); PF-18.
*Edge notes.* **CONFLICT K-16 (raised by the ux-designer):** locked fact 21 / ART 7.3 say an honest numeral ticks "one step per real change, never a tween", while HR-13 and `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` 3 cap digit-glyph substitutions at 3 per second; a breach would exceed the cap if every step were shown. This GDD throttles and shows the then-current step, exactly as HUD & Suit Layer does for its own honest numerals (HS-14 amends the same locked rule, and its K8 row accepts the amendment); exempting honest ticks from the ceiling is rejected because a photosensitive player gains nothing from the distinction. Honest grid boundaries on the card are not counted in the Filter's `N_r` (which counts lying readouts only); the combined HUD budget (card numeral plus suit numerals plus lying readouts) is the HUD's arbiter HS-47, which itself records an open counting unit and an ASK to the Filter and the accessibility-specialist when lying digits at `N_r` 2 or more fill the ceiling *(OQ-IW-9: HS-47 states that the card's honest numeral shares the HUD's grid boundary and adds no event; the open counting unit is HUD CF-HS-10)*. The digit mechanism (glyph-cell substitution from a `ui_` atlas versus a text node) is -> ADR candidate; tabular numerals, slashed-zero shaping and integer-scale snapping of a vector font are `[UNV]` for the pinned build and ART 7.1 makes them asset-test requirements. The same honest-digit mechanism is offered as a reusable component to HUD & Suit Layer for the field-check reading (IW-35).

**IW-15. Zone bands are coded by pattern first, hue second.**
*Rule.* Every dial and linear instrument carries zone bands derived **at build time** from LS-7 band names (D.2), never typed as numbers in instrument data. A band has a pattern class: SOLID (normal-class bands: `NORMAL`, `COMFORT`), HATCHED (caution: `THIN`, `LOW`, `COOL`, `HOT`, `FROST`), NOTCHED (critical: `HYPOXIC`, `ANOXIC`, `DECOMP`, `VACUUM`, `SCORCH`, `FREEZING`, `DEEP_COLD`; overload for `load`); the three must be distinguishable from each other at 25 % scale in greyscale (the hue-removed test). Gauge faces carry no loop hue (identity is the fitting silhouette and, while running, the pip); Alarm Red is never a zone colour (a static red band on every dial would make red common and decorative, violating ART 4.1). A zone narrower than `ZONE_MIN_ARC_PX` (6 art px of arc, D.2) fails a build lint: the instrument must use a larger size class or merge bands in data. The needle position is geometry; the needle is **not** coloured by the Graph's hysteretic `band_*` fields (advisor ruling R11).
*Why.* Hue-removed readability (ART 4.7, HR-5 test); LS-7 is the single source of bands.
*Edge notes.* Because LS-7 bands carry hysteresis (`H_P` 5 dkPa, `H_T` 500 mC) while dial zones are static intervals, the needle can sit inside the NORMAL zone while the Graph's `band_o2` still reads THIN: the dial is a continuous reading and no consumer compares them. The exact authored patterns (hatched dashes, split-rail, notched teeth) at a 3-row DIAL_S band are the pixel mock's to confirm (OQ-IW-3).

#### Group D. Lying surfaces, state frames and the alarm mark

**IW-16. The gleam is drawn here, on glass and edges only.**
*Rule.* Instruments draw the gleam from `Perceived.leak_amplitude_pm` and the Filter's phase: hue fringe `#B07BE6` (never the core `#8A3FD1`), at an **edge** only (glass edge, needle leading edge, housing edge), never a fill, peak channel at most `LEAK_MAX_CH` (:= `DECOY_MAX_CH` 0.85), produced at runtime by a material parameter or modulate and never baked into a sheet (reserved-hue scan, ART:1207). **World gauge:** on the instrument's own material by a per-instance parameter (zero added draws); *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **fallback if per-instance CanvasItem shader parameters are unavailable in 4.7.2 (`[UNV]`):** a child edge sprite per lying instrument, cut from the `_msk` edge slice, whose `modulate.a` carries the amplitude: no new ShaderMaterial (the LO-13 budget of five holds) and one extra draw per lying on-screen instrument (a pooled material per quantised amplitude is rejected because it would spend the material budget); the edge thickness requested is the effective art px of D.9 (`ceil(2/k)` clamped 1-2, so at least 2 screen px at every rung; inside the Filter's `[PLACEHOLDER]` `GLEAM_EDGE_PX` range 1-2, PF-16, OQ-IW-4). **Card (the answer to OQ-PF-11 / PF C-3 for the card):** Instruments draw the gleam on the card's glass and needle edge and the digit well's top and bottom edge, in HUD art px (1 HUD art px = `H` screen px, `H` >= 3, so HR-12's 2-screen-px stroke always holds), from the same amplitude and phase as the wall gauge. The card frame, bezel and rivets are an anchor and never carry violet (PF-18, SNV-8). Cut order if Filter spike S-1 fails: housing and glyph gleams first, **never** the needle or the card numeral well (PF-42's "never needle and readout") (advisor ruling R5).
*Why.* The Filter owns amplitude and phase but no layer-2 node; HUD & Suit Layer (designed in this run) supplies only the frame and the gleam helper; the card glass is Instruments' node.
*Edge notes.* **Hue risk (art-director finding).** A gleam multiplied by a coloured light leaves the violet band: `#B07BE6` under the beam core `#FFE2A8` shifts toward magenta (hue about 321 degrees, still inside 270-330), and under Chemlight `#86B896` it reads blue (about 244 degrees), darker. A lying gauge lit only by a glowstick would lose its hue mode. Requirement: the gleam is composited so its hue is not modulated by the light's colour (intensity gated by the host's lit factor, hue fixed) -> ADR candidate (shared with the Filter's spike S-1 and OQ-PF-6); the motion mode survives regardless and HR-5 needs only each mode sufficient, so this is a risk, not a break. Instance-parameter transport and batching are `[UNV]` HIGH risk.

**IW-17. The Module-Body Presenter.**
*Rule.* For each module sheet the Presenter selects one **honest** frame from the Graph state and, where tagged, from `perceived(MACHINE_FRAME | HOUSING_GLOW | STATE_GLYPH).frame_override`:

| Graph input (`node(id)`) | Honest frame from the sheet | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `state` 0 OK, `served_pm` > 0 | `running` | rotor / moving-medium region animates (pipe-side witnesses are Failure & Trail's and Lighting's; the *machine's own* medium is part of this frame) |
| `state` 0 OK, `served_pm` = 0 | `stopped` | idle, not failed |
| `state` 1 DEGRADED without a hazard mode (WORN) | `running` or `stopped` plus the wear overlay (IW-18) | no condition word, no bar |
| `state` 1 DEGRADED with a hazard mode (1-9) | `running` or `stopped` by `served_pm` exactly as state 0, plus the wear overlay; `cracked` only for housings and tanks whose live hazard is LEAK or BREACH; the lamp pair follows `served_pm` (lit while `served_pm` > 0) | the machine still functions at reduced `eff` (LS-10); no condition word, no bar |
| `state` 2 FAILED | `stopped` for pumps and rotors; `cracked` for housings and tanks | lamp pair `unlit` (no function, LS-10) |
| `state` 3 DESTROYED | `cracked` plus the dead frame | binding lost (IW-30) |
| `state` 4 SALVAGED, 5 ABSENT | module gone; no frame | |

`frame_override` swaps to the *other honest frame of the same pair* (running/stopped, whole/cracked, lamp lit/unlit); the Filter never supplies a frame the sheet lacks: SPRITE_SWAP eligibility is `has_both_frames(def_id, pair)`, **a requested Item DB query** that implements SN AC-SN-152's eligibility (it exists in no GDD today), and both frames of every lie-capable `mach_*` sheet sit on one sheet page. The Presenter reads the Graph for *which honest frame*, never for a displayed value. The **state glyph** is the same function: an 8x8 chamfered-square lens mark on the module housing with two frames of different *shape* (run: a filled lens with a 2-px inner tick; stop: a hollow lens with a 2-px diagonal bar) so they survive the hue-removed and greyscale tests; it carries no loop hue, no flow mark and no fitting silhouette (ART 8.2: an 8x8 mark never identifies a loop), and a glyph lie swaps to the other honest glyph frame *(orchestrator ruling: the art-director's 8x8 shape-coded lens adopted over the ux-designer's 16x16 hue-while-running glyph; a hue swap beside a possibly lying needle is an oracle and fact 14 reserves 16x16 for HUD glyphs)*. No other sprite of the same module may draw the honest state beside an override; a WORN patch plate is a separate TRUTH-graded sprite (a lie that cracks a whole machine over a patch plate is a content lint case for Derelict Authoring, `PATCH_OVER_OVERRIDE`). (advisor ruling R7; PF-45, SN:1346)
*Why.* A SPRITE_SWAP lie is only fair if both frames are ordinary state tokens the player has seen.
*Edge notes.* ART 7.2 / 8.9 line 12 ("icons never swap to express state") needs an exemption for reading surfaces whose lie *is* a frame choice (state glyph, machine frames): bible refresh BR-1, the art-director's. Direction of change: the frame follows the same Graph value the audio reads (IW-32). Stage-3 machine frames and the stage-2 lamp-housing pair are the cuttable tier (IW-34).

**IW-18. WORN and patched presentation.**
*Rule.* A WORN subject (DEGRADED, no hazard mode, `medium` NONE, `acoustic` NONE; LS-11) shows module-body wear: the wear overlay frame (scuffs, and a visible patch plate if patched) on the machine and on the instrument bound to it; the gauge shows the perceived reading of the module's output as usual. No word, bar, rating, colour grade or numeral says "worn" (BS-6, IA-39). A patched subject is permanently DEGRADED `sev` 1 (LS-10, D.4) and keeps its patch plate; the plate is driven by the node's patched state (`hp_cap < HP_FULL`; the Graph's PATCH_WEEP id 27 row uses the same condition and draws the weep on the consequence channel, which Instruments never touch) and not by the WORN descriptor *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Failure & Trail draws nothing for WORN (FT:232, :637). The wear overlay is corruptible only through the machine-frame swap at stage 3 (a false-fixed scrubber looks whole while its witness says otherwise); it is honest at stages 0-2.
*Why.* Routed thread 5; BS-6 and IA-39: no condition surface.
*Edge notes.* Wear frames are authored once per module sheet, never per loose item (AD-BS-5 forbids a per-loose-item wear set as "the inspect card in disguise"). WORN is shown here on module-body surfaces only; the card shows no wear (IW-22). LS-11 (mode 10 WORN: "readable on the sprite, the gauge and the inspect card") and LSG:168 are read as: WORN reaches the card only through the module's reduced output reading; the card carries no wear frame, word or mark (advisor ruling R2).

**IW-19. The in-world alarm mark: TRUTH-graded, on the housing, alarm-eligible only.**
*Rule.* While the bound subject has an `alarm_eligible` failure descriptor (LS-13; severity >= 3; read through `failures(filter)`), an instrument housing that can carry it shows one Alarm Red `#E0102A` chamfered-square warning mark (ART:817 shape, ART 7.2 canonical size 16x16 art px, `ALARM_MARK_WORLD_PX` 16, "nothing smaller") on the **housing, not the face**. The world twin's exclamation stroke is **Void Steel**, not Paper White (Paper White is UI-only, fact 16, ART 4.6; the HUD twin keeps Paper White). **Where the mark may be drawn (stage-12 CD-GDD-ALIGN concern 1, P4 cost rule; rewritten 2026-10-27 as a decision table).** The mark is drawn only on a housing that is physically at the alarm-eligible subject. That is a property of placement data (`mount_room`, `subject_room`, `host_node`, IW-8) and not of a tag: the decision table below lists every case. An instrument with `hops(mount_room, subject_room)` >= 1 (REMOTE) satisfies none of the yes rows, so the lethal remote FALSE_OK of Beat C is never put beside its own contradiction by a lit Alarm Red mark on the lying instrument's housing, and believing the mark still costs a walk to where the subject is (P4: checking costs time, risk or trust). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The mark itself stays TRUTH-graded and untagged; this rule decides only on which housings it is drawn, from authored placement data (`mount_room`, `subject_room`, `host_node` and the binding), never from a lie state. The mark lives on a DIAL_L housing meeting that test (in the 36 px horizontal bay beside the 60x46 housing), on machine and console panels and on the tank prop meeting it; **DIAL_S carries none** *(orchestrator ruling: the art-director's narrowing adopted over the ux-designer's 4-row overlap of the DIAL_S arc; a 16x16 mark cannot sit on a 46x35 housing in a 48x48 cell; K-15 is therefore reframed from "overlap" to "which housings carry the mark", route art-director; the DIAL_S instrument's consequence-channel hiss, draught and sweep are its alarm)*. It is an **untagged node** (PF-4 default TRUTH), separate from every corruptible ref, so no HOUSING_GLOW or MACHINE_FRAME lie can add, move or suppress it; it is a witness and never itself a lying surface (SN L-A, SN:262). It never appears as the first warning (`alarm_eligible` is the only state in which an alarm-class presentation may play, and `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10 guarantees a prior tell on endogenous transitions). `alarm_eligible` is a field of the `FailureDescriptor` (LS-14) that also requires the fairness gate to be open: Instruments **never re-derive it from `sev`**. The mark pulses in the beacon rhythm (2-3 s period, never faster than 3 Hz, HR-13; ART 7.3), holds solid under reduced motion, is non-emissive, casts no light and is lit-gated by the renderer like the housing. The needle and zones keep showing the perceived reading. The card does not carry the mark. **Decoupling rule (so the mark is never a free falsifier):** a zone's onset (an LS-7 band edge) and `alarm_eligible` (`sev` >= 3) are different quantities and do not coincide; **the absence of the mark never proves a needle in an alarm zone is lying** (the rule is one-directional: absence is never evidence; presence is the ship speaking and outranks the needle, E-IW-37), and a lying FALSE_FAIL needle in an alarm zone without a mark is not distinguishable from a true reading approaching alarm on that evidence alone (advisor ruling R14; K-8).
*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **IW-19 decision table (housing, mount, binding, `hops`).** A mark also requires an `alarm_eligible` descriptor on the bound subject (above); the table decides only whether the housing may carry one.

| Row | Housing and mount | Binding | `hops` | Mark? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| (a) | machine or console panel (a module's own housing; no instrument needed) | `host_node` = the alarm-eligible node | 0 by construction | yes |
| (b) | LINEAR on a tank or reservoir prop | `host_node` = the tank's `stock` node, which carries the descriptor | 0 by construction | yes |
| (c) | DIAL_L, WALL_FACE | compartment ref with `mount_room` = `subject_room` | 0 | yes |
| (d) | DIAL_L, WALL_FACE | compartment ref with `mount_room` != `subject_room` | >= 1 (REMOTE) | **no** |
| (e) | DIAL_L, WALL_FACE | node ref or loop-component ref (a bus, a cable, a pump) | any | **no** (not on that node's own panel) |
| (f) | DIAL_L, MACHINE_PANEL | `host_node` = `subject_node` | 0 | yes (the panel's mark of row (a)) |
| (g) | DIAL_L, MACHINE_PANEL | `host_node` != `subject_node` | any | **no** |
| (h) | DIAL_S, GLYPH, FRAME, the card | any | any | **no** (no 16x16 bay; hiss, draught and sweep are its alarm) |

**Count.** Of the 18 manifest instruments, 6 can carry the mark: 3 DIAL_L by row (c) (`inst_cryo_bay_p_o2`, `inst_cryo_bay_T`, `inst_b_reactor_T`) and 3 tank sight-glasses by row (b) (`inst_res_main_level`, `inst_home_fuel_level`, `inst_res_b_level`). The other 12 never do: 3 DIAL_L by rows (d) and (e) (`inst_engine_bus_load`, `inst_a_machine_T`, `inst_b_dock_p_o2`), 6 DIAL_S and 3 GLYPH by row (h). Each manifest instrument's row is in the IW-29 table. **P0 asset re-justification.** The 16x16 world mark stays one P0 sheet because its principal hosts are not instruments: the machine and console panels of every alarm-eligible node (rows (a) and (f)) and the tank props (row (b)) carry it, and the manifest's own dials add only 3 hosts. If the art-director rejects the second Alarm Red surface (BR-3) the fallback is unchanged: no mark.

*Why.* P2 (the alarm class is never first) and P4 / SN L-A (a true alarm cannot be suppressed by a lie).
*Edge notes.* ART:375 / :400 / :463 grant "a failing instrument's warning mark"; ART:817 says the mark "lives on the critical strip" and "Alarm Red appears nowhere else on the UI": the bible is split against itself; the art-director's reading (ART 7.2's sentence is about the screen-space UI layers; a world mark on a physical housing is not UI) is adopted pending its refresh (BR-3). Fallback (b), if the art-director rejects a second Alarm Red surface: no mark (sweep and sound only), which changes nothing else here. The mark's exact absence on DIAL_S is confirmed against the pixel mock S-A1.

**IW-20. Sight-glass split, and linear instruments.**
*Rule.* Flow patterns on pipes (chevrons, dash-pulse, banding, static on dead branches) and the motion of a pipe's moving medium are world-state witnesses (TRUTH_PX, PF-5 rows 17a and 17b), drawn by Lighting and Failure & Trail, never by this GDD. The **fill-level reading of a tank sight-glass** (the home fuel tank, WM-20; the coolant reservoirs `res_main` and `res_b`; the O2 tank at the top-up port, SE VA-23) is a corruptible module-body reading drawn here as a LINEAR instrument whose level is the Graph `stock` (after M16 mutations). A machine's own moving medium belongs to its machine frame (IW-17) and is corruptible only by stage-3 SPRITE_SWAP. A LINEAR instrument is a vertical glass tube (housing about 12x62 on the 48x96 tank prop, clear of the pipe band because the tank is a prop) with a baked fluid column in the stored medium's palette hex (stock, not flow: lit-only and not emissive, so a dead loop's tank still shows its fuel), a 6x2 meniscus bead (6 wide, 2 tall, IW-7 arithmetic) that is the element that settles, trembles or glides, scale ticks at 0/25/50/75/100 % (no numerals), and a zone strip in the same three patterns; `travel_px` 50 `[PLACEHOLDER]` so that 1 art px = 20 permille = the store dirty-key quantum (LS-20), one size at MVP *(orchestrator ruling: the art-director's 50 px single size over the ux-designer's 24/16 two sizes; a longer travel keeps a lie's visible departure above 4 px and the glide rate above the never-rest rationale)*. The Filter's angle maths has no linear branch; the linear glide band is stated in pixels (D.11; request to the Filter, K-5): a visible departure `D_VIS_MIN_PX` and a rate floor `LINEAR_GLIDE_RATE_MIN_PXS` (0.5 px/s, the same tip-speed rationale as PF-17). A LINEAR instrument below a minimum travel is ineligible for travel lies (`TRAVEL_MIN_PX`, `[PLACEHOLDER]` 40; it keeps gleam and numeral modes). (advisor ruling R8)
*Why.* LS-22 Fuel row ("sight-glass module-body (corruptible)") reconciled with ART 2.3 and 3.5 ("fluid in the pipe proves the pipe"). The puddle, the drip and the pipe's flow pattern are the witnesses for the fuel tank; they share no node with the glass (FT:314).
*Edge notes.* The fuel loop's colour-blind backup (ART 4.7 Power vs Fuel) is the fill-level glass itself, so a lying home-tank sight-glass corrupts a backup channel on purpose; the pipe's flow pattern and the puddle remain the TRUTH backups. This is pillar-legal, but a colour-blind player then loses one more channel of truth than other players do, so P4's "at least one channel of truth" must hold per player and not on average: for each lie-eligible LINEAR instrument a hue-independent TRUTH witness (the pipe flow pattern or the puddle) must lie within `WITNESS_HOPS_MAX` (stage-12 CD-GDD-ALIGN concern 5; AC-IW-105; routed to the accessibility-specialist next to OQ-IW-13). The fluid column is a baked palette hex in the `_alb` sheet, a deliberate deviation from ART 8.3 ("baking hue into albedo breaks the dead-loop rule") because it is stock not flow; the running-emission path stays on `_msk`. The pod has no dash at MVP, so no pod sight-glass exists (WM AB-2, K-10).

#### Group E. The inspect view

**IW-21. Targeting, permission and the prompt.**
*Rule.* An instrument is an OBJECT subject of IA-2 class `INSTRUMENT`, registered through Interaction's `sync_section` from Layout's `subject_anchors(section_id)` (IA-37), counted toward `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` and `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` by name (D.8). Focus is the standard aim-weighted proximity among in-reach, in-arc, **lit** interactables (PC-6; `REACH_PX` 72, half-angle 70 degrees, `FOCUS_STICKY_MARGIN` 0.10). The prompt is Interaction's and HUD's standard lit-only plate: the keybind plus a state-neutral verb (`verb_inspect`, "R . Inspect"; RT on gamepad; the key is Interaction's and IA-38 and AC-IA-112 carry it since the 2026-10-27 consistency pass), drawn after `PROMPT_SHOW_DEBOUNCE_TICKS` 13 (HUD-owned; IA:892) and hidden immediately on unlit. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **`inspect` acts on the primary focus when that is an INSTRUMENT, and otherwise on the secondary INSTRUMENT slot**: the best lit INSTRUMENT in reach and arc by the same aim-weighted score (its own sticky margin), chosen independently of the primary focus. This is the arbitration for a MACHINE_PANEL gauge: with one sticky focus either the gauge took focus from the repair or valve prompt, or the gauge could not be inspected and `interact` on it gave no feedback. Now a valve or repair target holds the primary focus and keeps `interact`; the gauge on the same panel is inspected from the secondary slot; when both exist the plate lists both verbs (the primary verb, then `verb_inspect`) and each button acts on its own target. `interact` on an instrument still does nothing (no verb). No cycle control is added. This is a requested amendment to PC-6 and IA-2 (one primary focus plus one inspect-only secondary slot) and is provisional until the Controller and Interaction reply (OQ-IW-39, AC-IW-113). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Secondary-focus affordance:** when the secondary slot is occupied the `verb_inspect` row of the plate is drawn **anchored beside the secondary gauge** (the standard lit-only plate grammar at the gauge's `world_to_hud(anchor_px)`), and the primary verb's row stays on the primary target, so the player sees which gauge `inspect` will read; if the plates would overlap the inspect row takes the side away from the primary row. The marker is a plate anchor and a keycap, never a hue or a state word (pattern, not colour: the same keycap shape on every plate). **Which rule is in force until OQ-IW-39 closes: the fallback.** Until the Controller and Interaction accept the secondary slot, the placement rule governs: no INSTRUMENT shares a MACHINE_PANEL with an interactable verb target, enforced by the lint `PANEL_GAUGE_VERB_CONFLICT` (a build ERROR, owned by Derelict Authoring's lint set, IW-31); the amendment, if accepted, relaxes the lint to a warning. Under the fallback a battery dial (`inst_battery_stock`, `inst_b_a1_stock`), which the manifest places on its panel beside the breaker verb, would trip the lint, so until the amendment is accepted those two dials are placed `WALL_FACE` on the wall beside the panel and not on it; if the amendment is declined that placement stays and nothing becomes uninspectable. **Input versus prompt.** `PROMPT_SHOW_DEBOUNCE_TICKS` 13 and `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS` 20 delay **drawing the plate only**; `inspect` is accepted on any frame in which the target holds focus and `lit(ref)`, including the frames before the plate is drawn and the frames straight after a close (IA: "the verb works as soon as it is focused"). The debounce clock starts at the tick the target became the stable focus and restarts when the plate hides; it never gates a press. **`inspect` is a permission rule only:** a presentation-only context switch (`inspect_card`), not a simulation command, permitted only on a lit instrument; no command type of that name exists. Gamepad proximity focus plus this one contextual action satisfies HR-14 (advisor ruling R23, D12; IA-38, AC-IA-112). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*
*Why.* One focus model (PC-6); a cycle control or "nearest lit instrument regardless of focus" would add input or break the one-focus model.
*Edge notes.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: IA-38's own dormant sentence and the other stale assumptions listed here were refreshed by their owners in the 2026-10-27 consistency pass.)* If the card is cut, `inspect` stays bound and dormant (IA-38) and stage-1 reach loses the card surface; nothing else changes (advisor ruling R1; K-12, which lists the stale "dormant" assumptions owed a refresh: BS:183, BS:332, PC:210 row 10, PC OQ 4, IA OQ 16, IA-38's "If the card does not ship" sentence). This GDD answers PC OQ 4 and IA OQ 16: **the card ships live at MVP**, because it is the only numeric precision path (ART 3.1 forbids numerals on world gauges), HR-14 needs the mirror card, and SN-16 reach stage 1, PF-18, AC-PF-23 and AC-SN-122 are all written against a card that lies from stage 1.

**IW-22. Card content: label, one numeral with its unit, the same dial. No verdict.**
*Rule.* The card carries exactly: (1) the instrument's label = `name_key` (the reading's name, e.g. the quantity on a specific fitting; caps, Hull Grey on Ground, 5 art px cap); (2) the same needle or meniscus and zone bands as the wall instrument, at HUD art px, drawn at an integer multiple `CARD_MAG` of HUD art px (**fixed at 1**, range 1-2; checkpoint ruling C7: the 72x72 plate at `H` 6, the maximum on 1080p, is 432 px, 40 % of the height, and at `CARD_MAG` 2 the plate would break `CARD_MAX_FRAC_H` 0.5 at maximum `H`, so 2 cannot be the default; a conditional `CARD_MAG` 2 where it fits is a Vertical Slice revisit, OQ-IW-15); (3) one numeral with its unit in the bitmap instrument face (tabular numerals, slashed zero), Paper White `#DDE4EA`, 7 art px cap (21 screen px at `H` 3). Never OK / FAILED / DEGRADED / WORN or any condition word, rating or bar; no reason string; no true value beside a lying one; no lie count, stage name, verdict or sanity-correlated icon (PF-38); no module-state frame and no wear; no alarm mark (IW-19); no loop glyph (the art-director proposed a 16x16 Graphite loop glyph; R2's list is closed, so it is not carried, OQ-IW-16). All text goes through `tr()` with StringName keys (advisor ruling R2; K-3). **Division with HUD & Suit Layer** (HS-57, HS-48): HUD supplies the card's *frame register*: the chamfered bezel nine-slice, the single `Theme`, the tabular-numeral label and the gleam-on-glass helper; this GDD owns the card's *contents* (label, dial, numeral, glass), its context and its parity with the wall gauge. The frame is bit-identical with the Filter on or off and never gleams (HS-3); the gleam is applied to the card's reading glass through the HUD's helper, driven by the amplitude and phase the Filter supplies (IW-16).
*Why.* BS-6 ("no ... inspect-card condition"), IA-39 (no reason string), FT-4 and SN L-A: a state word is an oracle with no world analogue; the lie can only be a number and a needle. IDB:1074's "may name the module and its state in words" is answered **no** for state and **yes** only for the instrument label (routed to Item DB as a refresh).
*Edge notes.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Why open the card: three authored decisions the zone band cannot answer.** The card ships live at MVP, so it needs a stated use beyond "precision"; each row ties a decision to an authored instrument and to a number in this GDD.

| Decision | Instrument and authored fact | Why the band or the needle is not enough | What the card adds |
|---|---|---|---|
| Is the second opinion agreeing, or has a lie started? | the two `p_o2` dials, `inst_cryo_bay_p_o2` and `inst_engine_room_p_o2` (L-2I partners) | A floor-rate glide moves `p_o2` 1.5 dkPa/s (PF D.2 at 24 px); one tip pixel is 3.0 dkPa, so the needle departs by a pixel after about 2.0 s and the zone band not at all | The lying numeral ticks in single `lsb` steps (1 dkPa), so its digit has changed within about 0.7 s (D.12 `T_digit`), 1.3 s sooner than the needle shows a pixel; the honest partner numeral moves only in the Graph's 5 dkPa quanta (LS-20), so "the digit keeps moving while the partner holds" is the readable tell, and the numeral is no more precise than the quantum *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2)* |
| Is the reactor room still below the heat damage onset? | `inst_b_reactor_T` in the HOT band (42 C authored, DA); `HEAT_DMG_ONSET_MC` 45000 sits inside the HOT zone (40 .. 70 C) and the dial draws no tick there | The band says HOT for the whole 30 K; the needle's 2 px between 42 C and 45 C is not a threshold the player can see | A whole-degree numeral to compare with the onset the suit taught (the Graph's `T` quantum is 1000 mC, so the numeral resolves exactly the degree and no finer) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| How much fuel is left against the hop? | `inst_home_fuel_level` (percent, `lsb` 10 permille) against `HOP_FUEL_ML` (WM-20, a placeholder) | The sight-glass resolves 20 permille per pixel and carries no mark for the cost of a hop | A numeral formatted in whole percent to decide whether to refuel before committing the pod; the honest value moves in 20 permille (2 %) store quanta (LS-20), so the numeral is good to 2 %, not 1 % *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |

The cost of opening is **time and keeping the gauge in the beam**; opening the card adds no Attention stimulus (Attention has no `inspect` class and the beam's light term accrues whether or not the card is open; confirmation requested, OQ-IW-40). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The card is therefore essentially free beyond what reading the gauge already costs, and the predicted equilibrium is that a player opens it often; that is accepted (D94 stands, and card parity makes it harmless). The exposure in Beat A comes from the dark room and from standing lit, not from the card. If the user wants the card to carry a real cost, the option is an Attention stationary-in-beam term, listed as an explicit option for the Attention owner in OQ-IW-40; this GDD does not add one.

"Magnified" (ART:747) means the same ref, the same drawn value and the same leak at HUD scale `H` with a label and a numeral, not a mixel-scaled sprite: at the default view (1080p, `k` 3, `H` 3) the card dial has the same screen size as the wall dial (the ui-programmer's C-UI-2); the card's value is zoom-independence (3.0x the world dial at `k` 1, 6.0x at 2160p `k` 1), an opaque Ground plate, the label and the numeral (D.7). `CARD_MAG` is 1 at MVP; a conditional `CARD_MAG` 2 where the plate fits is a Vertical Slice revisit (OQ-IW-15), not an MVP decision.

**IW-23. Card placement.**
*Rule.* The card is one opaque Ground plate (HR-2) at HUD scale `H_card` (D.6), at plate size 72x72 HUD art px `[PLACEHOLDER]` at `CARD_MAG` 1 (label row, dial block, numeral row, margins), snapped to the HUD art-px grid, anchored beside its instrument through `world_to_hud(anchor_px)` (the unjolted pose, quantised to the HUD grid; PIPE C11) and edge-clamped inside the 5 % safe margin like prompt plates. The **side** is chosen at open by trying right, left, above, below the instrument's screen footprint; the first candidate that (a) fits the safe area, (b) does not overlap the instrument's footprint, (c) does not cover the **chest-point keep-out** (a disc around the per-facing chest point, `CARD_KEEPOUT_CHEST_R`) and (d) does not cross the **beam corridor** (a capsule from the chest point to the instrument anchor, half-width `CARD_KEEPOUT_BEAM_HALFW`) and (e) does not cover a **teammate's figure** (a disc of `CARD_KEEPOUT_CHEST_R` around each other seat's chest point), a **lit interactable in reach** (its footprint) or the instrument's **witness anchor** (`witness_anchor_px`, a disc of `CARD_KEEPOUT_WITNESS_R`, so the plate cannot hide the drip, frost or crewmate that is the honest second reading) wins and is **latched**. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Every candidate is clamped to the safe rect and slid along its free axis on the HUD grid **before** it is tested, so the rectangle that is tested is the rectangle that would be drawn. If none qualifies, (a) and (b) stay hard and the soft constraints are relaxed in a fixed order: first `H_card` is stepped down one step at a time to `HUD_MIN_SCALE` and every candidate is tried again; only if no clean candidate exists at any `H_card` is a soft overlap accepted, choosing the candidate with the fewest violations in the order (c) chest disc, (e) teammate, interactable and witness, (d) beam corridor, then the least overlap area, then the larger `H_card`. The earlier fallback, "farthest from the chest point, clamped", could clamp the plate over the instrument it magnifies (reachable at 720p, `H` 6, `k` 4) and is retired. While the camera is stable the plate does not move; under camera drift (look-ahead continues while aim is live) its position is re-projected from `world_to_hud(anchor_px)` each frame on the grid so it stays attached to its instrument, and the side is re-chosen only on a cut (zoom, window resize, camera cut) or when the latched rect becomes invalid; a re-choice is a cut, never a slide. The plate never covers the instrument it magnifies (a hard rule under every fallback), the player's figure or the beam cone's origin (the beam stays the brightest thing, ART 3.5; avoided by (c), (d) and (e) whenever a clean candidate exists, D.6 reachability) (advisor ruling R3).
*(orchestrator ruling: R3's "held while the camera is stable and re-projected on a cut" is read as "does not move while nothing moves, follows its anchor under drift, side changes only on a cut". Read literally as a fixed screen position it would detach the card from its instrument by up to the look-ahead distance, the ui-programmer's C-UI-4; no Pipeline change is needed. The ux-designer's `CARD_REANCHOR_PX` drift threshold is retired in favour of per-frame follow.)*
*Why.* "The same instrument magnified" needs the card beside it; PIPE C11 names "the inspect card's anchor"; the player must keep the gauge lit, so the card must not hide the beam.
*Edge notes.* The HUD's non-Ground cap `HUD_NONGROUND_PX_MAX` (HS-56) is measured "at rest" with no prompt, mark or notice, and the card exists only while a lit instrument is being inspected, so the card is outside that measurement; the card's own non-Ground share (about 0.7 % of a 1080p frame at `H` 3 and `CARD_MAG` 1, D.6; about 1.04 % at `CARD_MAG` 2, art-director estimate, which is not an MVP setting) is stated here so the HUD's UX spec can record the exclusion. No HUD element shakes under camera jolt (HR-13); `world_to_hud` is the unjolted pose. The world is never dimmed behind the card (ART 7.4).

**IW-24. Card lifecycle: immediate close, no dwell, real time.**
*Rule.* The card opens on `inspect` while the target (the primary focus or the secondary INSTRUMENT slot, IW-21) is lit and its ref is resolvable; there is no re-open debounce and no cooldown *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. It appears as a cut on the next render frame: no scale-in, fade, slide or pop (HR-13 forbids HUD scale change). It closes immediately, in the same render frame, on: `lit(ref)` false; `cancel` or B; a second `inspect`; kernel restore or `hard_start`; the instrument's binding lost (DESTROYED, SALVAGED, ABSENT or unresolvable); the instrument leaving reach; a section transit; **movement intent past the dead-zone (the card closes and the avatar moves in the same frame, IW-25)**; **any context change other than back to `gameplay`** (pause or `chart_table`, `chat`) and any `loco_state` change to `STAGGERED`, `DOWNED`, `DEAD` or `IN_POD` (PC contexts note), each in the same render frame and **without re-opening** when the context returns. Re-open needs a new press and nothing else: only the plate's redraw obeys `PROMPT_SHOW_DEBOUNCE_TICKS` 13 (HUD-owned; IA:892, IA:1085; cited by name, no second value is registered), and a press is never dropped for it (IW-21). The real `cancel` key is X (B on a pad). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Light-loss unlatch cue:** a close whose cause is `lit(ref)` false (the beam drifted off) is the only close that is not the player's own act, so it plays a distinct, lighter **unlatch** cue (UI bus, `UI_INSTRUMENT_CARD_UNLATCH` requested from Audio, which owns naming and assets, provisional) on the same render frame, with no fade and no visual transition beyond the cut; no other close cause plays it. On the first light-loss close per profile, when `lit(ref)` next rises the `verb_inspect` plate is shown without the `PROMPT_SHOW_DEBOUNCE_TICKS` wait (once), using the existing keycap and no new text, so a reader can tell the rule ("it closes when the light leaves") from a bug. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The card is **not** a pause: the sim keeps ticking, the card runs in real time on `present_time`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2)* **Resolved 2026-10-27 — delegated ruling G2 (re-review; user delegated to best judgment): pause closes the card, the creative-director's stated lean (P3: precision is a transient act that costs standing lit, not a state you bank).** Pause is a context change (`chart_table`), so it closes the card in the same render frame like any other; the needle then freezes with `present_time`. On resume the needle is bit-identical, the card stays closed, its plate does not animate back in, and a new `inspect` press is needed. A direction held through the pause neither re-opens the card nor is consumed by it (the card is already closed at the pause; held-input handling after a resume is the Controller's, OQ-IW-44). The IA-6 exemption "the inspect card of an already-lit instrument" means only that opening is not re-validated by `act_gate` as a command; it never permits a card on an unlit instrument (advisor ruling R4; EM-22). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*
*Why.* EM-22: keeping `lit` true after the light went "would be a window where the sim says lit and the world is dark, a P1 lie"; P3: precision costs staying lit.
*Edge notes.* `lit(ref)` is read each render frame, so a card closes at most one tick after the light goes. At a pool edge the card closes once on the first false and stays closed: no strobe (HR-13). If the focus moves to another instrument while the card is open, the card stays on its latched ref; there is no navigation between instruments at MVP. A card opens only from the `gameplay` context on a press, never because `lit` turned on while the player was in `chart_table` or `chat`. A press whose ref has no instrument record is consumed silently with a diagnostic (no error text, no sound).

**IW-25. Card input, co-op and falsification.**
*Rule.* In the `inspect_card` context *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: entering the context sends `set_move_intent {NONE}` and clears the run latch but does not cancel a running channel (PC contexts note: the second-instrument check on the pipe being welded costs nothing); zoom stays reachable; `inspect`, `cancel` (X) and B close the card; the sim keeps ticking and the HUD critical strip stays live, so a breach during inspection is felt through the consequence channel. **Movement past the dead-zone closes the card and moves the avatar in the same frame; no move press is swallowed.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* Two refinements that do not reverse D94: (1) **held-at-entry latch**: a direction already held (a key or a stick octant) at the moment the context is entered is latched and is not a fresh move; it neither closes the card nor moves the avatar until it returns to neutral or changes octant, so pressing `inspect` while running does not close the card on the next frame (the latch is the Controller's, requested, OQ-IW-44); (2) **card dead-zone margin for a gamepad stick**: while the card is open the stick's effective dead-zone is `MOVE_DEADZONE + CARD_PAD_DEADZONE_MARGIN` (a tunable, default 0.10 normalised `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 0-0.25, exposed to the accessibility settings), so stick drift or a tremor in the hand does not close the card unintentionally; a stick inside the enlarged dead-zone does nothing, as inside the plain one, and a deliberate push past it closes the card and moves in the same frame. A keyboard has no margin. The earlier rule zeroed and swallowed the first move press, which with lethal friendly fire and restart from the last autosave could kill a player by eating an input, and the UI frame must never betray the player. P3's stillness cost survives because the gauge must stay in the beam: a player who steps away loses `lit` anyway. A move also interrupts a running channel as any move does (PC-7). **Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): D94 ratified.** The PC context matrix is amended (`move_*` in `inspect_card`: closes the card, then live) and the Controller's GDD carries the amendment; the buffer-and-replay fallback is retired (OQ-IW-17, AC-IW-39) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. **Aim and cursor (K-14 closed 2026-10-27):** `set_aim` stays live so the chest lamp can hold the gauge lit, and the OS cursor is hidden and inert in `inspect_card` (PC-16, the PC context matrix, HS-52). The card is seat-local: never replicated, never visible to another seat, never a command; each seat's card reads `perceived(ref, its own PlayerId)`; a teammate's beam keeps `lit(ref)` true for my card (lit is world state) but my reach loss closes it. **Opening the card is not a falsification:** it is not a channel, lies identically to its gauge and so can never debunk; it calls no `record_falsification`; OQ-SN-12's INSPECT `check_kind` is reserved for any future *channelled* inspect verb (VS-A) (advisor ruling R13, R20).
*Why.* SN-16 / AD-C1: the card must never be a free falsifier; P4 needs a precision tool that is not a truth oracle. Answers the condition in AC-SN-111.
*Edge notes.* A teammate's friendly harm while the player stands still in the card context is the consequence channel's business: the player may close the card at any time at no cost, by `inspect`, `cancel` or simply by moving. Two cards cannot open per client (one seat per client).

**IW-26. Card parity: the same instrument, not a second one.**
*Rule.* The card uses the **same** `PerceptionRef` as its wall gauge (there is no card ref and no card lie), reads the same `Perceived` through the same memo, shows the same drawn value (it reads the instrument's own settle triple and tremor state), the same motion mode, the same gleam parameters and the same HR-5 tri-modal tell, from stage 1. A lie that starts, ends or changes while the card is open appears on the card in the same frame as on the wall gauge, with no flash of the true value. Closing and reopening a card on an active lie shows the same `Perceived` and plays no new sound (the sound is the Filter's request on the ref, not on the card). Opening the card never replays a settle and never sweeps the needle in from zero. "Second instrument" in P4 is always a *different* gauge elsewhere, never the card. The card dial renders the same angle set as the wall needle (it must not be a more precise render of a lying value than the wall is of the same value). The card numeral is `displayed_value` of the **same** `.needle` ref's `Perceived` (checkpoint ruling C2): it is not a second lie target and carries no `lie_surface_id` of its own. In every non-HONEST mode the card needle draws `displayed_pm` verbatim (the Filter's trajectory, wander included, which never rests), while TICK (continuous tick) and STALE (held digits) govern the numeral only, so the card and the wall gauge never disagree about which motion mode is active (PF-9, PF-18, AC-PF-23; HR-14).
*Why.* Otherwise the card is either a free falsifier or a separate lie target.
*Edge notes.* The card has no module-frame surface, so if the wall shows a stage-3 `whole` frame while the module is `cracked`, the card shows only the reading and there is nothing for it to disagree with. Stress is the one honest-only difference of *form* that may arise (tremor versus the held notch under `reduced_motion`, K-18 resolved by D96); it is honest-only on both and never differs in a lie *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.

#### Group F. Boundaries, content floors and fallbacks

**IW-27. Reduced motion and accessibility substitutes.**
*Rule.* With reduced motion (HR-8): honest settle becomes a **step** (drawn = `displayed_pm`); honest tremor is replaced by a **held stress notch pair** (each `max(NOTCH_PAIR_PX, ceil(NOTCH_MIN_SCREEN_PX / k))` art px, at least 4 screen px at every rung *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*), shown while the IW-12 predicate holds **and** `motion_mode` is HONEST, and never on a lying reading (it would be an oracle); the alarm mark holds solid. Glide, wander and lying-tick scaling are the Filter's (PF-39: `RM_GLIDE_SCALE` 0.6, rate floor not below 0.3 deg/s, wander never scaled, lying tick never below `LIE_TICK_MS`) and are not restated. The notch is two Void Steel nicks cut into the world bezel's top edge about 4 px either side of centre, and, on the card, two Hull Grey squares on the Ground plate above the dial *(orchestrator ruling: the art-director's placement adopted; R19 said "on the bezel", but HR-2 forbids Hull Grey on a bezel fill, so the card's notch sits on the Ground side of the dial rim)*. No setting removes a leak mode, hides the gleam or the card numeral, or adds an Attention or sanity surface. Instrument labels and the card obey the 21/15 text tier and UI scale `H`; the instrument face is a bitmap font at integer `H` only (advisor ruling R19). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* **Reduce flashing defaults ON at first launch** (user ruling, D79): the card's honest numeral already rides the shared hold grid (D.3) and adds no flash surface that the default would need to suppress; under the HUD's stricter arbiter for reduce flashing (HS-47) a grid boundary may be skipped and the card shows the then-latest step at the next one, which the lag bound of `2 x HONEST_TICK_MS` already covers. Card tremor is suppressed under `reduced_motion` (IW-12, D96).
*Why.* HR-8: the backbone of the lie (never-rest, continuous tick) survives; tremor is the one signature that needs a static substitute.
*Edge notes.* Accessibility Settings (Alpha) depends on this GDD (IDX:209): its reduced-motion toggle reads the Filter's published scalars (`reduced_motion_glide_pm`, `breath_depth_pm`, `lie_tick_min_ms`) and this rule's substitute *(provisional — Accessibility Settings undesigned)*. The screen-reader text for a card (Alpha) is built from `Perceived.displayed_value` only, never `true_value`; the pinned engine's accessibility live-region maturity is `[UNV]`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Tell survival for a deaf, colour-blind player.** With the sound removed and the hue removed, a lie keeps the never-rest motion mode on the needle, the continuous tick on the card numeral and the gleam as a **luminance** fringe; the claim of at least two tells therefore holds only if the fringe `#B07BE6` keeps at least `GLEAM_LUM_CONTRAST_MIN` 3:1 (the WCAG non-text ratio, `[PLACEHOLDER]`) against the glass it rides in greyscale, measured at S-A1 and tested by AC-IW-120. **Tell-survival rule** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: for every combination of reduced motion (scale 0.6), captions on or off, greyscale or a colour-vision filter, and sound off, at least two of the three tells survive on every lying instrument: the never-resting needle (the wander is never scaled, PF-39), the luminance gleam (above `GLEAM_LUM_CONTRAST_MIN`) and the sound (not counted when sound is off or unheard). The numeral tick is a mode of the motion tell and is not counted as a separate tell. Where reduced motion lengthens a digit hold, D.12 accepts it explicitly (`T_VIS_MAX_RM_S`). Tested by AC-IW-125; the caption tier is recorded in `design/accessibility-requirements.md` once that file exists (OQ-IW-42). Captions for lie sounds (Alpha) are a gate dependency of the accessibility tier and not an Instruments feature. `design/accessibility-requirements.md` does not exist; `/ux-design accessibility` sets the tier (OQ-IW-42).

**IW-28. What Instruments never do (scope guard).**
*Rule.* No schematic, overlay, minimap, ship-status, loop-summary or "lit schematic console" screen, ever; **rejected: lit schematic console** (answers CT:63, CT-1 at CT:223, CT:537 and CT:685 C4; LSG:51 anti-pillar 3). No numerals on a world gauge; no text on an in-world gauge; no closed ring or progress ring (curves are reserved for dial arcs and needles, ART 3.4); no condition word, bar, rating or loot value; no weld-preview number or seam readout (`surge[]` is never printed, LSG:170); no sanity meter, lie counter, "spotted" statistic, stage name or Attention, threat-level, proximity or direction indicator on any instrument (nothing Attention publishes feeds a needle); no instrument shows a horror, a threat level, a proximity or a direction (Pillar 3; HE Interactions downstream); a needle moves only when the ship's loop moves, the tell never moves one, and no instrument displays or sonifies the Attention level (AA UI Requirements; AT-17); no reading of a crewmate or a horror; no Alarm Red word; no violet or flesh pixel in an authored sheet; no pod dash; no new sim state, hook, mutator or block. A lint over the scene asserts that no node outside an instrument or the card renders Graph state at a distance (advisor ruling R16; PF-38; HE:377).
*Why.* Anti-pillars (no combat, no PvP, no authored story, no win state) and P1.

**IW-29. The MVP manifest is a content floor plus an indicative list.**
*Rule.* Floors, each a lint over the instrument data and Derelict Authoring's placements: (i) **L-2I per seat and per direction** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; BLOCKING 1, restated in the words of Sanity SN-15 L-2I)*: a lie on an instrument is eligible for a seat only if the loop keeps at least `SECOND_INSTRUMENT_MIN` (Sanity, value 1) honest second reading drawn from the L-2I list: **a fixed gauge on the same loop in another compartment** (a DIAL or LINEAR instrument reading another compartment's or node's quantity on that loop; a GLYPH is a machine-state mark, not a gauge, and is not credited as a partner, this GDD's reading of "gauge", OQ-IW-43), **or the scanner** (`tool_scanner`, honest in the MVP, SN-19), credited only if the seat carries it and can equip it, which means **not carrying a module** (Suit SE-11: `equip_tool` answers `BUSY` while carrying; Sanity makes no single-instrument-loop lie for a carrying seat), **or, for an ALARM-direction lie only**, `suit.o2` (the `p_o2` loop) or `suit.warmth` (the COOLANT loop's thermal quantity `T` only, Sanity QA-3), each valid only for the seat's current compartment. **For a COMFORT-direction lie the suit is never a credit** (at its activation the seat's compartment band must be OK, L-G, so the suit reading would agree with the lie). `suit.integrity`, `suit.reserve` and `suit.charge` never count. Because the suit reads the seat's own room, a REMOTE row (`hops` >= 1) gets **no suit credit in either direction**. An instrument whose only credit is the scanner is **scanner-dependent, seat-conditional**: it draws no lie for a seat without a usable scanner, so every per-site count is a **range**, not a number (IW-29 counts below); (ii) at least 3 lie-eligible surfaces per site (OQ-SN-11, advisory; AC-SN-134), **counted for a seat that can use the scanner** and reported also for a seat that cannot (carrying a module), so the count is a range `[no usable scanner, usable scanner]` and the gate is on the upper end while the lower end is reported as a finding *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; BLOCKING 1; which seat Sanity's AC-SN-134 counts for is OQ-IW-43)*; (iii) every instrument has a consequence-channel witness within `WITNESS_HOPS_MAX` 2 (Sanity-owned, L-W; linted by DA-34 against the LS-22 table) that shares no node with it (FT:314, AC-138); (iv) no decorative gauge (SN:262): every record's computed `beyond_witness` set (IW-8) is non-empty (GLYPH and FRAME carry STATE); a local dial on a quantity whose witness is continuous and which is not inertial (a `load` dial in the room of its bus) has an empty set and fails; (v) **no instrument with `hops(mount_room, subject_room)` >= 1 (REMOTE) carries the alarm mark** (stage-12 CD-GDD-ALIGN concern 1; IW-19): the lint `ALARM_MARK_ON_REMOTE` is `hops >= 1 AND mark_flag`, and it also fails the build when a mark is bound to any subject other than the machine or console panel, tank prop or compartment the housing physically belongs to; (vi) **each derelict has at least one lethal-capable REMOTE instrument with a verified hop count** (lie-eligible, 1 <= `hops` <= `WITNESS_HOPS_MAX`, its subject room carrying a `lethal_fault_modes` fault or a lethal band) as the Beat C fixture (lint `NO_BEAT_C_FIXTURE`, advisory until Sanity rules on cold-band lethality, OQ-IW-35); the proposed fixtures are `inst_b_dock_p_o2` (B) and `inst_a_machine_T` (A) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; BLOCKING 1)* **Both fixtures are REMOTE and COMFORT-direction, so the suit never credits them and each is scanner-dependent as listed**: the fixture is valid only for a seat with a usable scanner (the lint takes an explicit `seat_scanner_usable` input and passes only when the fixture is comfort-eligible for the seat with it set true; the carrying-seat result is reported, never gated). A fixed same-loop partner would make a fixture seat-independent; that is a P0 ask to Derelict Authoring (below). Indicative manifest on the LSG MVP slice (home 5 / derelict A 4 / derelict B 5 compartments), 18 instruments; ids are illustrative, Derelict Authoring places and names (`scr_a`, `res_main` and `b_a1` are real DA ids; room keys are DA's); the battery readings are DIAL_S `stock` dials, not pip ladders (IW-6). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The `mount_room` / `subject_room` columns are this GDD's proposal; the four one-hop REMOTE rows (`inst_engine_bus_load`, `inst_a_machine_T`, `inst_b_breach_P`, `inst_b_dock_p_o2`) were checked against the DA room trees *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (home: `spine_corridor` joins `cryo_bay`, `cargo_bay`, `engine_room` and `airlock`; A: `a_dock - a_spine - {a_life, a_machine}`; B: `b_dock - b_spine - {b_reactor, b_pump - b_breach}`), and Derelict Authoring owns the final placement:

| Site | Instrument (illustrative id) | Kind | Quantity / ref | `mount_room` -> `subject_room` (`hops`) | Derived `beyond_witness` | IW-19 row | COMFORT-direction credit (L-2I) | ALARM-direction credit (L-2I) | Authored subject state and notes |
