*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; BLOCKING 1: rewritten against Sanity SN-15 L-2I)* The Filter's `quantity` term (PF-9) and the Graph's published `true_value` kinds (LS-19 additions, 2026-10-27) retired the one-compartment-instrument-per-room fallback, so `inst_cryo_bay_T` is eligible; `inst_engine_bus_load` additionally waits on the Graph's `load_pm` kind (IW-9). **Counts are ranges `[no usable scanner, usable scanner]`, never one number.** *Usable scanner* means the seat carries `tool_scanner` (it is in the start kit, SE-15) and is not carrying a module (SE-11); the scanner credit is the only credit most rows have. With a usable scanner every listed row has a credit: **home 9, A 4, B 5** (18; home 8 if the scanner cannot read `load`, OQ-IW-43). Without one, only rows with a **fixed same-loop gauge in another compartment** stay eligible: **home 3** (`inst_cryo_bay_p_o2`, `inst_engine_room_p_o2`, `inst_scr_main_glyph`), **A 0**, **B 1** (`inst_scr_b_glyph`, credited by the `b_dock` gauge). The three suit-alarm credits (`inst_cryo_bay_T`, `inst_a_life_p_o2`, `inst_b_reactor_T`) are alarm-direction only, hold only while the seat stands in the subject compartment and only while the subject is healthy, so they are listed in the table and **not counted** in the lower end. The ranges are therefore **home [3, 9], A [0, 4], B [1, 5]**, counted by credit (structural eligibility). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The direction a lie can take also depends on the subject's live state (L-D, L-G): `inst_cryo_bay_T` and `inst_b_reactor_T` are hops-0 dials authored in a not-OK band, where Sanity's COOLANT guard (`band_t` of the seat's compartment) invalidates a comfort lie for the only seat that can read them and an alarm lie needs a healthy subject, so they count only once their room is back in OK; the upper ends are for that recovered state (at the authored states, home 8 and B 4). The `suit.warmth` credit on these two rows and the COOLANT guard rest on one assumption, that a compartment `T` dial belongs to the COOLANT loop in `lie_targets.json` (OQ-IW-43 (d)); both hold or neither does. The earlier counts (5, 3 and 4 for home, A and B, with A at the floor) rested on a suit credit for comfort-direction lies that SN-15 L-2I does not grant, and are retired; **derelict A is no longer AT-FLOOR at the upper end (4) and is the weakest site at the lower end (0: every A lie needs the scanner)**. A seat that is hauling salvage then receives no lie on A at all, which is Sanity's accepted consequence of SE-11 (EC-SN-42, EC-SN-62), not a defect here; but "scan everything" must not become dominant for the seat that does carry the scanner, so the lower end is reported and the asks below make it seat-independent. **Which lies the first scanner-less seat sees.** The first lie of a world is ALARM-direction by construction (SN-16), and the alarm credits are the widest (fixed gauge, scanner, or the suit in the seat's own compartment), so the Beat B anchor (`inst_engine_room_p_o2`, fixed partner) fires for every seat; the COMFORT lies (Beat C) need a fixed partner or a usable scanner. **L-2I status of this manifest (floor (i)): satisfied by the scanner for a usable-scanner seat; satisfied without it only where a fixed partner is listed.** Fixed partners exist for the `p_o2` gauges (home: `cryo_bay` plus `engine_room`) and for the scrubber glyphs of home and B (credited by a `p_o2` gauge in another compartment); **not** for A's glyph or A's `p_o2` (the only A `p_o2` gauge is in `a_life`, the glyph's own compartment), for `T` (no second `T` gauge on any site; `suit.warmth` is an alarm-direction credit only), for `P` (no second `P` gauge; the suit credits `T` only), for `inst_engine_bus_load` (no second bus-load reading), the two coolant levels, the home fuel level or the batteries. **P0 asks to Derelict Authoring (replacing the earlier battery-partner ask, which would still leave A scanner-only):** (1) in derelict A, a second `p_o2` dial reading another compartment, for example `inst_a_spine_p_o2` mounted in and reading `a_spine`, which makes `inst_a_life_p_o2`, the new dial and `inst_scr_a_glyph` fixed-credited (A lower end 0 to 3); (2) in derelict B, a second `p_o2` dial reading another compartment, for example `inst_b_spine_p_o2` in `b_spine`, which makes the Beat C fixture `inst_b_dock_p_o2` seat-independent (B lower end 1 to 3). A `T`-loop partner for fixture A is not asked: fixture A stays scanner-dependent (OQ-IW-35). The two asked dials are owed rows and are not added to the 18 above. Counts are indicative; no pod dash (K-10). **No acceptance criterion may assert that the manifest as listed passes floor (i)** (advisor ruling R17); AC-IW-108 and AC-IW-109 run against a test-owned fixture manifest with an explicit `seat_scanner_usable` input.
*Why.* Too few eligible surfaces make "scan everything" dominant (SN D.12); a decorative gauge makes a lie a non-event.
*Edge notes.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The one-compartment-instrument-per-room fallback is retired: the Filter's `quantity` term in `GRAPH_COMPARTMENT(cid, quantity)` (PF-9) and the Graph's published `true_value` kinds (LS-19 additions) make `inst_cryo_bay_T` eligible beside `inst_cryo_bay_p_o2`. Placement is Derelict Authoring's; the record format and the lints are this GDD's. The glyph rows carry STATE, not REMOTE: a glyph is an 8x8 mark on the module housing, so it is always local to the machine it reports (IW-6, IW-17).

**IW-30. Binding validity and the DEAD instrument.**
*Rule.* If the bound node is DESTROYED, SALVAGED or ABSENT, or the ref is unresolvable, the instrument shows its **DEAD** frame: a glass hairline crack (1 px Hull Grey diagonal) and the needle snapped to a 4-px stub at a fixed angle (an honest static frame on the sheet); no prompt, no card, no gleam, no tremor, no pip. The DEAD frame is truth-graded (untagged) and cannot lie. A compartment-bound gauge is never DEAD while the compartment exists. A missing Filter datum falls back to the honest path (PF-44). *(orchestrator ruling: the art-director's snapped stub over the ux-designer's "needle at the low stop"; a needle at zero would assert "reads empty" instead of "has no reading".)*
*Why.* "Dead gauges on a module" are falsifier-map class C (DA); a gauge that vanishes would read as a bug, one that keeps reading would read a destroyed thing.
*Edge notes.* The stub is not a lie: no tag, no `lie_surface_id`.

**IW-31. Placement record (requested) and subject registration.**
*Rule.* Layout and Derelict Authoring are asked for an instrument placement record (registry: `instrument_placement_record`) authored as a `Kit placement` (category `mach` or `prop`) carrying `instrument_id` (a key into `assets/data/instruments/`), `node_ref` or `room_key` plus `quantity`, `mount` WALL_FACE | MACHINE_PANEL, `host_face`, and since the design review `mount_room`, `subject_room`, `host_node` and an optional `witness_anchor_px` (the screen anchor of the drawn witness, a card keep-out, D.6); Layout's `subject_anchors(section_id)` exposes `anchor_px` so Interaction registers an `INSTRUMENT` subject. The node-to-subject binding that SN-16's lit-entry rule names as its one assumption is authored data here; the sim never queries Instruments at runtime (KRN H10). Derelict Authoring lints `INSTRUMENT_NO_BINDING`, `PANEL_GAUGE_VERB_CONFLICT` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (an INSTRUMENT on a MACHINE_PANEL that also carries an interactable verb target, an ERROR while OQ-IW-39 is open) and the at-least-3-eligible-surfaces floor. *(provisional — routed to Layout and Derelict Authoring)* (advisor ruling R23)
*Why.* LY-4's schema has Hardpoint, Anchor, Fixture anchor, Kit placement and Loose slot but no instrument with a Graph binding; without a record the binding lives nowhere.

**IW-32. Sound.**
*Rule.* Instruments request no sound directly. A lying instrument's sound is the Filter's request (`FA_LIE_SIG_GAUGE` for needle, card (needle and numeral) and glyph; `FA_LIE_SIG_SPRITE` for housing and machine; PF-20; Audio owns assets and mix). An honest instrument's tick, settle and tremor are motion only. Card open and close sounds are UI-bus, Audio-owned, and triggered by a presentation-local `card_opened` / `card_closed` signal Instruments publish (the outbound half of registry `inspect_card_context_signal`; event names `UI_INSTRUMENT_CARD_OPEN` / `_CLOSE` requested, Audio owns the naming) *(provisional — Adaptive Audio reply owed)*. Direction-of-change agreement (AAV-17): the machine frame, the sight-glass and the lamp brightness follow the same Graph value the audio reads; a sagging hum beside a full-speed rotor frame is a contradiction the Presenter must not create unless that frame is the lie.
*Why.* The Filter owns lie sounds and Audio owns assets and mix (PF-20); an honest instrument that made its own sound would add a sound channel with no witness value.

**IW-33. Pause, restore and hard start.**
*Rule.* Pause freezes `present_time` and with it every instrument motion (the world freezes undimmed, ART 7.4); an open card is closed by the pause itself (IW-24, a context change) and is not restored on resume *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2)*. On `hard_start` (restore, force_stage) the settle triple, tremor envelope, numeral throttle and cull history are reset: the needle shows `displayed_pm` from the first frame with no settle and no glide ramp, any open card closes, and tremor restarts from its onset ramp if the predicate still holds. A camera cut, zoom step or window resize re-projects every placement and re-chooses the card side by a cut; no settle fires. Instruments persist and replicate nothing (PIPE PR-9; PF-33).
*Why.* ART 7.4: the world simply stops, undimmed; a restore must reproduce what the player was shown, so no presentation state may survive it.

**IW-34. Fallbacks and cut order.**
*Rule.* If the card is cut: `inspect` stays bound and dormant; stage-1 reach loses the card surface (IW-21). If the per-instance gleam transport fails (Filter spike S-1): housing and glyph gleams are cut first, never the needle or the card numeral well. If the terminal-fitting mount is rejected: dials only on MACHINE_PANEL mounts. If the art-director rejects the alarm mark: no mark (sweep and sound only). If the user declines the D96 default (card tremor, suppressed under `reduced_motion`): the held notch on the card in all modes *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The Filter's no-cache wording refresh landed on 2026-10-27 (OQ-IW-1 closed); `prev_honest_pm` and the rest, window and dwell scalars are this GDD's presentation-local scalars (IW-11, C14). Stage-3 machine-frame sets and the stage-2 lamp-housing pair are the cuttable tier (a cut removes SPRITE_SWAP eligibility through `has_both_frames`; routed to Sanity to refresh SN-16's stage-3 `KIND_W` weights if taken); the MVP floor is dial + level sight-glass + card + alarm mark + honest WORN.
*Why.* A solo developer's art load is the binding constraint (about 20 sheets at P0); every fallback keeps the P1 and P4 contract and removes only presentation.

**IW-35. Flagged, not edited.**
*Rule.* Missing index edges (Pipeline PIPE:302, Emitter State EM:339, Failure & Trail soft pair, Sanity lie targets) and the unowned module-presenter row are recorded in Dependencies (K-10, K-11, advisor ruling R26); the stale ART 7.0 / 2.5 pod-dash lines are the art-director's (WM:1187). This GDD edits neither the index, the art bible nor another GDD. The field-check / scanner reading is HUD & Suit Layer's to draw (a hand-tool reading on the hand rail and an honest anchor at MVP, SN-19); Instruments supply only the shared honest-digit mechanism of IW-14 as a reusable component (advisor ruling R15; K-9; refresh owed on BS:1206).
*Why.* The index's explicit edges and the neighbours' own rows must not be silently rewritten by a downstream author (producer owns the index; art-director owns the bible).

**IW-36. Legibility contract: every figure at every rung.**
*Rule.* Every legibility figure in this GDD is stated at **every** zoom rung `k` in [1, `k_auto` + 2] (720p and Steam Deck 1280x800 `k_auto` 2: 1..4; 1080p 3: 1..5; 1440p 4: 1..6; 2160p 6: 1..8) and at HUD scale `H` from `H_default` = max(`k_auto`, 3) (Pipeline registry formula `hud_multiplier`, `H_default = max(k, 3)`; `k` there is the default rung `k_auto`, because the HUD does not change with zoom, PIPE PR-12) to 2 x `H_default` (`HUD_MIN_SCALE` 3), including the Steam Deck case (`H` 3 over `k` 2), the largest field (zoom 1x at 2160p) and the maximum `H` on small displays; the table is D.7. By construction the needle stroke (2 art px) is at least 2 screen px at every rung; the gleam and tremor art-px size is raised only at `k` 1 (D.5, D.9); the world needle's absolute floor is `NEEDLE_SCREEN_MIN_PX` (`[PLACEHOLDER]` 24, accessibility tier): DIAL_S at `k` 1 (18 screen px) is below it and is accepted because `k` 1 is a deliberate zoom-out and the card is the precision path; at zoom 1x on 2160p the world dial is a 56-px arc in a 3840-px frame, readable only by pattern and needle, which is the design's weakest point and is carried as a risk, not hidden. Tremor's marginal visibility at `k` 1 on the largest field is acceptable only because instrument tremor is never the only tell: every IW-12 stress source also has a sound or world-witness tell owned by Adaptive Audio or Failure & Trail, so zooming out never removes the warning (IW-12; AC-IW-106).
*Why.* Reflexion-log rule: an invariant written against the default must be re-checked whenever a later decision adds reachable states (zoom steps, resolutions).

**IW-37. Strings and localisation.**
*Rule.* Every string on an instrument or card is a `tr()` StringName key: the label (`name_key`), the unit (`unit_key`) and nothing else; a world gauge carries no text. Labels are never truncated (truncation would make two instruments ambiguous): a label wider than the plate's text width (plate width minus 8 art px) wraps to a second row and the plate grows by 7 art px, and the placement cap is recomputed with the taller plate (D.6). The bitmap instrument face covers the shipped Latin set; scripts it lacks (CJK, Cyrillic, Arabic right-to-left) need a fallback face, and the vector reading face is barred on instruments by ART 7.1 (OQ-IW-14). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **The card degrades element by element and is never removed for a locale:** the numeral cells (Latin digits with the slashed zero) and the dial or glass render in every locale, and a `name_key` or `unit_key` containing a glyph the bitmap face lacks falls back, for that one element, to the default-locale (Latin) string and then to the instrument's part-number stencil (`instrument_id`), so the only precision path survives everywhere. The decimal mark follows the locale key and is part of the plate variant. No string says a state, a verdict or a lie (IA-39; PF-38).
*Why.* A label that can be mistranslated into a verdict is a P4 oracle; a truncated label makes two instruments ambiguous and breaks the second-opinion check.

**IW-38. Diagnostics: a dev-only probe and nothing else.**
*Rule.* In development builds only, each instrument exposes a read-only `drawn_value(ref)` (the value it actually drew this frame, answering the Debug Overlay's render-probe ask DO-22, whose `DEFECT_BYPASS` class detects a corruptible node that skipped the Filter) and an `instrument_diag(ref)` record `{instrument_id, ref_key, motion_mode as drawn, settle_active, tremor_active, card_open, state}`. Both are tagged dev-only and stripped from release builds; neither is a value source for any gameplay or presentation node; neither exposes `true_value` (the Overlay calls that itself, DIAGNOSTIC). Instruments publish a stable integer `ref_key` per instrument (the Filter's, PF-9) and keep four counters for the Overlay and the lints: `diag.instr_pm_clamped` (a `displayed_pm` outside 0..1000 was clamped), `diag.inspect_unresolved` (IW-24), `diag.instr_quantity_collision` and `diag.gleam_over_cap` (IW-16). The Overlay treats an absent probe as `bypass_check: ABSENT`, never clean (DO-22). In development builds only, Instruments registers one diagnostics channel at composition-root time under the Debug Overlay's channel contract (DO-12, `diag_panel_contract`), carrying the counters `diag.instr_pm_clamped`, `diag.inspect_unresolved`, `diag.instr_quantity_collision` and `diag.gleam_over_cap` and the `instrument_diag(ref)` rows. The channel is a dev-only panel registration, not a kernel block, hook, mutator or dirty key, so IW-2's zero-registration rule is unchanged; it is stripped from release builds.
*Why.* The Overlay's classifier can otherwise only compare `perceived` with `true_value`; a node that bypasses the Filter is invisible to it.
*Edge notes.* An out-of-range `displayed_pm` is clamped to the end stop and counted; it is never an error shown to the player.

### States and Transitions

An instrument has three independent axes: a **visibility axis** (does the world's light reach it, is its card open, does its binding still exist), a **motion axis** driven by the Filter's `motion_mode`, and an **ALARM flag**. All state is presentation-local, non-persistent and non-replicated; none of it is a sim block, and every state is reset by `hard_start` (IW-33). The sim never reads any of it.

#### S1. Visibility axis

| State | Meaning | Visual | Prompt / card | Entry | Exit | Who may cause it |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DARK` | exists; `lit(ref)` false | the renderer shows a dark or dim face (HullSurface lighting, not an Instruments hide); the gleam rides the host and is dark too; the pip, if the loop runs, is visible | prompt hidden; card refused | scene load with no light on the face; `lit` falls below `LIT_OFF_PM` 120 | `lit` rises to `LIT_ON_PM` 150 | Emitter State (the `lit` bit, owned by the technical-artist); Lighting (what the beam, fixtures, lantern pools and Chemlight reach); the player (aim, movement, throwing a glowstick) |
| `LIT` | `lit(ref)` true | readable face; honest or perceived reading; gleam if lying | prompt may show (focus plus debounce); card openable | `lit` rises; a card closes while the instrument is still lit | `lit` falls; binding lost; card opens | Emitter State; Interaction (focus, prompt); the player |
| `CARD_OPEN` | `LIT` and the Adapter context is `inspect_card` for this ref | the wall gauge unchanged, plus the card on layer 2 | card open | `inspect` pressed on a lit target (the primary focus or the secondary INSTRUMENT slot, IW-21); no debounce gates the press *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | any IW-24 close condition | the Adapter (`{inspect_card, ref}` signal) for entry and for `inspect` / `cancel` / B exits; Emitter State (`lit` false); Graph (binding lost); Save/Load via kernel restore; Pipeline (camera cut re-places, never closes) |
| `DEAD` | bound node DESTROYED, SALVAGED or ABSENT, or the ref is unresolvable | DEAD frame: hairline crack, snapped needle stub | prompt hidden; card refused | binding lost | binding restored (section re-load) | Graph (`node(id).state`, section load and unload); Layout (placement) |

#### S2. Motion axis (inside `DARK`, `LIT`, `CARD_OPEN`; independent of visibility)

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* `REST`, `SETTLING` and `FILTER_DRIVEN` are mutually exclusive states. **Tremor is an orthogonal flag, as ALARM is in S3**: it can be set in `REST` or `SETTLING` (never in `FILTER_DRIVEN`) and it adds its offset to whatever base the state draws (E-IW-10, AC-IW-65), so a settle and a tremor are active together by construction.

| State | Meaning | Entry | Exit | Who may cause it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `REST` | **at rest in time (IW-11 (b))**: no honest sample for `INSTR_REST_STEPS` Graph steps and no settle active; the needle sits on one pixel *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | initial; `INSTR_REST_STEPS` steps after the last honest sample once any ramp or settle has ended; on `hard_start`; on return to HONEST (the tremor flag does not affect `REST`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | the first honest sample (a ramp, or a settle if it passes the jump test); non-HONEST mode | (resting state) |
| `SETTLING` | closed-form settle in progress (IW-11) | a jump from rest while HONEST | `INSTR_SETTLE_MS` elapsed (to `REST`); re-based by another change (stays `SETTLING` with a fresh triple); non-HONEST mode (cleared at once) | the Graph (a real discrete change of the honest displayed value); the Filter (`motion_mode`) |
| `TREMOR` flag (orthogonal to `REST` and `SETTLING`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | honest stress tremor (IW-12) | predicate true while HONEST (onset ramp `TREMOR_ONSET_MS`) | predicate false (decay `TREMOR_DECAY_MS`, then the flag clears and the base state is unchanged); non-HONEST mode (cleared at once) | the Graph (the stress predicate from `read_view`: hazard-mode descriptors via `failures(filter)` (including an OVERLOAD descriptor on a cable of the bound loop component), and `stress_active` if granted); never Attention, sanity or a timer |
| `FILTER_DRIVEN` | `motion_mode` is GLIDE, TICK, STALE or RETURN; Instruments draw the received `displayed_pm` verbatim on the needle or meniscus (the Filter's trajectory, wander included, which never rests) and, on the card, the received digits (continuous tick, or the held digits of STALE) | the Filter's `motion_mode` leaves HONEST (the settle triple, tremor and notch are cleared that frame) | `motion_mode` returns to HONEST (CLEAR; discrepancy under `LEAK_EPS_DEG`, PF-12): to `REST`, no event, no flash | the Perception Filter only; Instruments never infer it |

Reduced motion changes only how `SETTLING` and the `TREMOR` flag are drawn (a step and a held notch pair, IW-27); the states and their causes are the same.

#### S3. ALARM flag

`ALARM` is set while an `alarm_eligible` `FailureDescriptor` exists on the bound subject (read through `failures(filter)`, refreshed per Graph step) and cleared with it. It is orthogonal to every state above and to `motion_mode`: a lying needle never sets, moves or clears it. Only the Graph causes it (LS-13 fairness gate). It is drawn only on housings that carry the mark (IW-19) and never on the card.

#### S4. Transitions

| From | Event / guard | To | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DARK` | `lit(ref)` true | `LIT` | no dwell (EM-22) |
| `LIT` | `lit(ref)` false | `DARK` | an open card closes in the same render frame |
| `LIT` | `inspect` on a lit target (primary focus or secondary slot); no debounce gates the press *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | `CARD_OPEN` | the Adapter publishes `{inspect_card, ref}`; no command is submitted |
| `CARD_OPEN` | `inspect` again, or `cancel` (X) / B | `LIT` | |
| `CARD_OPEN` | `lit(ref)` false, reach lost, section transit, restore or `hard_start` | `DARK` / `LIT` per cause | immediate close; re-open needs a new press |
| `CARD_OPEN` | the Adapter context becomes `chart_table` (pause) or `chat`, or `loco_state` becomes STAGGERED, DOWNED, DEAD or IN_POD *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | `LIT` or `DARK` per `lit` | closes in the same render frame; not re-opened when the context returns to `gameplay` (E-IW-60); in co-op the overlay does not pause the sim, so the card never stays drawn under a modal |
| `CARD_OPEN` | movement intent past the dead-zone *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | `LIT` or `DARK` per `lit` | closes and the avatar moves in the same frame (IW-25, E-IW-29) |
| `CARD_OPEN` | binding lost | `DEAD` | card closes that frame |
| any | binding lost (node DESTROYED, SALVAGED or ABSENT; ref unresolvable) | `DEAD` | |
| `DEAD` | binding restored | `DARK` or `LIT` per `lit` | section re-load |
| `REST` | first honest sample whose accumulated change passes the jump test inside the window (IW-11 (b)) on a `settle_eligible` quantity *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | `SETTLING` | triple set *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `SETTLING` | any further change of the honest displayed value | `SETTLING` | re-based: `from` = value now drawn |
| any HONEST state (`REST` or `SETTLING`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | stress predicate true while HONEST | `TREMOR` flag set; the base state is unchanged | onset ramp; the drawn position is the base plus the tremor offset, clamped (E-IW-10) |
| `TREMOR` flag set | predicate false | flag cleared after the decay; base state unchanged | decay, then the needle rests on a pixel |
| any HONEST state | `motion_mode` becomes GLIDE, TICK, STALE or RETURN | `FILTER_DRIVEN` | triple, tremor and notch cleared that frame |
| `FILTER_DRIVEN` | `motion_mode` HONEST | `REST` | no settle (E-IW-36: a return is not a jump) |
| any | `hard_start`, cull re-entry, camera cut | `REST` | all presentation scalars reset; the needle shows `displayed_pm` at once |
| any | pause (kernel PAUSED) | unchanged, frozen | `present_time` stops; an open card was already closed by the `chart_table` row above and stays closed on resume; the plate does not animate back in *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2)* |

**Invariants.** (I-1) `CARD_OPEN` implies `LIT` at the moment of the frame in which it is drawn. (I-2) `SETTLING` and the `TREMOR` flag imply `motion_mode` HONEST in that frame *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. (I-3) `DEAD` implies no prompt, no card, no gleam, no tremor, no pip. (I-4) At most one card is open per client, and it is for the ref of the target of the press, the primary focus when it is an INSTRUMENT and otherwise the secondary INSTRUMENT slot (IW-21), at the time of the press *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

### Interactions with Other Systems

"In" is what Instruments take; "Out" is what Instruments give. **Instruments give nothing to the simulation**: no command, no mutator, no hook, no block, no dirty key, no persisted field. Status vocabulary: **designed** (a GDD on disk, header Status Designed); **designed in this run** (written in this orchestrated run, may be revised); **undesigned-provisional** (no GDD on disk; this GDD states a request and re-checks it when the neighbour lands). Glob of `design/gdd/` at authoring: 27 files including this one (re-counted 2026-10-27: 28 `.md` files, the 26 MVP GDDs plus `game-concept.md` and `systems-index.md`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; HUD & Suit Layer and Debug Overlay (True Values) are Status Designed (**designed in this run**); Accessibility Settings does not exist.

#### I-A. Upstream systems (Instruments depend on them)

| Neighbour (status) | In (to Instruments) | Out (from Instruments) | Interface owner | Named interface |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Command & Simulation Layer** (designed, locked) | dirty-key notifications as dirty marks only; `tick_alpha`; the `PRESENTATION` class; the restore event | nothing: no block, hook, mutator, command or persisted state | kernel | `PRESENTATION` subscriber class (KRN:106-110; CR-10); H10 (systems stay scene-tree free; Instruments' nodes are scene-tree) |
| **Life-Support Graph** (designed, locked) | `read_view` non-value reads: `compartment(cid)` (the bound compartment's existence and the `band_*` fields are **not** read for display), `node(id)` / `edge(id)` (`state`, `sev`, `served_pm`, `stock`, `forced_mode`), `loop_component(loop, any_node)` (membership and anchor node only; the `load` reading is the Graph's requested `load_pm` kind, IW-9), `stress_active(section_id)` (LS-19 additions, IW-12 (iv)), `failures(filter)` (the hazard-mode descriptors of the stress predicate, `SEAL_STRAIN` / `LEAK` / `BREACH`, `alarm_eligible`), `nodes_in(cid)`, `neighbors(cid)`, `section_of(id)`; the LS-7 band **names** at build time; the closed consequence-channel list | nothing; never `true_value`, `summary()`, `power_use()`, `can_join()`, `block_hash()` | Graph | `read_view` (LS-19); registry formula `atmosphere_derive` (this GDD is in its `referenced_by`); LS-19 additions (2026-10-27) now provide `stress_active(section_id)`, the `true_value` kinds and `stock_pm`, and the Graph has refreshed LSG:998 and LS-20 (K-17 closed on the Graph side); requests: the `load_pm` kind with its quantum `LOAD_QUANTUM_PERMILLE` and the three bus cases (IW-9, OQ-IW-20) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| **Perception Filter** (designed in this run; PF-7, PF-9, PF-18, PF-45 provisional until this GDD lands; PF I-B row provisional) | `perceived(ref, viewer)` -> `Perceived` (`displayed_pm`, `displayed_value`, `frame_override`, `motion_mode`, `tick_phase_ms`, `leak_amplitude_pm`, `leak_modes`, `lie_id`, `lie_kind`); the leak phase; the lying-tick digits; `hard_start` (the Filter's term, PF-33, raised by a kernel restore or `force_stage`) | the PF-45 calibration record (IW-8); the `lie_surface_id` list; the needle, `_msk` and frame-pair assets; the **answer to OQ-PF-11**: the gleam on the card's glass is drawn here (IW-16) | Filter owns the lie; Instruments own the needle, digit mechanism, settle, tremor and card | `perceived` (PF-7), `Perceived` (PF-8), `PerceptionRef` (PF-9); **adopted (PF-9, OQ-PF-29, 2026-10-27 consistency pass):** `GRAPH_COMPARTMENT(cid, quantity)` and a LINEAR branch (pixel band, D.11); **requests:** a no-cache amendment (a presentation-local scalar derived from an honest displayed value and cleared on any non-HONEST frame, cull, cut and restore is not a cache, K-17); a lying needle on a ref with no world readout carries the never-rest wander whatever the numeral mode (OQ-IW-19); an optional per-step honest memo refresh for on-screen refs (OQ-IW-18); zoom-dependent gleam edge within the Filter's `[PLACEHOLDER]` `GLEAM_EDGE_PX` range 1-2 (D.9); the combined numeral count (K-16) |
| **2D Lighting & Occlusion** (designed in this run) | the lit-surface result and HullSurface for gauge faces; the emissive layer (mask bit 2) for the housing pip; the five-`ShaderMaterial` budget (LO-13); the default-off leak include block (PF-43) | instrument sprites with `_msk` slices; a default-zero leak parameter; the pip emission frames | Lighting owns materials; Instruments own gauge art and readings | LO-13, LO-14 (the emissive layer "casts no light and exposes nothing"), `PIP_EMIT_MAX` (Lighting spike) |

#### I-D. Neighbour rows quoted, and what each requires of this GDD

Each dependency GDD's own statement of the edge is quoted so that the two sides can be checked against each other. Where this GDD answers, accepts or contradicts a quoted row, the result is stated.

1. **Perception Filter, I-B row (PF:556).** "*In-World Instruments & Inspect View (Hard dependent) | `perceived(ref, viewer)` -> `Perceived` (PF-8) for needle, readout, glyph, housing, machine frame; the gleam amplitude and the leak-material parameters; the `motion_mode` | the PF-45 calibration record in the tag; a separate pivoted needle of at least 16 art px; a `_msk` glass and needle-edge slice; both honest state frames on one sheet page; honest settle and tremor **only** when `motion_mode` is HONEST; the card is the same ref as the wall gauge; the card drawn only when its instrument is lit | Instruments own the needle sprite, digit mechanism, settle and tremor, the card. The Filter owns the lie trajectory and its return.*" **Accepted** (IW-7, IW-8, IW-10, IW-11, IW-12, IW-13, IW-17, IW-26, IW-24). One amendment: PF's "readout" is the card numeral only, `displayed_value` of the needle's own ref and not a separate ref (IW-6, checkpoint ruling C2); the wording refresh is routed to the Filter and to Sanity. Also PF-45 ("Instruments also owe: a pivoted needle as a separate sprite or slice, a `_msk` slice naming the glass and needle-edge regions the gleam uses, both honest state frames on one sheet page, and the honest settle applied only when `motion_mode` is HONEST") is **answered** by IW-8; PF-18 (the card lies exactly when the gauge lies; violet only on the reading's glass) by IW-16 and IW-26; PF UI Requirements (never cache a `Perceived`, never apply settle or tremor to a non-HONEST reading, never address the Filter by `peer_id`) by IW-5 and IW-13.
2. **2D Lighting & Occlusion (LO:436).** "*`lit(ref)` from Emitter State for 'unreadable until lit' | the lit-surface result, HullSurface for gauge faces, the emissive layer for a running loop's faintly lit gauge ('a lit gauge is a lantern', ART 7.0) | Instruments: gauge art and readings. Lighting: the surface and the self-emission term | ... Instruments must repeat: a dead loop's gauge is dark until the beam finds it; the inspect card is drawn only for an instrument that is currently lit (gated by `lit(ref)`, never by the renderer).*" **Repeated** (IW-3, IW-24) with one refinement: the faint self-emission is a non-readable, corruptible housing pip, not a lantern (IW-4, K-6, advisor ruling R21), because LO-14 itself says the emissive layer "casts no light and exposes nothing". The "lantern" wording in this row and in ART 7.0 is stale.
3. **Interaction System (IA-38, IA:465, IA OQ 16).** IA:465: "*In-World Instruments & Inspect View | the `inspect` permission rule (a lit instrument only) | the card, rendering `true_value` through the Filter | Instruments*". IA-38: "*In-World Instruments owns the card and renders `true_value` through the Perception Filter ... If the card does not ship at MVP, `inspect` stays bound and dormant.*" **Accepted with two corrections:** Instruments never render `true_value` (they render `perceived`; only the Filter calls `true_value`), and **the card ships** (IA OQ 16 answered, IW-21).
4. **Player Controller & Input (PC:210 row 10, PC-16 at PC:147, PC:77, PC OQ 4).** PC:210: "*Adapter -> Instruments (`inspect`, `inspect_card` context) | HR-14 mirror card, lit-only; whether the inspect card ships at MVP is open ... | open*". PC-16: "*`inspect` enters the `inspect_card` context (an `INPUT_SURFACE` of In-World Instruments)*". **Answered:** the card ships (PC OQ 4); the card is a `PRESENTATION` surface and the Adapter is the `INPUT_SURFACE` (K-1), so PC-16's parenthesis is stale; PC:77's purpose column "Interaction's inspect verb" contradicts IA-38 (inspect is a context switch, not a verb); `set_aim` in the card context is requested (K-14).
5. **Emitter State (EM:339, EM:864, EM-22).** EM:339: "*HUD & Suit Layer / In-World Instruments | nothing | `lit(ref)` gates prompt drawing; the HUD anchors with `world_to_hud`; a lit instrument is 'faintly lit by its own emission' (art bible 7.0) | the HUD | presentation | provisional*". **Corrected:** `lit(ref)` gates the **prompt and the card**, and "faintly lit by its own emission" is the housing pip (a find-aid, K-6). EM-22 ("no dwell ... keeping `lit` true after the light went would be a window where the sim says lit and the world is dark, a P1 lie") is honoured by IW-24.
6. **Life-Support Graph (LSG:864, LSG:998, LSG:1003, LS-22).** LSG:864: "*Perception Filter / In-World Instruments | `true_value(kind,id)` and `read_view` — the Filter is the only non-diagnostic reader that may wrap a true value; it may corrupt the module-body channel (sprites, gauges, inspect cards, descriptors in mode `WORN`) but may not remove, alter or add anything on the consequence channel ... | nothing (PERCEPTION_OUT never feeds the Graph, CR-12) | PRESENTATION*". **Accepted.** LSG:998: "*gauges read `read_view` at render rate for smooth needles (dirty keys only wake discrete presentation); a lit inspect card shows one instrument's precise reading and nothing else; gauges are per node, so two instruments on different nodes are the player's 'second opinion'.*" **Partly contradicted (K-17):** gauges read values only through `perceived` (R18, PF-3); smooth needles come from IW-11; the card and "per node, second opinion" clauses are accepted (IW-22, IW-29). LSG:1003's UX flag (`design/ux/instruments.md` before epics) is **owed after this GDD**. LS-22 gives every gauge quantity one physical witness driven by the same Graph value (IW-29 floor iii).

FT:232 (WORN "draws nothing: the module-body channel; sprite wear, gauge, inspect card: the module presenter and In-World Instruments") **accepted** (IW-18).

Instruments | Not listed as a dependent in the index; it draws in both layers, so it is one.*" **Accepted** (IW-7, IW-23); the missing index edge is flagged (Dependencies). PR-8's rationale at PIPE:198 ("a gauge's needle lag" rejected) is the conflict carried as **K-2**.

The in-world inspect card (a corruptible module-body surface) may name the module and its state in words | On inspect | In-World Instruments (provisional)*". **Partly contradicted (K-3):** state in words is answered **no** (BS-6, IA-39, FT-4, SN L-A); the module's name appears only as the instrument label (IW-22). Refresh owed by Item DB.

BS:1206 lists Instruments for the field-check reading: **declined**, HUD & Suit Layer draws it (K-9).
11. **Derelict Authoring (DA:1571, DA-34, DA:1340).** DA:1571 assigns "gauges and instruments reading authored systems" to "Suit & Equipment, HUD (undesigned)": **contradicted** (K-4, IW-1). DA-34's witness lint and DA:1340's "module presenter (IDB `sprite_family`)" are **accepted** (IW-17, IW-29).
12. **Sanity (SN:1346-1347, SN:262, SN-16).** "*Lie-capable instruments ship the art authoring requirements (separate pivoted needle, `_msk` glass and needle-edge slice, both honest state frames)*" and "*The card is the same instrument as its wall gauge and lies exactly when the gauge lies, from stage 1; its frame is an anchor and violet appears only on the reading's glass (SNV-8)*": **accepted** (IW-8, IW-16, IW-26).
13. **Chart Table (CT:537, CT:685 C4, CT-1).** "*In-World Instruments & Inspect View | none | none | owns the `inspect_card` context and any lit, lie-capable schematic | Instruments | C4 terminology; this layer designs no prop*". **Corrected:** the Adapter owns the `inspect_card` context (IW-2) and there is **no** schematic (IW-28, advisor ruling R16).
13a. **HUD & Suit Layer (designed in this run; HS-57, its node table, its Instruments row).** HS-57: "*Inspect card: layer 2, frame and components HUD-owned; content, context and card-gauge parity (the card reads the same `perceived` ref as the wall gauge and lies exactly when it lies) are In-World Instruments'.*" Its Instruments row: "*Owns world gauges, the inspect card's content, context and card-gauge parity; the card is layer 2 and the HUD supplies the layer, `Theme`, shared components and the gleam-on-glass helper; owns `NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX`*". **Accepted**; the gleam is applied by this GDD's card glass through the HUD's helper (IW-16, IW-22). HS-14 (honest numerals on a shared hold grid `HONEST_TICK_MS`, amending "one step per real change") is **adopted** for the card numeral (IW-14, K-16), and so is HS-47's sentence "*The inspect card's honest numeral (In-World Instruments) shares the same grid boundary and adds no event*": the card requests no arbiter slot, a boundary with no displayed change is not an event, and the HUD's grid-skip rule bounds the card numeral's lag at `2 x HONEST_TICK_MS` (IW-14, D.3). HS-33 (the scanner reading slot is HUD's) **confirms** K-9. HS-29 and HS-14 state "tremor not used" for suit readings; that is the suit's rule, not an Instruments rule (IW-12).

This GDD edits none of them (advisor ruling R26); each is listed in Cross-References X-3.

## Formulas

Every formula below is presentation-side. None is hashed, persisted or replicated, none is read by another system's simulation, and none feeds the kernel's `state_hash` (KRN D.12); so floating-point arithmetic on `present_time` is permitted for motion (D.1, D.4, D.5), while every quantity read from the Graph or the Filter stays an integer (per-mille, dkPa, mC, permille) and every formatted string is integer-only (D.3). D.2 and D.12 run in the instrument build tool, not in the game. The Perception Filter's formulas (glide parameters, PF D.2; `lie_displayed_value`, PF D.3; wander, PF D.4; `lying_digit_tick_schedule`, PF D.9; fringe width, PF D.10; presentation hash, PF D.14) and the Graph's `atmosphere_derive` (LSG D.3) are **cited by registry name and never restated**; a number quoted from one of them carries its owner. D.1 to D.12 are registered as formulas, not only as constants, so that both bounds of each travel together (reflexion log: "when a formula has two bounds, register the formula"); registry entries are written from this final text, not from the specialist drafts. `INSTR_`-prefixed constants are this GDD's `[PLACEHOLDER]` values (Tuning Knobs); `[UNV]` marks an engine behaviour with no reference file (the engine reference has no `scripting.md` and no `core.md`). **Rounding convention (one rule for every formula):** `round` in D.1 (`tip`), D.5 (`o`) and D.11 (`y(pm)`) is half away from zero (the behaviour of GDScript `round`, so `round(0.5)` = 1 and `round(-0.5)` = -1); `floor` is the mathematical floor; `floor_div` is the explicit helper of D.3 (never native integer division on a negative numerator); per-mille, dkPa and mC values are integers, so a "16.7 pm" quantum is the integer step 16 or 17 depending on where the value falls on the span. Every acceptance vector in this document uses this convention.

### D.1 Needle angle, honest sample ramp and tip pixel (`needle_angle`)

The needle angle formula is defined as:

`a = clamp( ((T_c - t_sample) + tick_alpha) / GRAPH_STEP_TICKS, 0, 1 )`

`pm_honest = prev_honest_pm + (target_pm - prev_honest_pm) * a`

`pm_draw = pm_honest` (HONEST, no settle) `| settle_response(from_pm, to_pm, t_jump)` (HONEST, a jump, D.4) `| displayed_pm` (every other `motion_mode`, verbatim)

`theta(pm) = -arc_deg / 2 + pm * arc_deg / 1000` (degrees, clockwise from 12 o'clock)

`tip(pm) = round( pivot + needle_len_px * (sin theta, -cos theta) )` (whole art px, y down)

`px_per_pm = needle_len_px * (arc_deg * pi / 180) / 1000`; `tip_speed = needle_len_px * omega` (px/s)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T_c` | int ticks | >= 0 | kernel tick of this render frame (read view) | the tick the frame is drawn at |
| `t_sample` | int ticks | <= `T_c` | local | the tick at which the current honest sample was first drawn (the Filter's honest memo was invalidated by an LS-20 quantum crossing) |
| `tick_alpha` | float | [0, 1] | PIPE PR-8 rule 5 (kernel `tick_alpha`, clamped) | the one interpolation factor between kernel ticks |
| `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` | int | 6 (REG) | Graph | one Graph step is 100 ms |
| `displayed_pm` | int per mille | 0..1000 (out of range is clamped and counted, `diag.instr_pm_clamped`) | `Perceived` (PF-8) | the Filter's displayed reading |
| `target_pm` | int per mille | 0..1000 | local (IW-11 (b)) | the accepted honest sample: `displayed_pm` after the reversal dwell (equal to it unless a one-quantum reversal is still held), the needle target in HONEST *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `INSTR_REVERSAL_DWELL_STEPS` | int Graph steps | 4 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 3-8 | this GDD | how long a reversal of the last accepted change by at most one quantum must persist before the needle follows it |
| `prev_honest_pm` | float per mille | 0..1000 | local scalar of IW-11 | the value drawn when the current sample arrived (so a sample arriving mid-ramp continues from where the needle is); unset after a cull, camera cut or restore, in which case the first pull snaps |
| `arc_deg` | int deg | 240 (a build lint rejects any other value, IW-8) | calibration record | dial sweep |
| `needle_len_px` | int art px | 24 (DIAL_L), 18 (DIAL_S); >= `NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX` 16 | calibration record | pivot-to-tip length |
| `pivot` | int pair art px | the arc centre | sheet | needle pivot |
| `omega` | float rad/s | 0 to 0.035 | the Filter's glide rate in deg/s times pi/180 (PF D.2, cited) | tip speed of a gliding needle |

**Output Range:** `pm_draw` is within [0, 1000]. `theta` is within [-120, +120] degrees at `arc_deg` 240; the tip is inside the circle of radius `needle_len_px`. The ramp is monotone between `prev_honest_pm` and `displayed_pm`, never extrapolates, and ends at `displayed_pm` when `a` reaches 1, after which the needle rests. One tip pixel is 9.95 pm on DIAL_L (`px_per_pm` 0.1005) and 13.3 pm on DIAL_S (0.0754). **The honest resolution is the LS-20 quantum** (checkpoint ruling C14), in needle terms:

| Quantity | LS-20 quantum | Span | Quantum in pm | On DIAL_L | On DIAL_S | Vs `INSTR_SETTLE_JUMP_PM` 40 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `p_o2` | 5 dkPa | 300 dkPa | 16.7 | 1.7 px | about 1.25 px | below: never fires a settle |
| `P` | 10 dkPa | 1100 dkPa | 9.1 | 0.9 px | 0.7 px | below |
| `T` | 1000 mC | 150000 mC | 6.7 | 0.7 px (DIAL_L only) | n/a | below |
| `stock` | 20 permille | 1000 permille | 20 | n/a | 1.5 px (battery) | below; on a LINEAR glass 20 pm is exactly 1 px at `travel_px` 50 (D.11) |
| `load` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | 20 permille (`LOAD_QUANTUM_PERMILLE`, requested of the Graph, IW-9) | 1250 permille | 16.0 | 1.6 px | n/a | below: one quantum never fires a settle |

(The `load` reading rides the loop dirty key with the requested quantum above; until the Graph grants it the cadence is not claimed.) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* A slow honest ramp therefore draws as a detent (step, then rest) and a fast one (a decompression crossing a quantum every Graph step) as a continuous sweep.

**Example:** DIAL_L (`needle_len_px` 24), `inst_cryo_bay_p_o2` (0..300 dkPa). The room vents from 185 dkPa (pm 617) to 180 dkPa (pm 600) in one quantum; the new sample is drawn with `prev_honest_pm` 617, `displayed_pm` 600. At `T_c - t_sample` = 3 and `tick_alpha` 0.5: `a = 3.5 / 6` = 0.583, `pm_honest` = 617 + (600 - 617) x 0.583 = 607.1, `theta` = -120 + 607.1 x 0.24 = +25.70 degrees (sin 0.4337, cos 0.9011), offset (10.41, -21.63), tip (+10, -22). At rest on `displayed_pm` 600: `theta` +24.0, tip (+10, -22); at 620: `theta` +28.8, tip (+12, -21). Tie to PF D.2: at the floor glide `rate_floor` = max(0.5, 28.6 / 24) = 1.19 deg/s, `tip_speed` = 24 x 1.19 x pi/180 = 0.4985, about 0.5 px/s; on DIAL_S `rate_floor` = 28.6 / 18 = 1.59 deg/s gives 0.499 px/s.

### D.2 Quantity mapping and zone intervals (`zone_intervals`)

The zone intervals formula is defined as:

`pm(v) = clamp( floor( ((v - value_lo) * 1000 * 2 + (value_hi - value_lo)) / (2 * (value_hi - value_lo)) ), 0, 1000 )` (this formula and D.12 run in the build tool, outside GDScript, so the `[UNV]` integer-division note of D.3 does not apply; `floor` is mathematical floor)

`zone_i = [ pm(edge_lo_i), pm(edge_hi_i) )`, `edge_*_i` read from LS-7 **by band name**

`arc_px_i = (width_i / 1000) * (arc_deg * pi / 180) * r_in`; lint: `arc_px_i >= ZONE_MIN_ARC_PX`

`pm_min(r_in) = ZONE_MIN_ARC_PX * 1000 / (r_in * arc_deg * pi / 180)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `v` | int | gauge span | a band threshold (build) or the Filter's `displayed_value` (runtime, D.1) | the value in the quantity's integer unit |
| `value_lo`, `value_hi` | int | per IW-9 | calibration record | the gauge span |
| `edge_*_i` | int | | LS-7 (LSG:76) by band name, never retyped in instrument data; `load`'s 1000-permille edge is the one non-LS-7 edge (this GDD's calibration, the brownout, D.6 `sat` < 1000) | band thresholds |
| `width_i` | int per mille | 1..1000 | derived | zone width on the dial |
| `r_in` | int art px | 25 (DIAL_L), 19 (DIAL_S) | IW-7 | band inner radius (the conservative radius for the arc length) |
| `ZONE_MIN_ARC_PX` | int art px | 6 (range 4-10) `[PLACEHOLDER]` | this GDD | smallest zone arc the face may carry |

**Output Range:** each zone is 1..1000 pm wide; zones tile 0..1000 with no gap; the lint fails the build for any zone below `ZONE_MIN_ARC_PX` (use a larger size class or merge bands in data). `pm_min` is 57.3 pm on DIAL_L and 75.4 pm on DIAL_S. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The `load` span 0..1250 permille and its 1000-permille edge (800 pm) are unchanged by the move of `load` onto the Graph's `load_pm` kind (IW-9): the Graph clamps to the span, so every `load_pm` maps into 0..1000 pm and the NOTCHED zone (200 pm, 20.9 px on DIAL_L) is the overload stop only; a dead or idle bus (`load_pm` 0) sits at the low stop, inside the SOLID zone (0..800 pm).

**Example (arithmetic; the thresholds are read from LS-7 at build and shown only to prove the lint):**

| Instrument | Span | Zones in pm (name: width) | Smallest zone | DIAL_L arc | DIAL_S arc | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `inst_cryo_bay_p_o2` | 0..300 dkPa | ANOXIC 0..333: 333, HYPOXIC 333..467: 134, THIN 467..600: 133, NORMAL 600..1000: 400 | THIN 133 | 14.0 px | 10.6 px | both sizes pass |
| `inst_airlock_P` | 0..1100 dkPa | VACUUM 0..91: 91, DECOMP 91..364: 273, LOW 364..636: 272, NORMAL 636..1000: 364 | VACUUM 91 | 9.5 px | 7.2 px | both pass (DIAL_S has 1.2 px to spare) |
| `inst_a_machine_T` | -60000..90000 mC | DEEP_COLD 0..133, FREEZING 133..300, FROST 300..400: 100, COOL 400..467: **67**, COMFORT 467..667, HOT 667..867, SCORCH 867..1000 | COOL 67 | 7.0 px (67 >= 57.3) | 5.3 px (67 < 75.4) | **DIAL_L only**, which is why `T` is DIAL_L only |
| `inst_engine_bus_load` | 0..1250 permille | SOLID 0..800 (load 0..1000), NOTCHED 800..1000 (load 1000..1250): 200 | NOTCHED 200 | 20.9 px | 15.9 px | passes |

### D.3 Honest numeral formatting and the hold grid (`honest_numeral`)

The honest numeral formula is defined as:

`step = floor_div( displayed_value + lsb / 2, lsb )`; `shown = step * lsb`

`t_grid(j) = j * HONEST_TICK_MS` on `present_time`; at each `t_grid(j)` the card shows `step` computed from the **then-current** `displayed_value` (skipping any intermediate step, never queued, never faded)

`HONEST_TICK_MS` >= `honest_tick_floor_ms` (HUD-owned, HS-47; PF-18's 334 is the bound without the beacon share and is not used here)

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Native-unit carriage.** The numeral is formatted from the native-unit `displayed_value` (dkPa, mC, permille), which the instrument carries beside `prev_honest_pm` as one more presentation-local scalar (`prev_honest_native`, cleared with the others, IW-11 (c)); the pm triple is never used to rebuild a digit, because pm is lossy (`p_o2` 3.33 pm per dkPa, `T` 6.7 pm per C). **Lag bound restated:** the numeral shows the then-current native step at each grid boundary, so it lags the Filter's value by at most `HONEST_TICK_MS` (500 ms), or `2 x HONEST_TICK_MS` (1000 ms) when the HUD skips a boundary; the needle can additionally hold a one-quantum reversal for `INSTR_REVERSAL_DWELL_STEPS` 4 steps (400 ms) that the numeral does not hold, so the numeral can lead the needle by up to the dwell plus one boundary, 900 ms (1.4 s with a skipped boundary); the two never differ by more than one quantum, and both are honest.

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **The honest sample value.** The honest `displayed_value` is the Graph's integer value read at the Graph step in which the LS-20 bucket changed: the true value at that step, within one step's change of the bucket boundary, and neither the boundary itself nor a bucket centre. The numeral shows the accepted sample (IW-11 (b)) at each grid boundary.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `displayed_value` | int | gauge span, in the quantity's integer unit | `Perceived` (PF-8) | the Filter's displayed value |
| `lsb` | int | `p_o2` 1 dkPa, `P` 10 dkPa, `T` 1000 mC, `load` and `stock` 10 permille | calibration record (IW-9) | the quantity's least significant unit |
| `floor_div` | function | | this GDD | floor division for negative numerators: GDScript integer division on negatives truncates toward zero `[UNV]`, so the helper is explicit and has AC test vectors |
| `HONEST_TICK_MS` | int ms | 500 pinned, ratified 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions, D91 iii) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* (the 400-500 range is withdrawn; 30 ticks is the only feasible grid, HS-47) | HUD & Suit Layer (HS-14), cited by name | the shared hold grid; no second constant is registered here | *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*
| `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` | int | 3 (REG) | Pipeline | the transition ceiling; PF-18's arithmetic gives 334 without the beacon share, which this numeral does not use |

Unit formatting is integer-only: `p_o2` `shown` dkPa to kPa with one decimal (`shown div 10`, `.`, `shown mod 10`); `P` whole kPa (`shown div 10`); `T` whole C (`floor_div(shown + 500, 1000)`); `load` and `stock` whole percent (`shown div 10`). A value beyond the span shows the clamped bound with no "<" mark; the format never reveals more precision than `lsb`; an integer zero carries no sign, so a temperature that rounds to zero shows "0".

**Output Range:** a numeral in tabular cells of fixed width ("21.0", "101", "-22", "62"); at most `1000 / HONEST_TICK_MS` transitions per second per card (a "rolling second" is the half-open interval [t, t + 1000 ms): with a closed interval a 500 ms grid would show three boundaries, at 0, 500 and 1000, and the limit of two would be wrongly failed) (2 at 500 ms; fewer when the HUD skips a boundary), riding the HUD's shared grid boundary and adding no luminance event of its own (HS-47); numeral lag is at most `HONEST_TICK_MS`, and at most `2 x HONEST_TICK_MS` when the HUD's grid-skip rule skips a boundary. A lying numeral is the Filter's (`lying_digit_tick_schedule`, `T_tick` = max(`LIE_TICK_MS` 400, N_r x 334) (400 / 668 / 1002 / 1336 ms for N_r 1..4)), drawn as received.

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* **Flash counting unit (user ruling, D91 ii).** The registry's `flash_count` unit is `ceil(n_transitions / 2)` over a rolling second (a flash is a pair of opposing luminance transitions; an unpaired leftover transition counts as a whole flash). This GDD keeps counting card-numeral grid boundaries as transitions and keeps its own cap of `1000 / HONEST_TICK_MS` boundaries per rolling second, which is the stricter reading, so no limit above is loosened. Constraint satisfied: the card numeral's flash contribution must stay at or under `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` 3 on its own, before the HUD arbiter adds the suit numerals and lying readouts. Derivation: n_transitions <= 1000 / 500 = 2 per rolling second (one event per grid boundary however many digits change, AC-IW-96), so flash_count <= ceil(2 / 2) = 1, a third of the ceiling, leaving 2 of the 3 flashes for the HUD's other surfaces. Worked example: the venting run below produces numeral transitions at t = 0, 500, 1000 and 1500 ms; any rolling second [t, t + 1000 ms) holds two, so ceil(2 / 2) = 1 flash per second. The per-area weighting of `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` and the exemptions for sub-threshold digit swaps and prompt show-starts are the HUD's and Pipeline's arbiter (D91 iv, HS-47); this file claims no exemption for the card numeral and keeps the strict grid cap.

**Example:** `inst_cryo_bay_p_o2` venting at 15 dkPa/s from 190 dkPa, with the Graph value floored to an integer dkPa at each 100 ms step. The LS-20 `p_o2` buckets (5 dkPa) are crossed, and the Filter's honest memo refreshed, at 0.1 s (188), 0.4 s (184), 0.7 s (179), 1.1 s (173) and 1.4 s (169); the honest sample at each crossing is the integer value at that step. On the 500 ms grid: t = 0, memo 190, "19.0"; t = 500 ms, the latest memo is 184 from the crossing at 0.4 s, "18.4" (188 is never shown); t = 1000 ms, memo 179 from 0.7 s, "17.9"; t = 1500 ms, memo 169 from 1.4 s, "16.9" (173 is never shown). Two transitions per rolling second, and the card never shows a value the room did not have. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* `floor_div` vectors: `T` -22400 mC gives floor_div(-22400 + 500, 1000) = -22, "-22"; `T` -300 mC gives floor_div(200, 1000) = 0, "0".

### D.4 Closed-form settle (`settle_response`)

The settle response formula is defined as:

`tau = present_time - t_jump`

`y(tau) = 1 - exp(-zeta * omega * tau) * ( cos(omega_d * tau) + (zeta / sqrt(1 - zeta^2)) * sin(omega_d * tau) )`, `omega_d = omega * sqrt(1 - zeta^2)`

`drawn_pm = clamp( from_pm + (to_pm - from_pm) * y(tau), 0, 1000 )` for `0 <= tau < INSTR_SETTLE_MS / 1000`; `drawn_pm = to_pm` afterwards

**Trigger** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: rest is defined in time, IW-11 (b))*: with `rest_pm` the value the needle held when the instrument left rest, a new honest sample starts a settle iff `motion_mode` is HONEST, **the quantity's calibration record has `settle_eligible` true** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (true for `load` only at MVP; every other quantity draws the D.1 ramp whatever its rate, so a fast vent or a hatch to vacuum on `p_o2` or `P`, which can exceed 40 pm in 400 ms above about 30 dkPa/s, is a sweep and never a settle: detent, not settle), the instrument was at rest (no honest sample for `INSTR_REST_STEPS` Graph steps and no settle active) when the window opened, the sample arrives within `INSTR_SETTLE_WINDOW_STEPS` Graph steps of the first sample after rest, and `abs(target_pm - rest_pm) >= INSTR_SETTLE_JUMP_PM`; then `from_pm := the value now drawn`, `to_pm := target_pm`, `t_jump := present_time`. A sample that does not satisfy the test is drawn by the D.1 ramp, and a run that leaves its window without a jump is a sweep until rest is re-entered. **Re-base:** while a settle is active, any further change of the honest displayed value sets `from_pm := the value currently drawn`, `to_pm := the new value`, `t_jump := now` (velocity is discarded); one triple always suffices. Any non-HONEST frame clears the triple and bypasses this formula.

`Mp = exp( -pi * zeta / sqrt(1 - zeta^2) )`; `t_p = pi / omega_d`; `t_s(2 %) = 4 / (zeta * omega)`; residual at `INSTR_SETTLE_MS`: `exp(-zeta * omega * INSTR_SETTLE_MS/1000) * sqrt(1 + (zeta / sqrt(1 - zeta^2))^2)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `zeta` (`INSTR_SETTLE_ZETA`) | float | 0.7 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 0.70-0.77 (joint lint below; the effective ceiling at omega 20) | this GDD | damping ratio; at or below about 0.69 the overshoot exceeds 5 % (ART 7.3); above about 0.77 at omega 20 the exact 2 % time falls under 250 ms (0.78: 180 ms; 0.80: 188 ms; the textbook approximation wrongly gives 250 ms at 0.8) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `omega` (`INSTR_SETTLE_OMEGA`) | float rad/s | 20 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 18-20 (joint lint below) | this GDD | natural frequency |
| `INSTR_SETTLE_MS` | int ms | 400 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 360-400 (joint lint below) | this GDD (ART 7.3 bound) | the settle window |
| `INSTR_SETTLE_JUMP_PM` | int pm | 40 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 25-100 | this GDD | the jump threshold on the change accumulated inside the window; 40 pm is 4 px on DIAL_L |
| `settle_eligible` | bool per quantity | `load` true; `p_o2`, `P`, `T`, `stock` false at MVP | calibration record (IW-9) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | gates the settle trigger by quantity; a quantity is set true only when an authored action that reaches the jump threshold from rest is confirmed for it (OQ-IW-36) |
| `INSTR_REST_STEPS` | int Graph steps | 3 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 2-5 | this GDD | rest in time: no honest sample for this many steps (300 ms) and no settle active; replaces the retired `INSTR_SETTLE_REST_PM` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `INSTR_SETTLE_WINDOW_STEPS` | int Graph steps | 4 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 3-5 | this GDD | the jump window after leaving rest (400 ms) in which the accumulated change is compared with `INSTR_SETTLE_JUMP_PM` |
| `from_pm`, `to_pm` | float / int pm | 0..1000 | the triple (IW-11) | start (the value drawn) and target |
| `t_jump` | float s | `present_time` | the triple | set at the jump; frozen while PAUSED because `present_time` freezes (PIPE PR-9) |

**Joint parameter lint (`settle_params_lint`, build time; the three parameters are only safe together, and the earlier published box was not).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the box 0.70-0.8 / 18-20 / 360-400 admitted 0.7 / 18 / 360, whose end snap is 1.51 px, and the patch "MS >= 380 at zeta 0.7" still admitted 0.7 / 18 / 380 at 1.17 px.)* A parameter set passes iff all of: (1) `zeta` < 1 (the closed form is the underdamped case; `zeta` >= 1 is rejected) and `Mp` <= 0.05 (ART 7.3), i.e. `zeta` >= 0.690; (2) the **exact** 2 % settling time `t_s` lies between 250 and 400 ms (ART 7.3), where `t_s` is the last time `abs(1 - y(t))` exceeds 0.02, found numerically by the lint (a 0.1 ms sweep over 0 to 0.8 s); the textbook estimate 4 / (`zeta` x `omega`) is only an envelope approximation (286 ms at the defaults against the exact 299 ms) and is not the test *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; (3) the end snap on a full-range 1000 pm DIAL_L jump, `100.5 x exp(-zeta x omega x INSTR_SETTLE_MS / 1000) / sqrt(1 - zeta^2)` px, is at most 1.0, i.e. **`zeta x omega x INSTR_SETTLE_MS / 1000 >= ln(100.5 / sqrt(1 - zeta^2))`**. Passing corners *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (exact `t_s`): 0.7 / 20 / 400 (`t_s` 299 ms, snap 0.52 px); 0.70 / 18 / 400 (`t_s` 332 ms, 0.91 px); 0.7 / 20 / 360 (299 ms, 0.91 px); 0.75 / 20 / 400 (287 ms, 0.38 px). The corner 0.8 / 20 / 400 that the approximation passed is a failure under (2) (exact `t_s` 188 ms). **Expected failures:** 0.7 / 18 / 360 fails (3) at 1.51 px; 0.7 / 19 / 360 fails (3) at 1.17 px; 0.75 / 18 / 360 fails (3) at 1.17 px; 0.7 / 15 / 400 fails (3) at 2.1 px; 0.8 / 15 / 400 fails (3) at 1.38 px; 0.7 / 20 / 300 fails (3) at 2.1 px; 0.9 / 20 / 400 fails (2) with exact `t_s` 235 ms; 0.8 / 20 / 400 fails (2) with exact `t_s` 188 ms *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The ranges in the variable table are the outer bound of each knob and are **not jointly safe**; the lint is the authority.

**Output Range:** `drawn_pm` is within [min(from, to), max(from, to) + Mp x abs(delta)] and clamped at the end stops (the pin stops an overshoot at 0 or 1000); exactly `to_pm` from `INSTR_SETTLE_MS` on. Arithmetic at the defaults: `sqrt(1 - 0.49)` = 0.71414; `Mp` = exp(-3.0795) = 0.0460 (4.6 %, under 5 %); `omega_d` = 14.283 rad/s; `zeta x omega` = 14; exact `t_s(2 %)` = 299 ms (inside 250-400; the approximation 4 / (`zeta` x `omega`) gives 286 ms and is not asserted) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; `t_p` = 220 ms; residual at 400 ms = e^-5.6 x 1.40 = 0.0052, so the final snap on a full-range 1000 pm jump on DIAL_L is 0.52 art px (hidden by tip rounding; under 0.5 px for any jump up to about 950 pm). Overshoot reaches 1 px only for abs(delta) >= 216 pm (DIAL_L) or 288 pm (DIAL_S): smaller jumps read as an ease-out, which is still the settle signature. Reduced motion: `drawn_pm = to_pm` at the jump (D.10).

**Example:** `inst_engine_bus_load` (DIAL_L), a breaker closes and the load reading steps from 300 to 700 pm (delta 400, from rest, 400 >= 40). At tau 100 ms: exp(-1.4) = 0.2466; `omega_d` x tau = 1.4283 (cos 0.1420, sin 0.9899); bracket = 0.1420 + 0.9802 x 0.9899 = 1.1123; `y` = 1 - 0.2743 = 0.7257; `drawn_pm` = 300 + 0.7257 x 400 = 590.3. At tau 220 ms (the peak): `y` = 1.0460, `drawn_pm` = 718.4, an overshoot of 18.4 pm, 1.85 px on DIAL_L. At 400 ms: 700, still. Re-base: at tau 100 ms a further sample moves the target to 720; `from_pm` := 590.3, `to_pm` := 720, `t_jump` := now.

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Settle reachability on the manifest** (which instrument can settle under which authored action; everywhere else the honest signature is "step, then rest"). A settle needs 40 pm of accumulated honest change inside 400 ms from rest.

| Instrument (quantity) | Authored action | Change in the dial's pm | Settle? | Overshoot at the tip |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `inst_engine_bus_load` (DIAL_L, `load`, 0 .. 1250 permille, so 1 permille = 0.8 pm; supply taken as 2500 W for illustration, Graph D.6 and DA-20 own it) | close the cryo branch breaker `j_cryo` (+640 W) | 256 permille = 205 pm (20 px of travel) | yes | 9.4 pm, 0.9 px |
| same | install `mod_heater` (+500 W) | 200 permille = 160 pm (16 px) | yes | 7.4 pm, 0.7 px |
| same | close B's seam breaker with `r_b` SCRAMmed (+1240 W) | 496 permille = 397 pm (40 px) | yes | 18 pm, 1.8 px (needs a bus dial in view) |
| `p_o2` dials | a breach at the D.3 example rate, 15 dkPa/s | 6 dkPa = 20 pm in 400 ms (3.33 pm per dkPa on the 0..300 dkPa span) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | no: a sweep. A faster vent (above about 30 dkPa/s) would reach 40 pm, but `settle_eligible` is false for `p_o2`, so it is still a ramp | n/a |
| `P` dials (`inst_airlock_P`, `inst_b_breach_P`) | opening a hatch to vacuum | not claimed: the venting rate is the Graph's (LSG D.3), OQ-IW-36 | not claimed | n/a |
| `T` dials | any | a room cannot move 6000 mC in 400 ms | no | n/a |
| `stock` (LINEAR, DIAL_S) | one refill or refuel press | not claimed: the chunk size is Interaction's and the Graph's M16, OQ-IW-36 | not claimed | n/a |

On the verified actions only the `load` dial settles, and its overshoot reaches a full pixel only for steps of 216 pm (DIAL_L) or more, so most settles that do fire draw a slower ease-out rather than a visible bounce; atmosphere and store dials show the detent (step, then rest) whatever their rate, by the `settle_eligible` flag. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **What this means for the fantasy.** The same-instrument contrast of a settle against a glide exists only if the scanner can read `load` (the `load` dial is lie-eligible only through the scanner credit, IW-29, OQ-IW-43); otherwise honest settle and lying glide are met on different instruments, and the lesson ("needles that wander are lying; needles that shake are warning") is carried by the detent, the tremor and the glide, with the settle as the heavy honest case on the one dial that has it. The settle and its joint lint are kept for the `load` dial and for any action OQ-IW-36 confirms; no other rule depends on them.

### D.5 Tremor (`tremor_offset`)

The tremor offset formula is defined as:

`s(t) = 0.6 * sin(2 * pi * f1 * t + phi1) + 0.4 * sin(2 * pi * f2 * t + phi2)`, `t = present_time`; `f_1 = 6.0 + 1.5 * u_1` (6.0 .. 7.5 Hz) and `f_2 = 9.0 + 1.0 * u_2` (9.0 .. 10.0 Hz), two **disjoint sub-bands** of the 6-10 Hz tremor band, so `f_2 - f_1` >= 1.5 Hz and the beat period is at most 0.67 s *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (the earlier shared band let about 12 % of instruments draw near-equal frequencies and so long or permanent null windows with no tremor); `(u_i, phi_i)` from a presentation-local stateless hash of `(ref_key, i)`

`A_eff = max( TREMOR_AMP_PX, ceil( TREMOR_MIN_SCREEN_PX / k ) )`

`env(t) = min(1, (t - t_on) / TREMOR_ONSET_MS)` while `p_eff` holds; `env_off x max(0, 1 - (t - t_off) / TREMOR_DECAY_MS)` after it ends, with `env_off` = `env(t_off)` **latched at the instant the predicate ends** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (the earlier decay branch started from 1.0, so a predicate pulse that ended mid-onset jumped the envelope up to full before decaying); `p_eff = predicate OR (t - t_last_true) < TREMOR_HOLD_S`

`o = clamp( round( TREMOR_GAIN * A_eff * env * s(t) ), -A_eff, +A_eff )` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, **in frame-index space** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: `o` is a number of pre-rendered needle frames (OQ-IW-7: one frame index is one tip pixel along the arc, or one meniscus row of a LINEAR glass), and the drawn index is `idx_drawn = clamp( idx_base + o, 0, N_idx - 1 )` with `idx_base` the settle or ramp base index and `N_idx` the frame count of the sheet, so a non-zero `o` always moves the drawn tip by exactly `o` indices (the earlier tangent offset in art px could round to the same tip pixel on about 9 % of frames at 45 degrees) and the sum can never leave the end stops. The angular equivalent is `o x arc_deg / (N_idx - 1)` degrees

(The two-sine sum is wanted here: PF D.4 rejected it for the **wander** because a beat that dips to rest would break never-rest, whereas tremor has no never-rest duty and its irregularity is the point; a dip to rest between bursts reads as a real needle on a shaking hull.)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TREMOR_F_MIN_HZ`, `TREMOR_F_MAX_HZ` | float Hz | 6 and 10 (named here from ART 7.3, not new) | art bible | the tremor band; disjoint from the Filter's 0.40-0.45 Hz wander; the two sines draw from the sub-bands 6.0-7.5 and 9.0-10.0 Hz inside it | *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*
| `u_i`, `phi_i` | float | [0, 1), [0, 2 pi) | a presentation hash of `ref_key` (PF D.14 is the precedent; ADR candidate); **never** the kernel `rng_derive`, sim RNG is never used for presentation | per-instrument frequencies and phases, so two trembling gauges do not move in step |
| `TREMOR_AMP_PX` | int art px | 1 (ART 7.3 +-1), range 1-2 | this GDD | amplitude in art px |
| `TREMOR_MIN_SCREEN_PX` | int screen px | 2 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 2-3 | this GDD | the HR-12 floor applied to tremor |
| `k` | int | 1..`k_auto`+2 | PIPE zoom ladder | world scale |
| `TREMOR_GAIN` | float | 1.5 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 1.2-2.0 | this GDD | gain per unit of `A_eff` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: at `A_eff` 1, abs(o) = 1 when abs(s) >= 0.33; at `A_eff` 2, abs(o) = 1 when abs(s) >= 0.167 and 2 when abs(s) >= 0.5 |
| `TREMOR_ONSET_MS`, `TREMOR_DECAY_MS` | int ms | 150 (100-300), 600 (300-1000) `[PLACEHOLDER]` | this GDD | envelope; on a re-rise during a decay `t_on := t - env x TREMOR_ONSET_MS` so `env` stays continuous |
| `TREMOR_HOLD_S` | float s | 0 (range 0-1.0) `[PLACEHOLDER]` | this GDD | the playtest remedy if the predicate flaps at Graph-step rate |
| `predicate` | bool | | IW-12, refreshed per Graph step | true only while `motion_mode` is HONEST and a physical stress cause holds |

**Output Range:** `o` is an integer in [-`A_eff`, +`A_eff`]; 0 once the predicate has been false for `TREMOR_DECAY_MS`; exactly 0 in every non-HONEST frame. `A_eff` is 2 art px at `k` 1 and 1 art px at every `k` >= 2, so the offset is at least `TREMOR_MIN_SCREEN_PX` screen px at every rung *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (reachable now that the gain scales with `A_eff`: the earlier unscaled gain reached 2 only at abs(s) = 1, so the k=1 tremor was effectively 1 px and the HR-12 floor claim was false). **Tremor and wander stay distinct:** wander peaks at 2 art px at 0.35-0.45 Hz, tremor at `A_eff` (1 art px at every `k` >= 2) at 6-10 Hz. At `k` 1 the peaks are equal (2 art px) and the cue is frequency and flip rate (tremor 12-20 tip flips per second against the wander's 4-6, VA-6); that is an accepted limitation of the zoom-out rung, recorded as OQ-IW-41. **Activity lint (`TREMOR_ACTIVITY`)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: measured on the **drawn tip index**, not on `o`: for every manifest `ref_key`, in any 2 s window at `env` 1, the drawn index changes between consecutive render frames at least `TREMOR_TIP_CHANGE_MIN_HZ` times per second (8 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, set by a measured sweep of the manifest `ref_key`s; the value must stay above the wander's 4-6 flips per second by a non-overlap margin, so the cue stays flip rate, OQ-IW-41) at `A_eff` 1 and at `A_eff` 2; a failing `ref_key` takes the next `tremor_salt` in its record. (The earlier lint counted frames with abs(o) >= 1, a worst case of 46 % at `A_eff` 1 and 62 % at `A_eff` 2 in a sweep of 4000 random draws; that measured `o`, which is not what the player sees, and it is replaced.) **Short pulse:** a predicate that holds for 100 ms and ends has `env_off` = 100 / `TREMOR_ONSET_MS` = 0.67, the decay runs from 0.67 and `env` never exceeds 0.67 (AC-IW-121).

**Example:** `ref_key` hashes give `f1` 7.2 Hz, `f2` 9.1 Hz, `phi` 0. At `k` 3, `A_eff` = max(1, ceil(2/3)) = 1; mid-stress (`env` 1) at t = 0.050 s: `s` = 0.6 sin(2.262) + 0.4 sin(2.859) = 0.6 x 0.7705 + 0.4 x 0.2790 = 0.574; `o` = clamp(round(1.5 x 0.574 = 0.86), -1, 1) = +1 px. At `k` 1, `A_eff` = 2 and `o` = clamp(round(1.5 x 2 x 0.574 = 1.72), -2, 2) = +2 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Decay: 300 ms after the predicate ends `env` = 0.5, so the product is at most 0.75 in magnitude and rounds to +-1 only when abs(s) >= 0.667, mostly 0; at 600 ms `env` = 0 and the needle rests on one pixel.

### D.6 Card scale, size and placement (`card_layout`)

The card layout formula is defined as:

`plate_h = CARD_H_ART_PX[kind] + 7 * wraps` (`wraps` = 1 when the label wraps to a second row, IW-37, else 0; `CARD_H_ART_PX[DIAL]` 72, `CARD_H_ART_PX[LINEAR]` 88) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

`H_card = max( HUD_MIN_SCALE, min( H, floor( CARD_MAX_FRAC_H * screen_h / plate_h ) ) )`

`plate_screen = (CARD_W_ART_PX, plate_h) * H_card`

`a = world_to_hud(anchor_px)` re-read every frame (the unjolted pose, quantised to the HUD grid); candidate rectangles `R_c`, `c` in {right, left, above, below}, at gap `CARD_GAP_HUD_PX * H_card` from the instrument footprint `F`, each **clamped to the safe rect and slid along its free axis on the HUD grid before it is tested**; the **side** is chosen at open as the first `R_c` with (a) `R_c` inside the safe rect (5 % margin), (b) `R_c` disjoint from `F`, (c) `R_c` disjoint from the chest disc `D(chest, CARD_KEEPOUT_CHEST_R * k)`, (d) `R_c` disjoint from the beam capsule `C(chest, anchor, CARD_KEEPOUT_BEAM_HALFW * k)`, (e) `R_c` disjoint from every teammate disc `D(mate, CARD_KEEPOUT_CHEST_R * k)`, every lit interactable footprint in reach and the witness disc `D(witness_anchor, CARD_KEEPOUT_WITNESS_R * k)`; none qualifies: step `H_card` down by one (to `HUD_MIN_SCALE`) and test again; none at any `H_card`: (a) and (b) stay hard, then minimise the violation vector in the order (c), (e), (d), then the overlap area, then prefer the larger `H_card`; `pos = H_card * round(pos / H_card)` (the HUD art-px grid)

Re-choice of the side happens only on a camera cut (zoom, window resize) or when the latched rect fails (a), (b) or (c); the capsule (d) runs chest-to-anchor, which is the line the beam must keep lit, so it is stable while the gauge stays lit and is tested at choice time only.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `H` | int | [`H_default`, 2 x `H_default`], `H_default` = max(`k_auto`, 3) | Pipeline registry formula `hud_multiplier` (`H_default = max(k, 3)`; `k` there is the default rung `k_auto`, because the HUD does not change with zoom, PIPE PR-12) | HUD scale |
| `HUD_MIN_SCALE` | int | 3 (REG) | Pipeline | the floor on `H_card` |
| `CARD_W_ART_PX`, `CARD_H_ART_PX` | int HUD art px | 72 wide; 72 high for a DIAL, 88 high for a LINEAR (`CARD_H_LINEAR_ART_PX`, range 80-96) `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 64-88 for a DIAL | this GDD | plate size at `CARD_MAG` 1 (fixed). DIAL: a 5-px label row, the 46-px dial block, a 7-px numeral row, margins. LINEAR *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: 4 margin + 5 label + 3 gap + the 62-px glass block + 3 gap + 7 numeral + 4 margin = 88 |
| `CARD_MAX_FRAC_H` | float | 0.5 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 0.4-0.6 | this GDD | the plate's share of the frame height |
| `CARD_GAP_HUD_PX` | int HUD art px | 4 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 2-8 | this GDD | gap to the instrument |
| `CARD_KEEPOUT_CHEST_R`, `CARD_KEEPOUT_BEAM_HALFW`, `CARD_KEEPOUT_WITNESS_R` | int world art px | 28 (20-40), 12 (8-20), 24 (16-40) `[PLACEHOLDER]` | this GDD | the avatar (and each teammate) and the beam corridor the plate must not cover (28 is about half the 55-px crewmate figure), and the disc around the drawn witness *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `chest` | point | per facing | Controller (the per-facing chest point, \|x\| <= 2) | the beam origin |
| `screen_h`, `k` | int | display; 1..`k_auto`+2 | OS; Pipeline | frame height; world scale |

**Output Range:** `H_card` in [3, `H`]; the plate never exceeds `CARD_MAX_FRAC_H` of the frame height unless `H_card` is already the floor 3 (only for `screen_h` below 432). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Reachability of (a) and (b):** at `H_card` 3 (plate 216 px wide) on every display of D.7 and at every rung up to `k_auto` + 2, at least one horizontal side clears the instrument cell and the safe rect (right needs the anchor at or left of `x_safe_right - (half cell + gap + plate)`, left needs it at or right of `x_safe_left + (half cell + gap + plate)`, and the left bound is below the right bound), so a candidate that does not overlap the instrument always exists. **`CARD_REANCHOR_PX` is retired** (checkpoint ruling C9): the plate follows its anchor per frame on the grid, so there is no drift threshold.

**Example:** 1080p, `H` 3: `H_card` = max(3, min(3, floor(540 / 72) = 7)) = 3; plate 216 x 216 px (20 % of the height). 1080p, `H` 6 (the maximum): min(6, 7) = 6, plate 432 px (40 %). 720p, `H` 6: min(6, floor(360 / 72) = 5) = 5, plate 360 px (50 %), not 432 (60 %). A wrapped label (`plate_h` 79) at 720p, `H` 6: floor(360 / 79) = 4, plate 288 x 316 px (43.9 %). Placement at 1080p, `k` 3, `H` 3: chest at screen (960, 540), `inst_cryo_bay_p_o2` anchor 72 world art px east at (1176, 540), footprint 180 x 138 px (x 1086..1266); candidate right at gap 12 px: plate x 1278..1494, y 432..648; the chest disc (radius 84) ends at x 1044 and the beam capsule (half-width 36, y 504..576, x 960..1176) ends at x 1176, so the plate is disjoint from both and inside the safe rect (x 96..1824, y 54..1026): right wins and is latched. Non-Ground share of the card: about 30 % of the plate, 0.3 x 216^2 / (1920 x 1080) = 0.67 % of the frame at `H` 3. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **LINEAR example:** 1080p, `H` 6: `H_card` = min(6, floor(540 / 88) = 6) = 6, plate 432 x 528 px (48.9 %); 720p, `H` 6: min(6, floor(360 / 88) = 4) = 4, plate 288 x 352 px (48.9 %). **Fallback fixture (720p, `H` 6, `k` 4):** safe rect x 64..1216, y 36..684; chest at (640, 380); `inst_a_machine_T`-class DIAL_L anchor 72 world px east at (928, 380); housing footprint 240 x 184 (x 808..1048, y 288..472); chest disc radius 112, beam capsule half-width 48. At `H_card` 5 (plate 360) and 4 (plate 288) no side is clean (right and left overlap the footprint, the chest disc or the corridor; above and below overlap the footprint); at `H_card` 3 (plate 216) the right candidate slid up to y 36..252 is clean, so the plate is 216 px (30 %), never over the instrument, the chest or the corridor. The retired rule would have drawn the right candidate clamped to x 856..1216, over the instrument.

### D.7 Legibility at every zoom rung and HUD scale (`legibility_table`)

The legibility table formula is defined as:

`screen_px = art_px * k` (world) or `art_px * H_card` (card); ladder `k` in [1, `k_auto` + 2]; `k_auto = floor( min(W / 640, H_s / 360) )`; `shown_share = needle_len_px * k / screen_h`; **locate-only** flag when `k / k_auto < 0.5` (the Pipeline's own acuity line, PIPE:401; no new number)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `k` | int | 1..8 | PIPE C6 | world scale |
| `k_auto` | int | 720p 2, 1280x800 Steam Deck 2, 1080p 3, 1440p 4, 2160p 6 | PIPE | the default rung |
| `needle_len_px` | int | 24 (DIAL_L), 18 (DIAL_S) | IW-7 | art px |
| `NEEDLE_SCREEN_MIN_PX` | int screen px | 24 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 18-32 (accessibility tier) | this GDD | the world needle's absolute floor |
| `H_card` | int | D.6 | D.6 | card scale |

**Output Range:** the world table below (every dial, `k` 1..8), the needle's share of picture height by display, and the card table by `H`. The HR-12 stroke (2 screen px) holds at every rung because the needle is authored 2 art px thick; the gleam and tremor art-px size is raised only at `k` 1 (D.5, D.9).

**World dial by zoom rung (art px x `k`):**

| `k` | DIAL_L needle | DIAL_S needle | Outer arc diameter L / S | Tip 1-px step | Zone band (3 art px) | Wander peak (2 art px) | Gleam, art -> screen | Tremor, art -> screen | Needle stroke | `NEEDLE_SCREEN_MIN_PX` 24, L / S |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 24 | 18 | 56 / 44 | 1 | 3 | 2 | 2 -> 2 | 2 -> 2 | 2 | yes / **no** |
