| 2 | 48 | 36 | 112 / 88 | 2 | 6 | 4 | 1 -> 2 | 1 -> 2 | 4 | yes / yes |
| 3 | 72 | 54 | 168 / 132 | 3 | 9 | 6 | 1 -> 3 | 1 -> 3 | 6 | yes / yes |
| 4 | 96 | 72 | 224 / 176 | 4 | 12 | 8 | 1 -> 4 | 1 -> 4 | 8 | yes / yes |
| 5 | 120 | 90 | 280 / 220 | 5 | 15 | 10 | 1 -> 5 | 1 -> 5 | 10 | yes / yes |
| 6 | 144 | 108 | 336 / 264 | 6 | 18 | 12 | 1 -> 6 | 1 -> 6 | 12 | yes / yes |
| 7 | 168 | 126 | 392 / 308 | 7 | 21 | 14 | 1 -> 7 | 1 -> 7 | 14 | yes / yes |
| 8 | 192 | 144 | 448 / 352 | 8 | 24 | 16 | 1 -> 8 | 1 -> 8 | 16 | yes / yes |

**DIAL_L needle as a share of picture height by display (DIAL_S x 0.75):**

| `k` | 720p (ladder 1..4) | Steam Deck 1280x800 (1..4) | 1080p (1..5) | 1440p (1..6) | 2160p (1..8) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 3.3 % | 3.0 % | 2.2 % (locate-only) | 1.7 % (locate-only) | 1.1 % (locate-only) |
| 2 | 6.7 % (default) | 6.0 % (default) | 4.4 % | 3.3 % (ratio 0.5) | 2.2 % (locate-only) |
| 3 | 10.0 % | 9.0 % | 6.7 % (default) | 5.0 % | 3.3 % (ratio 0.5) |
| 4 | 13.3 % | 12.0 % | 8.9 % | 6.7 % (default) | 4.4 % |
| 5 | n/a | n/a | 11.1 % | 8.3 % | 5.6 % |
| 6 | n/a | n/a | n/a | 10.0 % | 6.7 % (default) |
| 7 | n/a | n/a | n/a | n/a | 7.8 % |
| 8 | n/a | n/a | n/a | n/a | 8.9 % |

At `k` 1 on 720p and the Steam Deck the ratio `k / k_auto` is exactly 0.5 (borderline, not below). The default needle is 6.0 to 6.7 % of the picture height at every display (6.7 % at 720p, 1080p, 1440p and 2160p, 6.0 % on the 1280x800 Steam Deck). **Zoom 1x at 2160p** is the weakest point: the world dial is a 56-px arc (24-px needle) in a 3840-px frame, readable only by pattern and the needle, and the card is the reading tool there. At `k` 1 the tremor is `A_eff` 2 art px = 2 screen px, at the edge of perceptibility; the design accepts it, since the tell is for a player standing at the instrument and instrument tremor is never the only tell (IW-12, AC-IW-106).

**Card by display (DIAL_L card, `CARD_MAG` 1, plate 72 x 72 HUD art px; the dial is drawn 1:1 in HUD art px):**

| Display | `k_auto` | `H` range | Default `H` -> `H_card`, plate px (share of height); numeral / label cap | Maximum `H` -> `H_card`, plate px (share); numeral / label cap | Card dial vs wall dial, `H_card / k`: at the default `k`, at `k` 1, at the top rung |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 720p | 2 | 3..6 | 3 -> 3, 216 (30.0 %); 21 / 15 | 6 -> 5 (capped), 360 (50.0 %); 35 / 25 | 1.5; 3.0; 3 / 4 = 0.75 |
| Steam Deck 1280x800 (`H` 3 over `k` 2) | 2 | 3..6 | 3 -> 3, 216 (27.0 %); 21 / 15 | 6 -> 5 (capped), 360 (45.0 %); 35 / 25 | 1.5; 3.0; 0.75 |
| 1080p | 3 | 3..6 | 3 -> 3, 216 (20.0 %); 21 / 15 | 6 -> 6, 432 (40.0 %); 42 / 30 | 1.0; 3.0; 3 / 5 = 0.6 |
| 1440p | 4 | 4..8 | 4 -> 4, 288 (20.0 %); 28 / 20 | 8 -> 8, 576 (40.0 %); 56 / 40 | 1.0; 4.0; 4 / 6 = 0.67 |
| 2160p | 6 | 6..12 | 6 -> 6, 432 (20.0 %); 42 / 30 | 12 -> 12, 864 (40.0 %); 84 / 60 | 1.0; 6.0; 6 / 8 = 0.75 |

The numeral cap (7 art px x `H_card`) and label cap (5 x `H_card`) meet the HR-12 floors of 21 and 15 screen px at `H_card` 3. "Magnified" (ART:747) means HUD scale, zoom-independence, an opaque plate, the label and the numeral, not a larger sprite: at the default view the card dial has the same screen size as the wall dial (ratio 1.0), at zoom 1x it is 3.0x to 6.0x, and at the top rung it is smaller than the wall dial (0.6x at 1080p, `k` 5), which is accepted because the card's value is the numeral and the label. The card exceeds the critical strip's size cap by design (HUD carries the modal exception, HS-56, provisional). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **LINEAR card** (plate 72 x 88, three of the 18 MVP instruments are LINEAR: `inst_res_main_level`, `inst_home_fuel_level`, `inst_res_b_level`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: 720p: default `H` 3 gives `H_card` 3 and 264 px (36.7 %), maximum `H` 6 gives 4 (capped) and 352 px (48.9 %); Steam Deck 1280x800: 3 gives 264 px (33.0 %), 6 gives 4 and 352 px (44.0 %); 1080p: 3 gives 264 px (24.4 %), 6 gives 6 and 528 px (48.9 %); 1440p: 4 gives 352 px (24.4 %), 8 gives 8 and 704 px (48.9 %); 2160p: 6 gives 528 px (24.4 %), 12 gives 12 and 1056 px (48.9 %); the numeral and label caps are those of the table above. The glass is drawn 1:1 in HUD art px, so at the default view its 62-px housing has the same screen height as the wall glass.

**Example:** the Steam Deck at its default (`k` 2, `H` 3): DIAL_L needle 48 screen px (6.0 % of 800), tip step 2 px, zone band 6 px, gleam 1 art px = 2 screen px; the card plate 216 px (27.0 %), numeral cap 21 px, the card dial 168 px across (56 x 3) against the wall dial's 112 (56 x 2), ratio 1.5.

### D.8 Instrument count against the subject budget (`instrument_budget`)

The instrument budget formula is defined as:

`N_loaded = N_home + N_attached_sites + N_site_current`; lint: `N_loaded <= INSTRUMENT_SHARE_MAX`; and, with Interaction's own lint, `F + L_seed + C <= INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` with the instruments counted inside `F`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `N_home` | int | 9 | IW-29 manifest | home-ship instruments |
| `N_attached_sites` | int | 0..9 | IW-29 manifest (derelict A 4, derelict B 5) | instruments on derelicts already welded to the home ship (they stay loaded) |
| `N_site_current` | int | 0..5 | IW-29 manifest | instruments on the derelict being boarded and not yet welded |
| `INSTRUMENT_SHARE_MAX` | int | 24 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 14-48 | this GDD (counted inside Interaction's `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET`) | the share of subject budget instruments may take |
| `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` | int | 120 (Interaction-owned, IA-37; `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` + `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE` <= 144; cited, not restated) | Interaction | the fixtures ceiling |
| `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` | int | 192 (range 128-384, REG) | Emitter State | the exposure ceiling |
| `F`, `L_seed`, `C` | int | | Interaction | fixtures, seeded loose items, containers; instruments are part of `F`, not additive |

**Output Range:** `N_loaded` 0..18 on the MVP manifest. **Example:** home + derelict B being boarded: 9 + 0 + 5 = 14 <= 24 (11.7 % of 120, 9.7 % of 144, 7.3 % of 192). Worst case, both derelicts welded on: 9 + 9 = 18 <= 24 (15 % of 120).

### D.9 Gleam edge and the card's gleam mark (`gleam_edge_px`)

The gleam edge formula is defined as:

`gleam_edge_art_px(k) = clamp( ceil( GLEAM_EDGE_SCREEN_MIN / k ), GLEAM_EDGE_PX_LO, GLEAM_EDGE_PX_HI )` (world; the screen thickness is `gleam_edge_art_px x k`)

`gleam_card_screen_px = 1 * H_card` (card: 1 HUD art px)

`a_gleam = leak_amplitude_pm / 1000`; `a_draw = min(a_gleam, LEAK_MAX_CH / C_FRINGE_MAX)` = min(`a_gleam`, 0.942); `peak_channel = a_draw * C_FRINGE_MAX`, `C_FRINGE_MAX = 230 / 255 = 0.902` (the brightest channel of `#B07BE6`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `GLEAM_EDGE_SCREEN_MIN` | int screen px | 2 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 2-3 | this GDD | the HR-12 stroke floor applied to the gleam |
| `GLEAM_EDGE_PX_LO`, `GLEAM_EDGE_PX_HI` | int art px | 1 and 2 | the Filter's `[PLACEHOLDER]` `GLEAM_EDGE_PX` range (PF-16, PF D.10) | the range inside which the zoom-dependent value is requested (OQ-IW-4) |
| `k` | int | 1..8 | PIPE | world scale |
| `leak_amplitude_pm` | int per mille | 0..1000; floor while ACTIVE is stage-indexed: `AMP_FLOOR_PM` 400 at stages 0-1, 325 at stage 2, 250 at stage 3 (PF-15, Filter-owned) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* | `Perceived` (PF-8) | the Filter's amplitude, passed through unscaled |
| `LEAK_MAX_CH` | float | 0.85 (:= `DECOY_MAX_CH`, REG) | Perception Filter / Lighting | the peak channel any Filter-emitted pixel may reach |

**Output Range:** world edge 2 art px = 2 screen px at `k` 1 and 1 art px = `k` screen px at `k` >= 2 (2..8); card edge `H_card` screen px (>= 3). `peak_channel` must stay <= `LEAK_MAX_CH` 0.85, i.e. `leak_amplitude_pm` <= 942 at the fringe colour (at alpha 1 the fringe peaks at 0.902, over the Filter's 0.85 ceiling). **Owner (design review): Instruments clamp the alpha at draw time**, so the cap holds even when the Filter's envelope reaches 943-1000 pm; the Filter is asked to keep its envelope at or below 942, and each clamped frame increments the dev-only counter `diag.gleam_over_cap` as a Filter-side defect. The amplitude is otherwise passed through unscaled. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Example:** `k` 1: ceil(2 / 1) = 2 art px = 2 screen px. `k` 3: ceil(2/3) = 1 art px = 3 screen px. Card at `H` 3: 3 screen px. A GLIDE lie at `AMP_FLOOR_PM` 400: gleam alpha 0.4, `peak_channel` = 0.4 x 0.902 = 0.361 <= 0.85. A 1000 pm amplitude: `a_draw` = 0.942, `peak_channel` = 0.850, and `diag.gleam_over_cap` increments. The zoom-dependent value stays inside the Filter's range 1-2 and needs the Filter to accept it (OQ-IW-4).

### D.10 Reduced-motion substitutes (`rm_substitute`)

The reduced-motion substitute formula is defined as:

`drawn_pm_rm = displayed_pm` (a settle becomes a step)

`notch_on = reduced_motion AND stress_predicate AND (motion_mode == HONEST)`

Glide, wander and lying-tick scaling are the Filter's `RM_GLIDE_SCALE` 0.6, the rate floor never below 0.3 deg/s, wander never scaled, the lying tick never below `LIE_TICK_MS` (PF-39, cited, not restated).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `reduced_motion` | bool | | Accessibility Settings (provisional — Accessibility Settings undesigned) | the player's setting |
| `stress_predicate` | bool | | IW-12 | the physical stress cause |
| `NOTCH_PAIR_PX` | int art px | 2 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 2-3 | this GDD | the size of each nick (world, Void Steel) or square (card, Hull Grey); raised to `ceil(NOTCH_MIN_SCREEN_PX / k)` so that it is at least 4 screen px (`NOTCH_MIN_SCREEN_PX` 4) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** the notch pair is shown or hidden; no animation. **Example:** `inst_engine_bus_load` under an OVERLOAD descriptor on a cable of the power loop with reduced motion on: no tremor; two Void Steel nicks sit in the bezel's top edge while the descriptor holds; the needle steps to round(1100 x 1000 / 1250) = 880 pm at once. If a FALSE_OK lie then starts on the same gauge (possible only for a seat with a usable scanner, and only if the scanner reads `load`, IW-29, OQ-IW-43) `notch_on` is false in that frame and the Filter's scaled glide and tick carry the lie *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

### D.11 Linear instruments (`linear_position`)

The linear position formula is defined as:

`y(pm) = round( travel_px * pm / 1000 )` (meniscus row from the bottom stop)

`D_vis_pm = 1000 * D_VIS_MIN_PX / travel_px`; `r_floor_pm_s = 1000 * LINEAR_GLIDE_RATE_MIN_PXS / travel_px`; `D_draw_min_px = (D_DRAW_MIN_DEG / arc_deg_ref) * travel_px`; eligible iff `travel_px >= TRAVEL_MIN_PX`

The Filter's angle maths has no linear branch (K-5), so the linear glide band is stated in pixels; this is a request to the Filter, not a new Filter formula.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `travel_px` | int art px | 50 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (one size at MVP), minimum 40 | calibration record | meniscus travel |
| `D_VIS_MIN_PX` | float art px | 4 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 3-6 | this GDD (request to the Filter) | smallest visible departure, derived from the dial (12 degrees x 24 px = 5.0 px, x 18 px = 3.8 px) |
| `LINEAR_GLIDE_RATE_MIN_PXS` | float px/s | 0.5 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 0.5-1.0 | this GDD (request to the Filter) | the dial's 0.5 px/s tip-speed rationale (PF D.2) applied to a meniscus |
| `TRAVEL_MIN_PX` | int art px | 40 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 32-64 | this GDD | below this a travel lie is ineligible (the instrument keeps its gleam and card-numeral modes) |
| `D_DRAW_MIN_DEG`, `arc_deg_ref` | float deg | 24 (Filter `[PLACEHOLDER]`), 240 | PF D.2, cited | the Filter's smallest departure as a fraction (0.10) of the arc, applied to the travel |

**Output Range:** meniscus row 0..`travel_px`; at `travel_px` 50, 1 px = 20 permille = the LS-20 store quantum, so a store crossing moves the meniscus exactly one row. **Example:** `inst_res_main_level`, stock 620 permille: `y` = round(50 x 620 / 1000) = 31 px. `D_vis_pm` = 1000 x 4 / 50 = 80 pm; `r_floor_pm_s` = 1000 x 0.5 / 50 = 10 pm/s, so a floor-rate glide crosses the 4 px in 8 s; `D_draw_min_px` = 0.10 x 50 = 5.0 px >= `D_VIS_MIN_PX` 4; the largest departure (0.4 of the travel) is 20 px, 40 s at the floor rate. A dial's 8.3 pm/s top rate (2 degrees/s) would move the meniscus only 0.42 px/s (and 0.25 px/s at the 1.19 deg/s floor), below the never-rest rationale, which is why the band is stated in pixels. At 24 px of travel a 10 % departure would be 2.4 px, inside the ±2 px wander; at the 40 px minimum it is 4 px, equal to `D_VIS_MIN_PX`, which is why `TRAVEL_MIN_PX` is 40. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Reachability after the L-2I correction:** the three LINEAR instruments are lie-eligible only through the scanner credit (IW-29: a single-instrument loop has no fixed partner), so the linear glide band, AC-IW-105 and the request to the Filter stay in force and are exercised for a seat with a usable scanner; for a seat that cannot use the scanner the three are TRUTH-default.

### D.12 The `lsb` visibility lint (`lsb_visibility`)

The `lsb` visibility formula is defined as:

`lsb <= (span / arc_deg) * rate_floor * (T_VIS_MAX_S - T_tick(N_r_max))`, i.e. `T_digit + T_tick(N_r_max) <= T_VIS_MAX_S` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, `rate_floor = max(GLIDE_RATE_MIN_DEG, 28.6 / needle_len_px)` (PF D.2); for a LINEAR instrument `lsb <= r_floor_pm_s * (T_VIS_MAX_S - T_tick(N_r_max))` (D.11)

`T_digit = lsb / ((span / arc_deg) * rate_floor)` (the interval at which the digit changes during a floor-rate glide)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `span` | int | `value_hi - value_lo` | calibration record (IW-9) | the gauge span in the quantity's unit |
| `arc_deg` | int deg | 240 | IW-7 | dial sweep |
| `rate_floor` | float deg/s | 1.19 (L 24), 1.59 (S 18) | PF D.2, cited | the Filter's floor glide rate |
| `T_VIS_MAX_S` | float s | 3.2 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (was 2.0), range 1.9-4 | this GDD | the longest a lying digit may stay unchanged before the numeral looks stale and the tri-modal rule loses a mode on the card. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: at 1 s the old range failed four quantities (`P` 1.83 s, `T` 1.34 s, `load` 1.61 s, battery `stock` 1.51 s), so the lower bound is 1.9 s; and the lint ignored the lying-tick period, because a digit can only change on a `T_tick` instant. The lint now adds `T_tick(N_r_max)` with `N_r_max` 4 = 1336 ms, which the default 2.0 s could not hold for any quantity but `p_o2`; the default is 3.2 s.)* |
| `T_VIS_MAX_RM_S` | float s | 4.5 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, range 4.0-5.0 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | this GDD | the same bound evaluated at the worst case over reduced motion: with `RM_GLIDE_SCALE` 0.6 (PF-39) the floor glide is 0.6 times as fast, so `T_digit` grows by 1/0.6 = 1.67 while `T_tick` is unchanged (the lying tick is never below `LIE_TICK_MS`) |

**Output Range:** the lint passes iff `T_digit + T_tick(N_r_max) <= T_VIS_MAX_S` and `T_digit / RM_GLIDE_SCALE + T_tick(N_r_max) <= T_VIS_MAX_RM_S` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (the `T_digit` column below is the first term; the sums with `T_tick` 1.336 s follow the table). **Example (this GDD's quantity ids):**

| Quantity | Units per degree | Max `lsb` (L / S) at `T_VIS_MAX_S` 3.2 s less `T_tick(4)` 1.336 s *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | Chosen `lsb` | `T_digit` at the floor (L / S) | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `p_o2` | 1.25 dkPa | 2.77 / 3.71 dkPa | 1 dkPa (one decimal of kPa) | 0.67 s / 0.50 s | pass |
| `P` | 4.58 dkPa | 10.2 / 13.6 dkPa | 10 dkPa (whole kPa) | 1.83 s / 1.37 s | pass |
| `T` | 625 mC | 1386 mC (L only) | 1000 mC (whole C) | 1.34 s | pass |
| `load` | 5.21 permille | 11.6 permille (L) | 10 permille (1 %) | 1.61 s | pass |
| `stock` (DIAL_S battery) | 4.17 permille | 12.4 permille (S) | 10 permille (1 %) | 1.51 s | pass |
| `stock` (LINEAR, `travel_px` 50) | 20 pm per px | 18.6 pm | 10 permille | 1.0 s | pass |

A whole-kPa `p_o2` (`lsb` 10 dkPa) would change its digit every 10 / (1.25 x 1.19) = 6.7 s, over twice the limit, which is why `p_o2` is shown with one decimal. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Combined lint at `N_r_max` 4 (`T_tick` 1336 ms), against `T_VIS_MAX_S` 3.2 s:** `p_o2` 0.67 + 1.34 = 2.01 s (DIAL_L) and 0.50 + 1.34 = 1.84 s (DIAL_S), pass; `P` 1.83 + 1.34 = 3.17 s, pass (0.03 s to spare, so `P` is the quantity that sets the default); `T` 1.34 + 1.34 = 2.68 s, pass; `load` 1.61 + 1.34 = 2.95 s, pass; battery `stock` 1.51 + 1.34 = 2.85 s, pass; LINEAR `stock` 1.0 + 1.34 = 2.34 s, pass. At `N_r` 1 (`T_tick` 400 ms) every sum is at most 2.23 s. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Worst case over reduced motion (`RM_GLIDE_SCALE` 0.6, `T_digit` / 0.6 + 1.34 s):** `p_o2` 1.12 + 1.34 = 2.46 s (L) and 0.83 + 1.34 = 2.17 s (S); `P` 3.05 + 1.34 = **4.39 s**; `T` 2.23 + 1.34 = 3.57 s; `load` 2.68 + 1.34 = 4.02 s; battery `stock` 2.52 + 1.34 = 3.86 s; LINEAR `stock` 1.67 + 1.34 = 3.01 s. Four of seven exceed the default `T_VIS_MAX_S` 3.2 s, so the lint is **evaluated twice**: at scale 1 against `T_VIS_MAX_S` 3.2 s and at scale 0.6 against `T_VIS_MAX_RM_S` 4.5 s (all seven pass, `P` with 0.11 s to spare). The difference is stated and accepted, not hidden: under reduced motion a lying digit may stay unchanged for up to about 4.4 s at the floor rate and so can look stale; that costs the card numeral's contribution to the motion tell for a player who asked for less motion, and the lie stays catchable because the needle's never-rest wander (never scaled, PF-39), the luminance gleam and the sound are untouched (the "two of three tells" rule, IW-27, AC-IW-125). **Honest and lying digit granularity** (P4) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2)*: the honest numeral is formatted at the same `lsb` as the lying one, but its value moves only in the Graph's LS-20 quanta (`p_o2` 5 dkPa, `P` 10 dkPa, `T` 1000 mC, `stock` 20 permille) while a lying numeral ticks in single `lsb` steps on every `T_tick`. **Step size and cadence are therefore a deliberate honest-versus-lie tell** (an honest digit jumps a quantum after a real crossing and rests; a lying digit moves one `lsb` at a time and never rests): the lie leaks through its own step size and cadence, which is on-pillar (the HR-5 tri-modal leak, the motion bit on the readout) and not an accidental oracle. The creative-director ruled a wording fix plus native-unit carriage, not finer Graph quanta; the earlier claim that digit granularity is no tell at all was false and is withdrawn.

## Edge Cases

*Every outcome below is the exact behaviour of the instrument presenters and the card (presentation only); the neighbour that owns the surrounding rule is named where the outcome depends on it. Ids are `E-IW-n`. "Diagnostics" names a counter the Debug Overlay may read from the dev-only probe of IW-38. Where an outcome depends on a provisional neighbour it is marked.*

**Zero, maximum and out-of-range values**

- **If `displayed_pm` arrives outside 0..1000** (a Filter or calibration defect): it is clamped to the end stop, `diag.instr_pm_clamped` increments, and nothing is shown to the player. The needle never leaves its arc and the card numeral shows the clamped bound. (E-IW-1)
- **If the true value is beyond the gauge span** (`T` below -60 C or above 90 C, a vent below 0 dkPa; `load_pm` is clamped to its span by the Graph, so it never reaches this case, E-IW-4): the needle pegs on the end stop, the numeral shows the clamped bound with no "<" or ">" mark, and the format never shows more precision than `lsb`. A "<" would be a second reading with no analogue on the wall. (E-IW-2)
- **If a displayed temperature rounds to zero** (-300 mC): the integer formatter yields 0 and the card shows "0", never "-0"; the integer has no sign. The vector `floor_div` helper is explicit because GDScript integer division truncates toward zero `[UNV]`. (E-IW-3)
- **If the bus is dead, idle or overloaded** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the Graph's `load_pm` (IW-9) is 0 when `supply` is 0 and `min(1250, floor(1000 x demand / supply))` otherwise. A dead bus (`supply` 0, `demand` > 0) rests at the low stop with no housing pip and the card shows "0"; an idle bus (`supply` > 0, `demand` 0) rests at the low stop with the pip lit; an overloaded bus (`demand` above `supply`) climbs into the NOTCHED zone and pegs at the 1250 stop, where the card shows "125". The clamp is the publisher's, so `diag.instr_pm_clamped` does not rise. A dead bus no longer reads as an overload: the player is told that nothing is carried, and the fix is supply. (E-IW-4)
- **If a store is empty or full** (`stock` 0 or 1000 permille): the meniscus sits on row 0 or row `travel_px`, or the DIAL_S needle on a stop; a dead tank still shows its stock because stock is not flow (IW-20), and a settle or tremor that would pass a stop is held by the stop pin. (E-IW-5)
- **If a settle's overshoot would carry the needle past an end stop**: the drawn value is clamped to 0 or 1000, so the overshoot is truncated rather than displayed beyond the scale; the settle still ends exactly on `to_pm`. (E-IW-6)

**Simultaneous and overlapping rules**

- **If a new honest sample arrives while a one-step ramp is mid-flight**: `prev_honest_pm` is set to the value currently drawn and a fresh ramp runs from there to the new `displayed_pm`; the needle never jumps back to the older sample, and no settle fires unless the sample-to-sample change meets the jump test. (E-IW-7)
- **If two or more quanta are crossed inside one Graph step** (a burst breach): the Filter's honest memo is invalidated once per step, so there is one sample with the larger delta. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* What counts is the change accumulated since the instrument left rest, compared with `INSTR_SETTLE_JUMP_PM` 40 inside the 400 ms window (IW-11 (b)): two `p_o2` quanta (33 pm) ramp; three (50 pm) from rest start a settle, which is the intended reading of a burst; a sustained fast vent that does not reach 40 pm inside its first window is a sweep and never settles until rest (300 ms without a sample) is re-entered. (E-IW-8)
- **If an honest value hovers on a quantum edge** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the Filter's buckets have no hysteresis, so the memo can flip between two adjacent buckets every Graph step. The reversal dwell (IW-11 (b)) holds each one-quantum reversal for `INSTR_REVERSAL_DWELL_STEPS` 4 steps: the needle makes at most the first move and then stays, with no tremor-like flips and no stress descriptor. A real reversal that persists is followed after 400 ms. (E-IW-59)
- **If a settle is active and the honest value changes again**: the triple is re-based (`from_pm` := the value now drawn, `to_pm` := the new value, `t_jump` := now); velocity is discarded, one triple suffices, and nothing accumulates. A small kink is accepted. (E-IW-9)
- **If a settle or ramp and an honest tremor are active together** (damage arrives with stress): the drawn position is the settle or ramp base plus the tremor offset of D.5; both are honest motions and they add. Tremor is clamped to +-`A_eff` and the sum is clamped at the stops. (E-IW-10)
- **If a bound node is patched or WORN (`sev` 1, mode WORN, no hazard mode)**: no tremor, because predicate (i) counts hazard-mode descriptors only; IW-18 shows its wear. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* A patched subject also carries a **PATCH_WEEP** (id 27) descriptor: it is not a hazard mode of the (i) list, so it never trembles either, and the patch plate is drawn from the node's patched state (`hp_cap < HP_FULL`, the Graph's own row 27 condition), not from the presence of a WORN descriptor, so a patched subject whose WORN is superseded by a live hazard mode keeps its plate. (E-IW-11)
- **If an OVERLOAD descriptor is active on a cable of the bound loop component in a section the player is not in**: a bus-load dial anywhere on that loop trembles, because the dial reads the loop's load and the predicate is read through `failures(filter)`; tremor stops when the descriptor clears. The tremor is the consequence of a real overload, not a tell Attention drives. (E-IW-12)
- **If both the bound node's own hazard-mode descriptor (predicate i) and a cable overload (predicate ii) hold**: the predicate is their OR and there is one tremor with one envelope; stress sources are never summed into a larger amplitude. (E-IW-13)
- **If `alarm_eligible` becomes true on a bound subject whose housing is a DIAL_S**: no mark is drawn (DIAL_S carries none, IW-19); the consequence channel (hiss, draught, sweep) is that instrument's alarm. The absence of a mark never proves a lie. (E-IW-14)

**Card lifecycle and the lit gate**

- **If `lit(ref)` goes false while the card is open**: the card closes in the same render frame (at most one tick after the light goes), the face goes dark by the renderer, and reopening needs a new press, accepted at once (only the plate's redraw is debounced, IW-21). (E-IW-15)
- **If `inspect` is pressed in the first ticks after focus lands, or straight after a close** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: it is accepted at once, because the debounce is a prompt-drawing delay and never an input lockout (IW-21); the card opens on the next render frame and no cooldown follows a close (E-IW-52). Frame-stepped vectors: a press at tick 4 after a lit focus lands and a press at tick 4 after a close both open the card (AC-IW-91). (E-IW-16)
- **If `lit(ref)` oscillates at the rim of the beam with the card open**: the card closes once on the first false and stays closed (no strobe, HR-13); the face is never shown or hidden by code, so the 150/120 hysteresis cannot flash it. A card cannot reopen without a press. (E-IW-17)
- **If two instruments are in reach and `inspect` is pressed**: it acts only on the focused INSTRUMENT (one focus model, PC-6); an unlit instrument is not focusable and an unfocused one is not a target. (E-IW-18)
- **If the focus moves to another instrument while the card is open**: the card stays on its latched ref; there is no navigation between instruments at MVP, and `inspect`, `cancel` or B closes it. (E-IW-19)
- **If the bound node becomes DESTROYED, SALVAGED or ABSENT while the card is open**: the card closes that frame and the wall node shows the DEAD frame (hairline crack, 4-px needle stub), with no prompt, card, gleam, tremor or pip. A gauge that vanished would read as a bug and one that kept reading would assert a reading from a destroyed thing. (E-IW-20)
- **If the section unloads or the player transits with the card open, or the kernel restores or fires `hard_start`**: the card closes; every presentation scalar is reset; the needle shows `displayed_pm` from the first frame with no settle or ramp. (E-IW-21)
- **If a teammate's beam keeps `lit(ref)` true but the local player leaves reach**: the card closes on the local seat's reach loss (reach is the local player's), even though lit is world state. (E-IW-22)
- **If `lit` turns on while the Adapter context is `chart_table` or `chat`**: no card opens; a card opens only from `gameplay` on a press. (E-IW-23)
- **If `inspect` is pressed on a focused ref that has no instrument record**: the press is consumed silently, `diag.inspect_unresolved` increments, and no error text or sound is produced. (E-IW-24)
- **If the game is PAUSED with a settle in flight or a card open**: `present_time` freezes, the needle, tremor and digits freeze bit-for-bit, an open card is closed by the pause (a context change, E-IW-60) and stays closed on resume, and resuming shows no jump (a tween would keep running, which is why no tween is used) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2)*. (E-IW-25)
- **If the window is resized, `k` or `H` changes, or a zoom cut occurs with the card open**: every placement is re-projected, the card side is re-chosen by a cut (never a slide), the card stays open, no settle fires and the numeral scheduler timestamp is reset. (E-IW-26)
- **If the camera drifts under look-ahead with the card open**: the plate follows `world_to_hud(anchor_px)` per frame on the HUD grid, and its side is re-chosen only if the latched rect fails the safe-rect, footprint or chest-disc test. (E-IW-27)
- **If the label is wider than the plate's text width** (a long locale string): it wraps to a second row, the plate grows by 7 art px, `H_card` is recomputed against the taller plate (it may fall by one step on a small display), and the label is never truncated; two truncated labels could become the same string and break the second-opinion check. (E-IW-28)
- **If a movement input arrives while the card is open** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: movement intent past `MOVE_DEADZONE` (plus `CARD_PAD_DEADZONE_MARGIN` for a gamepad stick) closes the card and the avatar starts moving in the same frame; no move press is swallowed (a stick inside the dead-zone does nothing, and aim stays live); a direction held at the moment of entry is latched and does not close the card until released or changed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The first step is never eaten while a teammate's friendly fire or a breach is in progress. Ratified 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions, D94); the Controller's context matrix carries the amendment (OQ-IW-17) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. (E-IW-29)
- **If a breach starts or a teammate hallucinated as a horror attacks while the card is open**: the sim keeps ticking, the HUD critical strip stays live and the consequence channel is felt; the card is not a pause and offers no shelter, and closing it costs one press, or the first step, at no other cost. (E-IW-30)
- **If the context changes while the card is open** (pause or `chart_table`, `chat` in VS-B, or a `loco_state` change to STAGGERED, DOWNED, DEAD or IN_POD) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the card closes in the same render frame and is not re-opened when the context returns to `gameplay`; a new press is needed. In co-op the overlay does not pause the sim, so the card never stays drawn under a modal. Prompts that name the close key quote the real binding, `cancel` = X (B on a pad). (E-IW-60)
- **If a valve or repair target holds the primary focus and a lit gauge on the same panel is in reach and arc** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `inspect` opens the gauge's card from the secondary slot, `interact` acts on the valve or repair, and the plate lists both verbs; with no lit gauge in reach the plate shows the primary verb only. Keyboard: R inspects, E interacts; gamepad: RT inspects, A interacts. Provisional on PC-6 and IA-2 (OQ-IW-39, AC-IW-113). (E-IW-61)
- **If every card candidate is blocked at the largest `H_card`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `H_card` steps down to `HUD_MIN_SCALE` before any soft overlap is accepted, the clamped and slid rectangle is the one tested, and the plate never covers the instrument (D.6 fixture: 720p, `H` 6, `k` 4). A witness, teammate or lit interactable inside the candidate counts as a violation like the chest disc. (E-IW-62)

**Hallucination interplay**

- **If a lie starts while the card is open**: the card gains the gleam and the motion mode in the same frame as the wall gauge, from the same `Perceived`; there is no flash of the true value and no new sound from the card (the sound is the Filter's request on the ref). (E-IW-31)
- **If a lie is active, the card is closed and then reopened**: the card shows the same `Perceived`, the same gleam phase and the same motion mode, plays no new lie sound, and never replays a settle or sweeps the needle in from zero. (E-IW-32)
- **If the Filter flips HONEST to GLIDE, TICK, STALE or RETURN mid-settle**: the triple, `prev_honest_pm`, the tremor and the notch are cleared that frame; the needle draws `displayed_pm` verbatim and snaps by at most the settle's current deviation (the lie already carries its gleam floor and sound from its first frame, and the first lie is on a fault-free subject at rest). (E-IW-33)
- **If the numeral is in STALE mode** (held digits): the card numeral holds its digits while the needle keeps drawing the Filter's trajectory, wander included; no lying needle rests (checkpoint ruling C2), so the wall gauge keeps its motion mode while the card is closed and a held numeral never reads as an honest gauge at rest. (E-IW-34)
- **If the stress predicate holds while the reading is lying**: no tremor and no notch are drawn; the lie carries its own gleam, never-rest motion and sound (HR-5). The absence of a tremor is therefore not an oracle, because the lie is separately detectable by all three modes. (E-IW-35)
- **If a lie ends** (RETURN to HONEST at zero discrepancy): the first HONEST frame has no jump, so no settle; `prev_honest_pm` is set to the returned `displayed_pm`; the gleam eases to 0 with `leak_amplitude_pm`; nothing announces the end (SN-16). If the Filter ever returned HONEST with a non-zero discrepancy the frame would snap (no settle, which would itself be an oracle). (E-IW-36)
- **If a lying needle sits in an alarm zone with no alarm mark, or an alarm mark shows beside a needle in a normal zone**: the rule is one-directional. The mark's **absence is never evidence**: a zone's onset (an LS-7 band edge) and `alarm_eligible` (`sev` 3 or more, the fairness gate open) are different quantities, the descriptor may be absent for reasons the player cannot see (fairness gate, DIAL_S carries no mark, gauges with `hops` >= 1 between mount and subject carry none), and so no needle may be called honest or lying from a missing mark. The mark's **presence is the ship speaking and outranks the needle** (P1 design test: the pipe): it is a TRUTH-graded untagged node that no HOUSING_GLOW or MACHINE_FRAME lie can add or suppress (SN L-A), so a lit mark beside a needle in a normal zone means the subject is in alarm and the needle is the thing that is wrong. By IW-19 the mark can appear only where the alarm-eligible subject physically is, so believing it still costs a walk. (E-IW-37)
- **If a stage-3 SPRITE_SWAP shows `cracked` on a machine that carries a WORN patch plate**: the patch plate (TRUTH) stays drawn and the swap draws the cracked body; this is a content lint case for Derelict Authoring (`PATCH_OVER_OVERRIDE`), not a runtime branch. (E-IW-38)
- **If a housing pip is lying** (a stage-2 HOUSING_GLOW lie shows a pip on a loop with `served_pm` 0, or hides it on a running loop): the pip is still a non-readable find-aid; it makes no face readable and no `lit(ref)` true, and finding a gauge by its pip is not proof its loop runs. (E-IW-39)

**Cull, camera, restore and data**

- **If an instrument was off-screen or in an unloaded section when the value changed**: its first pull snaps to `displayed_pm` with no settle and no ramp (`prev_honest_pm` is unset); a settle on re-entry would be a motion with no world cause. (E-IW-40)
- **If the kernel restores while a tremor predicate still holds**: the tremor restarts from its onset ramp (`TREMOR_ONSET_MS`), because the presentation envelope is reset by `hard_start`; the player may see the shake begin again, which is true to the cause. (E-IW-41)
- **If a lie is active on an instrument in a section that unloads and later reloads**: the instrument draws nothing while unloaded; on reload its first pull shows the current `Perceived`, because the lie belongs to Sanity and the Filter, not to the node. (E-IW-42)
- **If the calibration record is missing or malformed**: the honest path plus `lie_unrenderable` (PF-44) applies at runtime and the build fails for any kind listed in `lie_targets.json`. A missing record can only make a lie vanish, never make the truth break. (E-IW-43)
- **If a record names `arc_deg` other than 240 or `needle_len_px` below `NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX` 16**: the build lint fails; there is one dial family and a shorter needle is glide-ineligible. (E-IW-44)
- **If a zone would be narrower than `ZONE_MIN_ARC_PX` 6 on the chosen size** (for example `T` on DIAL_S: the COOL zone is 5.3 px): the build fails; the instrument must use DIAL_L or merge bands in data. (E-IW-45)
- **If two instruments share one `(subject, quantity)` pair** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the `INSTR_QUANTITY_COLLISION` lint fails the build (two such gauges would share one ref and one lie, so neither is a second opinion). Instruments of different quantities in one room (`inst_cryo_bay_p_o2` and `inst_cryo_bay_T`) are distinct refs and both lie-eligible, because the Filter adopted the `quantity` term (PF-9) and the Graph published the `true_value` kinds (LS-19 additions); the former one-instrument-per-room fallback is retired. (E-IW-46)
- **If a LINEAR instrument has `travel_px` below `TRAVEL_MIN_PX` 40**: it is ineligible for travel lies and keeps its gleam and card-numeral modes. (E-IW-47)
- **If `PlayerId` is unresolved** (a seat joining): the instrument draws the honest path for that frame; every seat's memo is keyed by its own `PlayerId`. (E-IW-48)
- **If the number of loaded instruments exceeds `INSTRUMENT_SHARE_MAX` 24**: the content lint fails; the runtime registers every INSTRUMENT subject that the manifest declares and never drops one. (E-IW-49)
- **If the composited gleam's peak channel would exceed `LEAK_MAX_CH` 0.85** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: Instruments clamp the drawn alpha to 0.942 (D.9) so the cap holds, and `diag.gleam_over_cap` increments as a Filter-side defect; the amplitude is otherwise never rescaled. (E-IW-50)
- **If a locale needs glyphs the bitmap instrument face lacks** (CJK, Cyrillic, Arabic right-to-left) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: that element (the label or the unit) falls back to the default-locale string and then to the part-number stencil, while the numeral and the dial or glass render as usual; the card is never removed, and a fallback face remains the real fix (the vector reading face is barred on instruments by ART 7.1, OQ-IW-14). (E-IW-51)

**Degenerate strategies**

- **If a player opens the card on every gauge every time as a free safety check**: it stays no safer than the wall, because the card lies exactly when the gauge lies and calls no `record_falsification`; the cost is standing still and keeping the gauge lit (P3). No cooldown is added, because an unexplained rule would be a lie about the world; inspect spam is a playtest signal that card parity is not reading through. (E-IW-52)
- **If a player scans every instrument to find the lie ("scan everything")**: each scan costs light and time, the lie-eligible surfaces per site are bounded (at least 3 for a usable-scanner seat, no decorative gauge, IW-29), and the only falsifiers are a witness, a different instrument or a crewmate; the strategy is bounded by exposure, not forbidden (SN D.12). (E-IW-53)
- **If a player reads the absence of a tremor, the absence of an alarm mark, or the presence of a pip as proof of a lie or of a running loop**: none of those is an oracle on its own (E-IW-35, E-IW-37, E-IW-39); the alarm mark's presence is the one exception and is a truth, not a lie detector (it says the subject beside it is in alarm; it says nothing about any remote needle, E-IW-37); the only mandatory lie evidence is the Filter's leak (gleam and motion mode), and the world remains the final witness. (E-IW-54)
- **If a player zooms out to `k` 1 to hide or to survey every gauge**: nothing about correctness changes; the world dial is legible by pattern and the needle (D.7), the gleam is widened to 2 screen px, the card is zoom-independent, and a dial at the largest field is the design's accepted weakest point. (E-IW-55)
- **If a player reads a gauge by a thrown Chemlight glowstick or a fixture instead of the beam**: the face is readable and the card openable exactly as under the beam (`lit(ref)` is world light); under the Chemlight the gleam's hue shifts toward blue, so the hue mode may fail while the motion mode and sound remain (the compositing requirement of IW-16 is the remedy). (E-IW-56)

**Co-op and seat-local hooks (later tiers)**

- **If two players look at the same gauge and only one is hallucinating**: each sees the Perceived keyed to their own `PlayerId`; one needle glides, the other rests, and each seat's card equals its own wall gauge. The other player's body and voice are the honest cross-check; no instrument reads a crewmate. (E-IW-57)
- **If two seats' beams light the same gauge**: `lit(ref)` is world state and stays true for both; each seat's card opens and closes by its own press, reach and the shared lit bit, and a card is never visible to another seat or replicated. (E-IW-58)

## Dependencies

Status vocabulary as in Interactions: **designed** (header Status Designed), **designed in this run** (written in this orchestrated run), **undesigned-provisional** (no file; this GDD states a request and re-checks it when the neighbour lands). Glob of `design/gdd/` at authoring (2026-10-27): 27 files (28 at the re-review re-count: the 26 MVP GDDs plus `game-concept.md` and `systems-index.md`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. "Index edge" says whether `systems-index.md` lists the edge (IDX:94, :200 list exactly Life-Support Graph, 2D Lighting & Occlusion, Perception Filter and Interaction System as upstream). This GDD edits no index row (advisor ruling R26); missing edges are flagged for the producer.

### Upstream (Instruments depend on them)

| System | Direction | Hard / Soft | Interface (what is taken) | Index edge | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Command & Simulation Layer | upstream | Hard | `PRESENTATION` subscriber class; `tick_alpha`; the restore event; notification = dirty mark (`hard_start` is the Filter's term, PF-33, and `present_time` is the Pipeline's, PR-9) | transitive | designed (locked) |
| Life-Support Graph | upstream | Hard | `read_view` for non-value inputs only (`compartment` for binding existence only, `node`, `edge`, `loop_component`, `failures`, `nodes_in`, `neighbors`, `section_of`, `stress_active`); LS-7 band names at build time; the closed consequence-channel list; registry formula `atmosphere_derive` | yes | designed (locked) |
| 2D Lighting & Occlusion | upstream | Hard | lit-surface result, HullSurface, the emissive layer (mask bit 2), the five-`ShaderMaterial` budget | yes | designed in this run |
| Perception Filter | upstream | Hard | `perceived`, `Perceived`, `PerceptionRef`, the PF-45 calibration contract, the leak phase and amplitude, `hard_start` (PF-33) | yes | designed in this run; PF-7, PF-9, PF-18, PF-45 and the PF I-B row are provisional until this GDD lands (OQ-PF-29 re-check) |
| Interaction System | upstream | Hard | `INSTRUMENT` subjects, `verb_inspect`, the `inspect` permission rule, focus and prompt rules | yes | designed in this run; IA:831 records the edge as Soft (refresh owed) |
| Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query | upstream | Hard | `lit(ref)`, `LIT_ON_PM`, `LIT_OFF_PM` | **no: add edge** (EM:339 names it) | designed |

### Flagged, not edited

1. **Missing index edges** for the producer: Emitter State, Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline, Player Controller & Input, Sanity (upstream), Failure & Trail (soft pair). (K-11; advisor ruling R26.)
2. **The unowned "module presenter" row**: no index row names the Module-Body Presenter (IW-17); this GDD owns it under the scope name "module-body reading surfaces" (K-11).
3. **Stale art-bible lines** (the art-director's): ART 7.0 and 2.5 pod dash (WM AB-2 withdrew it, K-10); ART 7.0 self-emission "lantern" wording (K-6); ART 2.7 "a needle that drifts on a real gauge" (AT-17). Listed in Cross-References X-3.
4. **No circular dependency** touches this system. The soft cycle Sanity <-> Perception Filter is one-way for Instruments: Sanity writes perception state, the Filter draws, Instruments ask the Filter. The card-context signal runs Adapter -> Instruments only; Instruments never call back.

## Tuning Knobs

Every knob here is data in `assets/data/instruments/` or a build/lint constant, never hardcoded (coding standards: gameplay values are data-driven). Every owned value is a `[PLACEHOLDER]` registry candidate except `NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX`, whose value is fixed by the Filter's relation (the eligibility floor 14.3 = 28.6 / `GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG`) and which this GDD owns. ★ marks a registry candidate that another GDD consumes (a cross-system fact); unmarked knobs are local tuning, listed here and in Cross-References X-5 and registered only if a neighbour begins to cite them. Safe ranges are tied to a derivation where one exists, because a few of the specialist ranges were wider than the geometry allows (the upper needle lengths are cell-bound: DIAL_L housing height is 1.5 x `needle_len_px` + 10 and must fit 48 rows, so `needle_len_px` <= 25; DIAL_S housing width is 2 x `needle_len_px` + 10 and must fit 48 columns, so `needle_len_px` <= 19; the three settle parameters are only safe together: the joint lint of D.4 keeps `Mp` at most 5 %, the 2 % settling time `4 / (zeta x omega)` inside 250-400 ms and the end snap at most 1 px).

**Owned knobs**

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ★ `NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX` | 16 | art px | 14.3-32 | fewer instruments eligible for needle glide; above 18 excludes DIAL_S, above 24 excludes DIAL_L | below 14.3 no needle can glide at 0.5 px/s within `GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG` 2.0 (28.6 / 2.0); a dial at exactly 16 has a 32-px sweep on the helmet-scale boundary (R25) | `GLIDE_RATE_BAND`, PF D.2 `rate_floor` |
| ★ `DIAL_L_NEEDLE_PX` | 24 | art px | 20-25 | above 25 the housing (1.5 L + 10) exceeds the 48-row cell | below 20 the glide `rate_floor` (28.6 / L) rises toward the 2.0 cap and the face loses legibility | cell 96x48, IW-7 arithmetic, D.2 `pm_min` |
| ★ `DIAL_S_NEEDLE_PX` | 18 | art px | 17-19 | above 19 the housing (2 L + 10) exceeds the 48-column cell | at 16 the sweep sits on the helmet boundary; below 14.3 glide-ineligible | cell 48x48, D.2 `pm_min` 75.4 |
| ★ `DIAL_ARC_DEG` | 240 | deg | 200-270 (a build lint rejects any value but the one in use) | above 240 the end stops sit lower and the arc widens beyond the cell; resolution per pixel drops | below 240 the resolution per pixel and the zone arcs shrink | PF-45 `arc_deg`, D.1, D.2 |
