## Visual/Audio Requirements

*Source: the art-director's proposal (consulted for this section), reconciled with the advisor rulings (R1-R26) and the checkpoint rulings (C1-C17, X1-X14). Where the art-director's pre-checkpoint figures differ from the rules above, the rules win and the figure is corrected here (housings 60x46 and 46x35, not 58x45 and 46x36; outer radii 28 and 22, not 27 and 21; `CARD_MAG` 1, not 2; no loop glyph on the card, C17; card tremor never scaled by a card multiple, C8; no lying needle rests, X1). Citation prefixes as in Cross-References. Art bible sections are cited by number ("ART 7.3"). `[PLACEHOLDER]` marks this GDD's own unregistered numbers; `BR-n` is a bible refresh owed by the art-director (X-3); `[UNV]` marks an unverified engine behaviour; "-> ADR candidate" marks an implementation choice kept out of the requirement.*

**What this section is for.** Instruments draw the ship's own voice, so the look is the product: the same dial must read as a trustworthy, heavy, physical object when it is honest, and as a thin, light, slightly wrong one only through the three leak channels of HR-5 (hue, motion, sound), never through a different object. The housing, bezel, fitting plate, hub, tube and cap are physical geometry (ANCHOR) and never lie; only the reading (needle, meniscus, card numeral, state glyph, housing-glow frame, machine frame) may. The consequence channel beside an instrument (frost, drip, draught, hiss, trails, lamp brightness, the flow pattern on a pipe) is drawn by Lighting and Failure & Trail; this system adds, alters and removes nothing there (LSG:168). This section names every visual and audio requirement and classifies each as TRUTH, ANCHOR or corruptible; it does not restate the rules (Detailed Design) or the arithmetic (Formulas D.1-D.12).

### VA-1. Governing art-bible principles

| Principle / rule | Cite | What it forces on this system |
|---|---|---|
| The ship is an instrument; who produced the number decides where it is drawn | ART 7.0 table | Ship-produced readings are lit sprites on layer 0; only the card is screen space (layer 2); the housing is an ANCHOR, only the reading may lie |
| What You Light, You Can Read | ART 1 Principle 1; ART 7.0 rule (1) | Nothing here is self-readable in the dark: no emissive needle, face or digit; the face is dark until the beam, a fixture, a lantern pool or a thrown Chemlight glowstick (`#86B896`) reaches it |
| Inside the light, state lives in the sprite, not the icon | ART 1 Principle 2; ART 7.2; ART 8.9 line 12 | No status banner, state word or icon swap on a face. The two exceptions (state glyph, machine frames) are reading surfaces whose lie is a frame choice (BR-1) |
| Emission is exposure | ART 1 Principle 3 | The housing pip casts no light and exposes nothing (IW-4; LO-14); no instrument is an emitter and no gauge is a lantern (K-6) |
| When the game lies, the lie leaks | ART 1 corollary; ART 4.4 stage 1 | The gleam (fringe `#B07BE6`, never core `#8A3FD1`) sits on an edge of the corruptible element, never a fill, never baked |
| UI shape grammar: curves only for dial arcs and needles; chamfers elsewhere; frames solid, heavy, static; readings thin, light, moving | ART 3.4 | Dial = open 240-degree arc strip on a chamfered rectangle (K-7); card frame chamfered; every curve is an arc or a needle (plus one 4x4 octagonal hub) |
| Readability contract: loop by section alone with hue removed; silhouette at 25 % | ART 3.1 | Zone bands coded by pattern and luminance as well as hue; loop identity by fitting silhouette; no numerals on a world gauge |
| Hero versus supporting shapes | ART 3.5 | Instruments are supporting shapes (rectangular, static, low contrast); the needle is the only moving element and never out-shines the beam; the pip is capped below fixtures |
| Reserved composition: no round gauge over a square console at body height; round features under 5.3 or over 32 art px | ART 6.6 rule 2 (ART:714); ART 8.9 line 10 | Open arc, never a closed circle; arc outer diameters 56 and 44 pass; hub 4 passes (IW-7) |
| Pipe band stays clear (rows 29-58 of the 72 px face) | ART 6.3 rule 7; ART 8.2 | Terminal-fitting exception for DIAL_S; DIAL_L never on a pipe run (BR-2) |
| Three motions: settle, tremor, glide; honest = cause, mass, rest; lie = none of these | ART 7.3 | VA-6; settle and tremor only when `motion_mode` is HONEST |
| Typography: bitmap instrument face, tabular numerals, slashed zero; 7 and 5 art px caps; type on opaque Ground | ART 7.1; HR-2; HR-12 | Card numeral Paper White 7 px; label and unit Hull Grey 5 px, both on Ground only |
| Colour: sane palette only; Paper White UI-only; Alarm Red a mark never text; violet and flesh never in a sheet | ART 4.2, 4.4, 4.6; ART 8.9 line 4 | VA-3 |
| Colour-blind table: loop pairs backed by shape, never hue | ART 4.7 | Zone coding and loop identity never depend on hue |
| Asset standards: naming grammar, palette lock, sheet pairing, `ui_` never lit | ART 8.3, 8.5, 8.9 | VA-11 |
| HR-5 tri-modal lie; HR-8 reduced motion; HR-11 focus is a shape; HR-12 minimums; HR-13 3 Hz ceiling and no HUD shake; HR-14 mirror card | ART 7.7 | VA-6 to VA-8 |

### VA-2. Instrument sprite specifications (layer 0, lit)

Kinds map one to one to the data `gauge_kind` of IW-8. Priority: **P0** = MVP floor (dial, level sight-glass, card, alarm mark, honest WORN); **P1** = stage-2 surfaces and the dead frame; **P2** = stage-3 machine frames (cuttable, IW-34).

| Kind | World form | Card form | Channel | Priority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DIAL | open-arc dial on a chamfered housing, two sizes | the same dial at HUD art px, flat `ui_` export from the same source | corruptible (module-body) | P0 |
| LINEAR (tank sight-glass) | vertical glass tube, fluid column, meniscus bead | the same glass | corruptible (fill-level reading only) | P0 |
| Numeral | **none in the world** (ART 3.1; IW-6) | digit cells in a glass-edged well | the card numeral is `displayed_value` of the `.needle` ref (C2) | P0 (card only) |
| STATE GLYPH | 8x8 chamfered lens, two shape-coded frames, on a module housing | not carried | corruptible (stage 2) | P1 |
| HOUSING PIP | 4x2 emissive pip on the fitting plate | not carried | corruptible (stage 2, `HOUSING_GLOW`) | P1 |
| MACHINE FRAME | the module sheet's frames (`running`/`stopped`, `whole`/`cracked`) | not carried | corruptible (stage 3) | P2 (both frames must exist at MVP for any lie-capable module) |
| WORN overlay and patch plate | scuff overlay and patch plate on the module and its instrument | not carried | honest at stages 0-2; part of the machine frame at stage 3 | P0 |
| ALARM MARK | 16x16 Alarm Red mark on the housing | not carried | TRUTH, untagged | P0 |
| DEAD FRAME | hairline crack and 4-px needle stub | no card | TRUTH | P1 |
| STRESS NOTCH (reduced motion) | two Void Steel nicks in the bezel top edge, each `max(2, ceil(4 / k))` art px square so that it is at least 4 screen px at every rung (IW-27) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | two 2x2 Hull Grey squares on the Ground plate above the dial (2 HUD px is at least 6 screen px) | honest-only presentation of the IW-12 predicate | P1 |

#### VA-2.1 DIAL template family (IW-7, advisor rulings R6 and R25, checkpoint ruling C5)

One template, two authored sizes, `arc_deg` 240. Angles are measured from 12 o'clock; the arc runs -120 to +120 degrees clockwise; the open 120-degree sector is at the bottom.

| Part | DIAL_L (panel, console) | DIAL_S (wall, at a terminal fitting) | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| Needle `needle_len_px` | 24 | 18 | A separate pivoted sprite, 2 art px thick (`NEEDLE_STROKE_ART_PX`), Hull Grey; never `draw_line` (4.7 has no feather, IW-10); both at or above `NEEDLE_LEN_MIN_PX` 16 |
| Pivot hub | 4x4 octagonal (chamfered square) | same | Under 5.3 px, passes ART:714; static |
| Gap, needle tip to band | 1 px | 1 px | The tip never overlaps the band, so needle against band is never Hull Grey on Hull Grey |
| Zone band | inner r 25, 3 px, outer r 28 | inner r 19, 3 px, outer r 22 | Ticks at band ends and zone boundaries only; no numerals |
| Arc extents | 56 wide x 42 high | 44 wide x 33 high | 2 x outer r by 1.5 x outer r (IW-7 arithmetic) |
| Housing (chamfered rectangle) | 60x46, bezel 2 px | 46x35, bezel 1 px | The 1-px bezel is a minimum-feature exception (BR-9) |
| Cell | 96x48 prop cell | 48x48 prop cell | ART 8.2; margins 36 px horizontal and 2 px vertical (L), 2 px horizontal and 13 px vertical (S) |
| Loop fitting plate *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | 16x11 seated on the bottom bezel (IW-7 lower-sector arithmetic) | 12x8 seated on the bottom bezel | Ring clamp, square junction box, padded collar, bolted flange or rivet line (ART 3.4, 7.2); loop identity without hue (VA-2.7) |
| Arc outer diameter (helmet-scale test) | 56 | 44 | Both over 32 and so outside the 5.3-32 forbidden band (ART:714); a dial at exactly 16 is not an authored size |

**Face plate.** Graphite mid (the darkest large plane allowed, two Graphite values per plane, ART 8.9 line 9); bezel Graphite lit; ticks, bands and needle Hull Grey (thin elements only: Hull Grey is never a plane, ART 4.1); Void Steel 1-px gaps inside the bands. Contrast ladder (ART 4.5): needle L* 61 on face L* 20 = 41; band L* 61 on face = 41; bezel L* 43. No Tungsten, no Paper White, no Alarm Red and no loop hue anywhere on a world face.

**Zone coding (IW-15).** Three codes by pattern and luminance, never by hue (ART 3.1 hue-removed test; ART 4.7):

| Zone family | Authored pattern in the 3-px band | Mean luminance | Survives |
|---|---|---|---|
| Normal (`NORMAL`, `COMFORT`) | SOLID: three continuous rows | 100 % of Hull Grey | 25 % scale as a bright continuous arc |
| Caution (`THIN`, `LOW`, `COOL`, `HOT`, `FROST`) | HATCHED: 3x3 px dashes with 3 px gaps (period 6) | about 50 % | 25 % scale as a dotted arc |
| Critical (`HYPOXIC`, `ANOXIC`, `DECOMP`, `VACUUM`, `SCORCH`, `FREEZING`, `DEEP_COLD`; overload for `load`) | NOTCHED: a split rail, two 1-px rows with a continuous Void Steel centre row | about 67 %, visibly two lines | 25 % scale as a dimmer continuous arc distinct from solid and dotted |

Zone intervals come from the LS-7 band names at build time and are never typed as numbers in instrument data (D.2). The alarm zone never uses Alarm Red: a static red band on every dial would make red common and decorative (ART 4.1, "red stays rare"). A `p_o2` dial has four zones (`NORMAL`, `THIN`, `HYPOXIC`, `ANOXIC`), so it uses solid, hatched, rail and rail, the two rails separated by a 2-px Void Steel tick. The split rail and the 1-px scale ticks are minimum-feature exceptions (BR-9); the fallback that leaves the envelope unchanged lifts the rail to two 2-px rows with a 2-px gap.

#### VA-2.2 LINEAR sight-glass (IW-20, advisor ruling R8)

| Part | Spec |
|---|---|
| Housing | about 12x62 chamfered, 2 px bezel, Graphite lit, 4-px end caps; mounted on a tank prop (the 48x96 prop cell), clear of the pipe band because a tank is a prop, not a wall run |
| Tube and travel | 6 px wide glass; `LINEAR_TRAVEL_PX` 50 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, so 1 art px = 20 permille = the LS-20 store quantum: one store crossing moves the meniscus exactly one row, with no sub-pixel state |
| Fluid | a baked column in the stored medium's palette hex (Fuel Ember for the home tank, WM-20; O2 Cyan for the top-up tank, SE VA-23; Coolant Ice for the reservoirs), clipped to the level row. It is **stock, not flow**: lit-only, not emissive, so a dead loop's tank still shows its fuel. A deliberate deviation from ART 8.3 ("baking hue into albedo breaks the dead-loop rule"), not to be re-litigated |
| Meniscus | a 6x2 (wide x tall) Hull Grey bead at the fluid top, always drawn; the element that settles, trembles or glides |
| Scale ticks | five thin ticks beside the tube at 0 / 25 / 50 / 75 / 100 %, 2x1 Hull Grey; no numerals |
| Zone strip | the same three patterns on a 3 px strip beside the tube |
| Fill-level redundancy | the fuel loop's colour-blind backup (ART 4.7 Power versus Fuel) is exactly this glass, so a lying home-tank glass corrupts a backup channel on purpose; the pipe's flow pattern and the puddle remain the TRUTH backups (R8) |
| Motion | the meniscus only; tube, caps and ticks are static ANCHOR |

#### VA-2.3 Needle and glass slices (the answer to PF-45 and R11)

Each corruptible dial sheet carries these named slices in `_alb`, and the same regions in `_msk`: `glass_l` / `glass_s` (the dial window inside the bezel, the gleam's glass edge; a 1-px specular highlight lives in `_spc`, never violet, never moving with the lie); `needle_l` / `needle_s` (the pivoted needle, drawn pointing up at rotation 0 with the pivot at its tail end); `needle_edge_l` / `needle_edge_s` (the leading, moving-side 1-px edge, where the gleam rides); `housing_edge_*` (the outer chamfer edge, stage-2 housing glow); `pip_*`, `lens_*`, `stress_notch_*`, `dead_*` (VA-2.4 to VA-2.8).

#### VA-2.4 Housing pip (`HOUSING_GLOW`, IW-4, advisor ruling R21)

| Property | Spec |
|---|---|
| Size and place | `PIP_PX` 4x2 art px `[PLACEHOLDER]` (range 2x2 to 4x2, OQ-IW-12) on the fitting plate; the only emissive element of an instrument |
| Hue | the bound loop's palette hex through the `_msk` region at runtime, never baked. The hull / integrity loop has no running medium and **no pip** |
| Frames | `lamp_lit` and `lamp_unlit` on one sheet page (PF V.11); the unlit frame is the same sprite with the emission off |
| Exists only while | the bound subject's `served_pm` > 0 |
| Emission cap | at or below fixture glow and well below the beam (ART 3.5); bloom radius at most 2 art px; casts no light and raises no exposure (LO-14); the numeric cap `PIP_EMIT_MAX` is set by the Lighting spike (ART 8.12) |
| Role | a find-aid in the dark, not a reading: it encodes presence only, never the needle value, and reading still needs `lit(ref)` |
| Hue-removed test | presence or absence of a bright 4x2 mark on a Graphite-lit plate; loop hues have L* 61-91 against Graphite lit L* 43, a gap of at least 18 L* |
| Truth class | corruptible through `HOUSING_GLOW` (stage 2). Never confusable with the fixture lamp, whose brightness from `served_pm` is consequence channel and casts a pool; the pip casts none |

#### VA-2.5 State glyph (stage 2, IW-17)

8x8 chamfered-square lens on the module housing (ART 8.2: an 8x8 state mark never identifies a loop). **Run** = a filled lens with a 2-px inner tick; **stop** = a hollow lens with a 2-px diagonal bar. The two frames differ in shape, so they survive the hue-removed and greyscale tests; both sit on one sheet page. No loop hue, no flow mark and no fitting silhouette on the lens: the loop is named by the fitting plate and the pip. Dropping the hue also avoids a hue swap beside a possibly lying needle, which would be an oracle (checkpoint ruling C3).

#### VA-2.6 Alarm mark (IW-19, advisor ruling R14, checkpoint ruling C4)

One glyph shape (the ART 7.2 chamfered square, Alarm Red `#E0102A`, exclamation stroke) at its canonical 16x16 (`ALARM_MARK_WORLD_PX`, "nothing smaller", ART 8.2), exported twice from one source: `ui_glyph_warn_16` (HUD, Paper White stroke, exactly as ART 7.2 defines it) and `mach_mark_warn_alb` (world, **lit**, so it is dark when unlit and lit-gated by the renderer like the face, stroke **Void Steel** because Paper White is UI-only, fact 16, ART 4.6, ART 8.3; the Void Steel-on-Alarm-Red contrast, L* about 5 against about 47, is measured in S-A1, and the fallback is an explicit BR-8 exception for one Paper White stroke). The mark sits on the **housing, not the face**: in the 36-px horizontal bay beside a DIAL_L housing, on machine and console panels and on the tank prop. **DIAL_S carries none** (a 16x16 mark cannot sit on a 46x35 housing in a 48x48 cell; its hiss, draught and sweep are its alarm). It is an untagged TRUTH node, separate from every corruptible ref (SN L-A); it pulses in beacon phase (2-3 s period, about 50 % duty, never faster than 3 Hz, HR-13 and ART 7.3), holds solid under reduced motion, is non-emissive and casts no light. The decoupling rule of IW-19 applies: the mark's absence never proves a lie. Fallback if the art-director rejects a second Alarm Red surface: no mark (sweep and sound only); nothing else changes.

#### VA-2.7 Loop fitting on the housing (identity without hue)

Loop identity is the fitting silhouette on the housing's lower sector (16x11 on DIAL_L, 12x8 on DIAL_S, both seated on the bottom bezel), plus the running-only pip. At 12x8 the ring clamp, padded collar and bolted flange are marginal at 25 % scale (ART 3.1 was written for pipe sections, not housings); redundant channels: a DIAL_S is mounted on the terminal fitting of the pipe it reads, the card label names the reading, and the pip's presence says "something here is running". S-A1 tests this. Gauge faces carry **no** loop hue (BR-4).

#### VA-2.8 Stress notch, dead frame, wear

* **Stress notch (IW-27).** World: two Void Steel nicks, each `max(2, ceil(4 / k))` art px square (2x2 at `k` 2 and above, 4x4 at `k` 1: at least 4 screen px at every rung) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, cut into the bezel top edge about 4 px either side of centre. Card: two 2x2 Hull Grey squares (at least 6 screen px at `H_card` 3) on the Ground plate above the dial (HR-2 forbids Hull Grey on a bezel fill, so the card's notch sits on the Ground side of the dial rim; a minor deviation from R19's word "bezel"). Static while the IW-12 predicate holds and `motion_mode` is HONEST; absent on a lying reading; exactly as discriminating as tremor and no more.
* **Dead frame (IW-30, checkpoint ruling C11).** A 1-px Hull Grey diagonal hairline crack across the glass and the needle replaced by a 4-px stub at a fixed angle. It is an honest static frame on the sheet, TRUTH, with no prompt, card, gleam, tremor or pip. A needle at zero would assert "reads empty" instead of "has no reading".
* **Wear (IW-18).** Scuff overlays and a patch plate on the module and the instrument bound to it: Graphite body, Hull Grey only on the bead and bolts (ART 6.6 note); no word, bar, rating, numeral or colour grade of the face. The patch plate persists and is TRUTH-graded.

### VA-3. Palette and semantic colour rules

| Element | Colour | Rule | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Face plate | Graphite mid | one of two Graphite plane values | ART 6.2; 8.9 line 9 |
| Bezel, housing, tank cap | Graphite lit `#5C6670` | solid, heavy, static | ART 3.4 |
| Ticks, bands, needle, meniscus bead | Hull Grey `#8C949C` | thin elements only; the ART 4.4 near-flesh sample test applies (hue at least 36 degrees under every Tungsten source) | ART 4.4; 8.9 line 3 |
| Gaps, nicks, world alarm stroke | Void Steel | 1 px; the only place a darker value is used | ART 4.1 |
| Fluid (sight-glass) | Fuel Ember / O2 Cyan / Coolant Ice (palette hex, baked) | stock colour, lit-only; Fuel Ember (hue 20, S above 80 %) must pass the ART 4.4 sample under Tungsten and Chemlight | ART 4.2 |
| Housing pip | the bound loop hex at runtime through `_msk` | emissive, casts no light, at most `PIP_EMIT_MAX`; the hull has none | ART 4.2; R21 |
| Alarm mark | Alarm Red `#E0102A`; world stroke Void Steel; HUD twin stroke Paper White | a mark, never text; lit albedo in the world; sampled in the ART 4.4 test | ART 4.6; HR-4 |
| Card plate | Ground `#1C2128`, opaque | every reading on Ground | HR-2; ART 4.6 |
| Card bezel | Graphite lit; a Hull Grey rivet per corner | frame register is HUD's nine-slice | ART 7.0; HS-57 |
| Card label and unit | Hull Grey on Ground (5.3:1) | 5 art px caps | ART 7.1, 7.6 |
| Card numeral | Paper White `#DDE4EA` on Ground (12.6:1) | 7 art px cap height; the only place Paper White exists in this system | ART 4.6, 7.1 |
| Gleam | fringe `#B07BE6` at runtime only | edge only, never core, never baked; peak at most `LEAK_MAX_CH` 0.85 | ART 4.4; PF-16 |
| In any `mach_*`, `ui_*` or `ovl_*` sheet, never | violet 270-330 degrees at S above 25 %; flesh 5-30 degrees at S 20-60 %, V 45-85 % | automatic scan | ART 8.9 line 4 |

**Loop hue rule (BR-4).** Gauge faces carry no loop hue: baking a loop hue into a face would show a hue on a dead loop's instrument and tint the face, which is state without a mask. Identity is the fitting silhouette and the running-only pip. **Luminance ladder (ART 4.5).** Collision pairs and their shape backup: Fuel Ember against Hull Grey (L* 61 and 61): the fuel column is told apart by position (inside the tube), shape (column plus meniscus) and the pip; Coolant Ice against Tungsten (91 and 91): coolant fluid is inside a glass tube and Tungsten is never a fluid. No pair relies on hue.

### VA-4. The inspect card (layer 2, `CARD_MAG` 1)

Anatomy of the 72x72 (DIAL) or 72x88 (LINEAR) HUD art px plate (`CARD_W_ART_PX`, `CARD_H_ART_PX[kind]`; checkpoint ruling C7; the LINEAR height is the 2026-10-27 design review), drawn on the HUD grid at `H_card` (D.6):

| Part | Spec |
|---|---|
| Frame | the ship's bezel dialect: chamfered rectangle, Graphite lit border, one rivet per corner, solid, heavy, static (ART 3.4, 7.0); no rounded corners; the nine-slice `ui_frame_card` is the HUD's frame register (HS-57) and is bit-identical with the Filter on or off and never gleams (HS-3) |
| Plate | opaque Ground; never dimming or blurring the world behind it (ART 7.4); about 30 % of the plate is non-Ground, 0.67 % of a 1080p frame at `H` 3 (D.6), stated so the HUD's UX spec can record the exclusion from the at-rest non-Ground cap (HS-56) |
| Label | `name_key`, caps, Hull Grey on Ground, 5 art px cap height, 1 px letter spacing; wraps to a second row (plate grows 7 art px) and is never truncated (IW-37); no state word, no verdict (R2) |
| Dial | exported from the same source art as the world dial into a flat `ui_` sheet (no light, no `_nrm` / `_spc` / `_msk`), Graphite values retuned only for Ground contrast, **identical geometry, zone patterns, needle length and band thickness**, drawn 1:1 in HUD art px |
| Glass (LINEAR) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | the same sight-glass at HUD art px, 1:1: the 12x62 housing with its 2-px bezel and 4-px caps, the 6-px tube, the fluid column, the 6x2 meniscus bead (`travel_px` 50), the five scale ticks and the 3-px zone strip, centred between the label row and the numeral row (4 margin + 5 label + 3 gap + 62 glass + 3 gap + 7 numeral + 4 margin = 88); exported from the same source as the world glass into `ui_card_glass_<loop>` (flat, no light maps); the meniscus bead is the element that settles, trembles or glides and carries the gleam on its top edge |
| Numeral | one numeral with its unit: tabular bitmap digits, slashed zero, distinct 1 / l / I, Paper White, 7 art px cap; fixed cells (sign, up to three digits, decimal point, one decimal); `p_o2` in kPa with one decimal, `T` in whole C, stores and load as whole percent; the format never reveals more precision than `lsb` (D.3) |
| Digit well | a recessed glass rectangle with a 1-HUD-px top and bottom edge carrying the gleam (the `glass` slice on the `ui_` sheet) |
| Pointer notch | a 6x4 chamfered wedge on the plate edge nearest the anchor, frame geometry owned by the HUD's nine-slice *(orchestrator ruling: kept as frame geometry, not content, so IW-22's closed content list is untouched)* |
| Stress notch | VA-2.8, honest and reduced-motion only |
| Never on the card | alarm mark, loop glyph (OQ-IW-16), wear, module-state frame, true value, lie count, stage name, condition word (IW-22) |
| Dead / unresolvable | the card does not exist (R4) |

**Card against wall (IW-26).** One `Perceived` per frame per instrument serves both; the card needle shows the same angle set as the wall needle (it must not be a more precise render of a lying value than the wall is of the same value); same wander and tremor phase (shared `present_time`, `ref_key`-seeded presentation noise); same gleam parameters; opening the card never replays a settle and never sweeps the needle in from zero. The card dial is drawn from the same tip-pixel frame index as the wall needle, so the angle sets match (the IW-10 decision; the ADR is X-6 item 7; spike S-A3). **Open and close** are cuts (R4): no slide, bounce, scale-pop, blur or glow (ART 7.3, 7.4); re-projection after a camera cut or zoom is a cut to the new anchor, never a slide and never a scale change (HR-13). Legibility of the card at every display and `H` is D.7's card table (216 px at 1080p `H` 3, 432 px at `H` 6; 360 px on 720p at `H` 6 because of the cap; the Steam Deck case is 216 px, 27 %).

### VA-5. Legibility reference

The world dial at every zoom rung `k` in [1, `k_auto` + 2] and every display, the needle's share of picture height, the gleam and tremor art-px sizes (`A_eff` 2 and gleam 2 art px at `k` 1; 1 art px at `k` 2 and above, so at least 2 screen px at every rung) and the card by `H` are tabulated in D.7 and not restated. The visual consequences this section states: at rung 1 every dial is its authored art-pixel size (a 56-px arc), so band patterns (period 6) and the needle stay resolvable, but tremor at 2 screen px is at the edge of perceptibility and the design accepts it (the tell is for a player standing at the instrument; tremor perceptibility floor `k` 2); DIAL_S at `k` 1 (an 18-screen-px needle) is below `NEEDLE_SCREEN_MIN_PX` 24 and is accepted because `k` 1 is a deliberate zoom-out and the card is the precision path; **zoom 1x at 2160p, a 56-px arc in a 3840-px frame, is the design's weakest point** and is carried as a risk (R-VA-6). Locate-only rungs (`k / k_auto` below 0.5) are listed in D.7.

### VA-6. Motion in pixels

Motion rules (before any number): settle and tremor only when `motion_mode` is HONEST; a glide is Filter-driven (`displayed_pm` over time) and the instrument adds nothing but the Filter-supplied wander; no `Tween`, no wall clock; `present_time` closed forms, frozen under PAUSED; every placement is integer art px (PR-8).

| Quantity | DIAL_L (needle 24) | DIAL_S (needle 18) | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Angular step per 1 px of tip travel | 1/24 rad = 2.39 degrees | 1/18 rad = 3.18 degrees | geometry |
| Glide at `rate_floor` = max(0.5, 28.6 / `needle_len_px`) | 1.19 deg/s, tip 0.50 px/s | 1.59 deg/s, tip 0.50 px/s | PF-17; D.1 |
| Glide at `GLIDE_RATE_MAX_DEG` 2.0 | tip 0.84 px/s | tip 0.63 px/s | PF D.2 |
| Wander `WANDER_AMP_PX` 2.2 at 0.40-0.45 Hz | plus or minus 5.3 degrees; peak tip speed 5.5-6.2 px/s | plus or minus 7.0 degrees | PF D.4 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (2.2 / 24 rad and 2.2 / 18 rad; the earlier figures used an amplitude of 2.0) | *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*
| Tremor plus or minus 1 art px at 6-10 Hz | plus or minus 2.39 degrees | plus or minus 3.18 degrees | D.5 |
| Glide against tremor | frequency 0.4 Hz against 6-10 Hz (15-25 times apart); wander is slow and smooth, tremor is jitter; both read without colour | | ART 7.3 |
| Tip-pixel flips per second on a gliding tip | slew alone 0.5 flips/s; with wander 4-6 flips/s | same | derived; the never-rest guarantee is carried by the wander (PF V.6.3) |

A **tip-pixel flip** is a frame whose rounded tip coordinate differs from the previous frame's. An honest needle at rest shows 0 flips over any 1 s window; a gliding or ticking needle shows at least 1 in every 1 s window (`NEVER_REST_MAX_S` 1.0). At the floor rate the slew alone is one pixel per 2 s, so the guarantee rests on the wander.

| Kind | Settle (honest jump) | Tremor (honest stress) | Lie (Filter-driven) |
|---|---|---|---|
| DIAL | a pure function of `present_time - t_jump`; overshoot at most 5 % of the jump; 250-400 ms; then exactly still; a jump whose overshoot would be under 1 px at the tip simply eases out | plus or minus 1 art px jitter about the true angle, irregular (two incommensurate frequencies in 6-10 Hz, phases from a hash of `ref_key`, presentation noise only, never a sim stream); the face stays static | the needle draws `displayed_pm` verbatim: slow slew plus wander, never at rest, in **every** non-HONEST mode, with the gleam on `needle_edge` and `glass`; TICK and STALE are modes of the **card numeral** only (X1, C2) |
| LINEAR | meniscus eases out, overshoot at most 5 % of the change, rounded to whole px, resolving to the exact quantised level | meniscus plus or minus 1 px at 6-10 Hz (liquid trembling in a stressed hull) | meniscus creeps at the Filter's linear rate with plus or minus 2 px wander: a level that will not sit still with nothing sloshing it |
| Card numeral | none (digits step once per real change on the HUD hold grid, never tween) | none (a numeral does not tremble) | continuous tick every `T_tick` as a digit-glyph substitution, never an inversion; or held digits in STALE |
| Glyph, pip, machine frame | an immediate frame swap, no cross-fade (ART 7.2) | none | the Filter's `frame_override` cadence, bounded by `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` 3 |

Honest continuous change is the one-step detent (IW-11 (a)): a ramp from `prev_honest_pm` over one Graph step (100 ms) through the single `tick_alpha` interpolator, then rest; a fast ramp (a decompression) crosses a quantum every step and reads as continuous. **Mode transitions.** HONEST to a lie: any in-flight settle residual is dropped (a lie never borrows settle) and the pop is at most the settle's current deviation (at most 1 px at the tip in practice, because the first lie is on a fault-free instrument at rest); RETURN to HONEST: no settle, the gleam amplitude eases to 0 with `leak_amplitude_pm`; `hard_start`, restore, camera cut and cull: snap to `displayed_pm`, no settle, no ramp. **Reduced motion (HR-8):** settle becomes a step, tremor becomes the stress notch, the alarm mark holds solid; glide, wander and tick scaling are the Filter's (PF-39). **Flash and shake (HR-13):** positional tremor is exempt from the 3 Hz luminance ceiling (ART 7.3; K-18 and OQ-IW-13 resolved by default 2026-10-27, D96: the card shows it, suppressed under `reduced_motion`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; the card frame, plate and keycaps never move; the tremor amplitude on the card is plus or minus `TREMOR_AMP_PX` HUD art px and is never multiplied by a card multiple (C8); no luminance transition on the card faster than 3 Hz.

### VA-7. The gleam (HR-5; the answer to OQ-PF-11 for the card)

| Property | World gauge | Card |
|---|---|---|
| Where | edge pixels of the `glass` slice and `needle_edge`; the housing edge for the stage-2 housing glow | the same regions on the card dial plus the digit well's top and bottom edge; the card plate, bezel and rivets never carry violet (SNV-8, PF-18) |
| Width | `gleam_edge_art_px(k)` = `ceil(2 / k)` clamped 1-2 (D.9): 2 art px at `k` 1, 1 art px at `k` 2 and above, at least 2 screen px at every rung | 1 HUD art px = `H_card` screen px, at least 3 |
| Colour | `#B07BE6` produced by the material, never baked | the same, a card-local parameter |
| Lit coupling | visible only while the host is lit (lit with its host, PF-16) | the card exists only while lit, so the gleam does too |
| Peak | at most `LEAK_MAX_CH` 0.85 (`leak_amplitude_pm` at most 942) | the same |
| Motion | steady with a slow swell at most 0.5 Hz in the Filter's phase; never faster than 3 Hz | the same phase (shared `present_time`) |

**Hue risk (art-director finding, carried).** A gleam multiplied by a coloured light leaves the violet band. `#B07BE6` = (176, 123, 230) under the Tungsten beam core `#FFE2A8` becomes about (176, 109, 152): hue about 321 degrees, still inside 270-330 but shifting toward magenta and flesh-adjacent; under the Chemlight `#86B896` it becomes about (92, 89, 135): hue about 244 degrees, outside the violet band, reading blue, and darker (L* drops about 30). A lying gauge lit only by a glowstick would lose its hue mode. Requirement: the gleam is composited so its hue is not modulated by the colour of the light (intensity gated by the host's lit factor, hue fixed) -> ADR candidate (shared with Filter spike S-1 and OQ-PF-6; OQ-IW-8). The motion mode survives regardless and HR-5 needs only each mode sufficient, so this is a risk, not a break (R-VA-4).

### VA-8. Per-event visual and audio requirements

The system produces no simulation event. The audio requests are the Filter's (lies) or UI-bus (card). "Channel" is the truth class of the thing drawn (VA-9).

| # | Event (source) | Visual / sprite state | Audio class | Channel | Anti-requirement |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | Instrument becomes lit (`lit(ref)` true, EM-22) | the face becomes readable by light alone; the prompt plate appears (HUD); the needle is simply at its value | none | n/a | no settle on reveal, no scan-in, no highlight |
| E2 | Instrument becomes unlit | the readable pixels fall to the ambient / dark by light alone; an open card closes by cut | none | n/a | no fade held by the sprite; no "last known" ghost |
| E3 | Continuous honest change of `displayed_pm` | a one-step detent ramp from `prev_honest_pm` through `tick_alpha`, whole-pixel tip positions, then rest | none (motion only, AA:556) | corruptible module-body, honest | no tween, no overshoot, no extrapolation |
| E4 | Discrete jump (repair, valve flick, `apply_damage`) | settle: 250-400 ms, overshoot at most 5 %, then still | none | honest | settle in a non-HONEST mode |
| E5 | Stress predicate begins / ends (IW-12) | tremor ramps in over `TREMOR_ONSET_MS` 150 and decays over `TREMOR_DECAY_MS` 600, resting on a pixel | none | honest-only | tremor on a lying reading; tremor with no predicate |
| E6 | Lie begins (Filter) | the needle draws `displayed_pm` verbatim (slew plus wander) from the current drawn angle; the gleam fades up with `leak_amplitude_pm` | the Filter's `FA_LIE_SIG_GAUGE` (needle, card, glyph) or `FA_LIE_SIG_SPRITE` (housing, machine), starting no later than the glide | corruptible | any settle or tremor on the lying instrument; a sound-only lie |
| E7 | Lie returns (RETURN to HONEST) | a glide to truth, the gleam amplitude to 0, then HONEST with no settle | the Filter's request goes OFF | corruptible | an announced end, a flash |
| E8 | `alarm_eligible` becomes true (LS-13) | the 16x16 mark appears on a DIAL_L, panel or tank housing, beacon-phase pulse, solid under reduced motion | none from this system (the sweep and `ann` belong to Audio and Attention) | TRUTH | the mark on a corruptible ref; the mark as the first warning; the mark on a DIAL_S or the card |
| E9 | Bound node WORN or patched (sev 1) | scuff overlay and patch plate on the module and housing | none | honest at stages 0-2 | a condition glyph; a colour change of the face |
| E10 | Bound node DESTROYED, SALVAGED, ABSENT or ref unresolvable | the DEAD frame; the card closes; no pip | none | TRUTH | a needle at zero |
| E11 | A loop starts or stops serving (`served_pm` crosses 0) | the pip appears or goes dark by emission-frame swap; machine frames per Graph state | none | corruptible (stage 2) | a pulsing pip; a pip brighter than a fixture |
| E12 | Card opened | the plate cut in beside the instrument, the needle already at its value and phase | UI-bus `UI_INSTRUMENT_CARD_OPEN` (requested name) | honest or corruptible as the wall gauge | a sweep from zero; a scale-in |
| E13 | Card closed (any R4 cause) | cut | UI-bus `UI_INSTRUMENT_CARD_CLOSE` (requested name) | n/a | a fade; a dwell |
| E14 | Camera cut or zoom step | instruments re-render at the new rung; the card re-anchors by cut | none | n/a | a card scale change |
| E15 | Pause | `present_time` freezes; the needle freezes mid-motion; an open card has already closed (cut, no fade) and stays closed on resume *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2)*; the world is not dimmed | none | n/a | dimming, blur |
| E16 | Hard start or restore | every instrument snaps to `displayed_pm`; no settle, no glide ramp; the stress notch is re-evaluated | none | n/a | any carried-over presentation state |
| E17 | Stage-2 housing-glow lie or stage-3 SPRITE_SWAP | the other honest frame of the pair is shown with the housing-edge gleam; both frames exist on one sheet page | the Filter's `FA_LIE_SIG_SPRITE` | corruptible | a frame that exists nowhere in the module's honest set |
| E18 | Reduced motion on | settle becomes a step; tremor becomes the stress notch; the alarm mark holds solid | none | honest-only | removing never-rest; adding a violet element |
| E19 | Chemlight, fixture or beam sweeps a gauge | continuous light on the sprite; legibility by light only | none | n/a | a binary pop (the lit bit gates the card and prompt, not the sprite) |
| E20 | `lit(ref)` falls while `inspect` is held | the card closes (E13) with the distinct unlatch cue (IW-24); no reopen without a new press | the UI-bus unlatch variant (requested name `UI_INSTRUMENT_CARD_UNLATCH`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | n/a | reopen flicker at a pool rim (there is no re-open debounce, IW-24: a press is never gated) |
| E21 | Honest card-numeral grid boundary (`HONEST_TICK_MS`) | one digit-glyph substitution of the cells that changed, one luminance event, the then-current step | none | honest | a count-up; a tween; an inverted plate |
| E22 | Lying card numeral tick or hold | a continuous digit-glyph substitution every `T_tick`, or held digits in STALE; the needle keeps its trajectory | the Filter's lie sound | corruptible | the needle held at rest |

### VA-9. Truth and corruptible classification of every emitted visual

The default is TRUTH (PF-4): an untagged node is TRUTH, and omission makes a lie vanish, never the truth break. "Ref" is the PF-9 `PerceptionRef` kind.

| Visual | Channel | Ref / tag | Reach stage (SN-16) | Lie kinds drawn | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Needle angle (world) | corruptible, module-body | `GRAPH_NODE` / `GRAPH_COMPARTMENT` (the `quantity` term, K-5) | 1 | FALSE_OK, FALSE_FAIL, GLIDE | motion mode and gleam mandatory |
| Sight-glass meniscus and fluid height | corruptible, module-body | `GRAPH_NODE` (tank) | 1 | FALSE_OK, FALSE_FAIL, GLIDE (linear branch) | the pipe's flow pattern and the puddle remain TRUTH |
| Card needle and numeral | corruptible, module-body | the **same ref** as the wall gauge (no card ref, C2) | 1 | identical to the wall | never a falsifier (R13) |
| Zone bands, ticks, scale marks | ANCHOR (static calibration) | none | n/a | none | a lying gauge moves the needle over a fixed scale |
| Housing, bezel, fitting plate, hub, tube, caps | ANCHOR (physical geometry, P1) | none | n/a | none | |
| Glass specular highlight (`_spc`) | TRUTH (a lit property) | none | n/a | none | never violet, never moves with a lie |
| State glyph lens | corruptible, module-body | `STATE_GLYPH` | 2 | FALSE_OK, FALSE_FAIL, SPRITE_SWAP | both frames required |
| Housing pip | corruptible, module-body (appearance frame only) | `HOUSING_GLOW` | 2 | SPRITE_SWAP of lit / unlit | casts no light |
| Machine frames, rotor cadence | corruptible, module-body | `MACHINE_FRAME` | 3 | SPRITE_SWAP | both honest frames must exist (content gate, AC-SN-152) |
| WORN scuff and patch plate | honest at 0-2; with the machine frame at 3 | `MACHINE_FRAME` | 3 | frame swap only | F&T draws nothing for WORN (FT:637); no word or bar |
| Alarm mark | TRUTH, untagged | none | never | none | cannot be added, altered or suppressed by any lie (SN L-A) |
| Dead frame | TRUTH | none | never | none | physical damage |
| Stress notch, tremor | honest-only presentation of a Graph predicate | none (a `read_view` predicate) | never on a lying reading | none | absent when `motion_mode` is not HONEST |
| Gleam | the leak itself, Filter-driven | n/a | per lie | n/a | drawn here (R5) |
| Card frame, plate, rivets, label, unit, pointer notch | ANCHOR | none | n/a | none | the label is a static `name_key` |
| Numeral scale and format | ANCHOR (a format rule) | none | n/a | none | the lie is the value, never the format |
| Trail VFX, frost, drip, flow pattern, lamp brightness, AIRFLOW, SEAL_STRAIN | consequence channel | not drawn here | never | none | this system adds, alters and removes nothing there (LSG:168) |

### VA-10. Audio

Instruments request no sound directly (IW-32). Every audio fact here is the neighbour's, named so the Audio GDD and the Filter can be checked against it.

* **A lying instrument's sound** is the Filter's request (PF-20): `FA_LIE_SIG_GAUGE` for the needle, the card (needle and numeral) and the glyph; `FA_LIE_SIG_SPRITE` for the housing and the machine. A world-surface lie is positional with an audible radius `HAL_RADIUS_PX` (Audio-owned `[PLACEHOLDER]`, default 192, never above `legibility_range_px` of the lying surface, AA-36); a card or HUD reference is owner-private. It starts no later than the glide (ART 7.3, HR-5), and a lying instrument over a machine with a real fault still lets the machine's true hiss or hum play (the falsifier, AA E-AA-49). Closing and reopening a card on an active lie requests no new sound (IW-26).
* **Honest settle, tremor and digit ticks are motion only** (ART 7.3; AA:556): the art-director asks for no sound on them.
* **Card open and close** are UI-bus sounds, Audio-owned, triggered by the presentation-local `card_opened` / `card_closed` signals (event names `UI_INSTRUMENT_CARD_OPEN` and `UI_INSTRUMENT_CARD_CLOSE` requested, Audio owns the naming) `(provisional — Adaptive Audio reply owed)`. Mood: a small mechanical latch in the Foundry dialect (heavy, short, blunt), consistent with a chamfered bezel and rivets; no tonal confirmation and no rising or falling pitch (a pitched UI blip would read as a verdict).
* **Mood of the lie** (the art-director's, for Audio): dry, instrument-grade and human-made, a stylus-on-brass or dial-escapement quality, quiet enough to be doubted; never a sci-fi chime, a stinger or a "wrong" synth. The visual lie is thin and light and the sound should be the same size.
* **Direction agreement (AAV-17).** The rotor, the sight-glass and the lamp brightness read the same Graph value as the audio, so the pip, the machine frame and the sight-glass stay consistent with each other unless one of them is the lie; a sagging hum beside a full-speed rotor frame is a contradiction the Presenter must not create unless that frame is the lie.
* **Alarm Red pairing.** The mark's sound pairing (`ann`, the sweep, the `attn` breach pulses) belongs to Audio and Attention; the mark itself is silent. No other instrument element is associated with red.
* **Blind-test classification (HR-5).** Hue removed: the lie is identified by motion mode (never-rest wander, continuous tick) and sound. Audio removed: by the gleam and the motion mode. Each mode alone is sufficient; the gleam alone cannot be isolated by a player (motion is always present) and is covered by the pixel-hue check (AC-IW-78).

All sheets are lossless PNG, palette-locked, binary alpha, nearest filtering. **BR-5:** ART 8.5's `state` token list has no `running`, `stopped`, `whole`, `cracked`, `lit`, `unlit`, `worn` or `dead`; add them.

**Not new assets:** no `vfx_` sheet (the gleam is a material parameter), no `light_` texture (the pip casts no light), no glyph for any state word, no schematic art, no pod dash, no card loop glyph, no numeral sheet in the world. **Counts (indicative, MVP slice home about 9 / derelict A about 4 / derelict B about 5, IW-29):** about 10 distinct quantity faces over 1-2 sizes (about 14 face slices), 5 `mach_gauge` sheets, 3 `mach_sightglass` sheets, 1 alarm mark, about 11 `ui_card_*` assets (the frame, the dial faces and needles, the digit well and, since the design review, three `ui_card_glass_*` sheets) and the wear set: about 20 sheets at P0, against the `mach_<module>` frames (4 modules x 4 frames) at P2.

**Asset acceptance deltas to ART 8.9 for these categories.** (1) The silhouette test (line 1) on both housings, the sight-glass and the fitting plates. (2) The hue-removed pattern test: the three zone codes are distinguishable at 25 % and 50 % in greyscale. (3) The ART 4.4 flesh sampling includes needle, bands, fluid columns and the alarm mark under every Tungsten source and Chemlight. (4) The reserved-hue scan passes on `mach_*`, `ovl_*` and `ui_*`: the gleam and the pip are never baked. (5) The minimum-feature scan: 2x2 for needle, pip and notches; 1-px elements limited to bezel highlights, Void Steel gaps, the DIAL_S bezel, the split rail and the scale ticks (BR-9). (6) The state-question exemption (line 12) lists the state glyph and the machine frames only (BR-1). (7) Parity check: the card dial export and the world dial export diff to zero on geometry (band positions, needle length, pivot).

**Bible refreshes owed (routed, not edited here; X-3):** BR-1 (reading-surface exemption to "icons never swap to express state"), BR-2 (terminal-fitting exception to ART 6.3 rule 7), BR-3 (Alarm Red scoped to the UI layers; the world housing mark stands), BR-4 (gauge faces carry no loop hue), BR-5 (state tokens), BR-6 (ART 7.0 "lantern" and pod-dash wording), BR-7 (ART 2.7 "a needle that drifts on a real gauge" dropped), BR-9 (minimum-feature exceptions).

### VA-12. Risks and spikes

| # | Risk | Mitigation / spike |
|---|---|---|
| R-VA-1 | DIAL_L (46 of 48 rows) and DIAL_S (46 of 48 columns) leave 1-px margins in their cells; the fitting plates and the alarm bay are tight | S-A1 pixel mock; first cut: the DIAL_S bezel |
| R-VA-2 | A 240-degree arc on a rectangle may read as the crewmate-reserved round-on-rectangle stack at head height (ART 6.6 rule 2) | S-A1 silhouette test at 25 % and 50 %; fallback an octagonal chamfered arc authored once for both sizes |
| R-VA-3 | Hatched, split-rail and solid at 3 px may not stay distinct at 25 % scale on 2160p rung 1 | S-A1 greyscale strip; fallback an outward tick on the alarm zone (envelope plus 1 px) |
| R-VA-4 | The gleam loses its violet hue under Chemlight (VA-7) | the compositing requirement (ADR candidate); motion and sound remain, so two of three modes survive |
| R-VA-5 | At rung 1 the tremor and the gleam are at the edge of perceptibility | `A_eff` and `gleam_edge_art_px` raise both to 2 screen px; tremor perceptibility floor `k` 2 accepted |
| R-VA-6 | Zoom 1x at 2160p: a 56-px arc in a 3840-px frame | readable only by pattern and needle; the card is the precision tool; carried, not hidden |
| R-VA-7 | Absence of the alarm mark as an oracle on a FALSE_FAIL | the decoupling rule (IW-19); confirm with Graph and Sanity |
| R-VA-8 | The card is the largest modal plate in the HUD (432 px, 40 % of the height at 1080p `H` 6; 45 % on the Steam Deck at maximum `H`) and may cover the view during a breach | the cap `CARD_MAX_FRAC_H`, the edge clamp and the keep-outs; the card closes on `cancel` and the sim keeps ticking |
| R-VA-9 | Lie-capable instrument count against a solo developer's art load (about 20 sheets at P0) | P0 / P1 / P2 tiers; machine frames cuttable (IW-34); faces generated at build time |
| R-VA-10 | The fuel colour-blind backup (the fill-level glass) is itself corruptible | the pipe flow pattern and the puddle remain TRUTH backups (R8) |

**Spikes (owner: technical-artist, 1-2 days each; fed to Open Questions).** **S-A1 pixel mock:** DIAL_L, DIAL_S, the sight-glass and the fitting plates at authored size, in greyscale and hue-removed, at 25 / 50 / 100 %, against a Foundry wall face and the pipe band; the three zone patterns; the helmet-scale silhouette comparison; the ART 4.4 flesh sample under Tungsten and Chemlight; confirms the DIAL_S bezel and pattern fidelity, the fitting-plate extents (16x11, 12x8) and the LINEAR bead extent (6x2) of IW-7 (the radii are fixed). **S-A2 card mock:** the plate at `H` 3-6 on 1080p / 1440p / 2160p / Steam Deck over a lit frame, to measure the non-Ground area and the chest-point keep-out. **S-A3 needle rasterisation:** needle rotation at base resolution and the card at integer scale, confirming identical angle sets and 1-px steps under nearest sampling `[UNV]` (OQ-IW-7). **S-A4 gleam compositing:** violet stays within 270-330 degrees under Tungsten and Chemlight (shared with Filter spike S-1, OQ-IW-8).

📌 **Asset Spec** — Visual/Audio requirements are defined. After the art bible is approved, run `/asset-spec system:in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view` to produce per-asset visual descriptions, dimensions and generation prompts from this section (the P0 sheets of VA-11 first; the machine-frame quartet is Item DB's art and a P2 cut candidate).

## Game Feel

*Every figure is a design target, in ticks (the kernel runs `TICK_RATE` 60, so 1 tick is 16.7 ms and 1 render frame at 60 fps), milliseconds or screen / art pixels, and is either quoted from the Formulas and Detailed Rules above or marks the place a playtest tunes it. Instruments are a reading surface, not an action: there is no windup, no hitbox and no avatar motion, so the section is organised around the two things the player's hands and eyes meet: the one button (`inspect`) and the needle's body language.*

### Feel Reference

**Reference moment: reading a damped mechanical gauge in a dark room by a torch you wear, with your hands free.** The borrowed qualities, each from a named source: from **Pandorum** the chest-mounted, hands-free beam that decides what can be read (the player's own light is the reading tool, so the cost of looking is standing still in it); from **Iron Lung** the sense that knowledge arrives only through a crude physical instrument of limited precision and nothing confirms it; from **Dead Space** the readout that lives on the world rather than floating in front of it; from **Signalis** the restraint of a low-colour mechanical interface whose frames are heavy and static while its readings are thin and moving (ART 3.4). The physical referent for the needle is a real analogue gauge with viscous damping: it comes to rest, a sudden large change on the bus-load dial gives it one soft overshoot (smaller changes ease out, and atmosphere and store dials step and rest like a detent), and a stressed hull makes it hum with a small irregular tremor *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

**Anti-references.** Not a floaty tweened HUD bar (nothing here eases for its own sake: the only eases are the closed-form settle and the one-step detent ramp, both caused by a world change). Not **Alien: Isolation**'s motion tracker (no instrument pings, points or warns; Attention never moves a needle, AT-17). Not **Eternal Darkness**'s fourth-wall tricks (the frame, the card plate and the menus never lie and never glitch; only a reading does, ART 7.3). Not **Project Zomboid**'s moodle pop-ups or condition bars (no state word, no rating, R2). Not a status-screen "refresh" (the card opens as a cut and offers no summary).

### Input Responsiveness

The only player input this system owns is `inspect` (and `cancel` / B to close it); movement, aim and zoom are the Controller's and are unaffected except where the table says so. Latencies are measured from the Adapter receiving the press to the first render frame that shows the result; the budget assumes 60 fps.

| Action | Max input-to-response latency | Frame budget (60 fps) | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `inspect` pressed on a lit target (primary focus or secondary slot, no debounce condition) | 17 ms target, 33 ms ceiling | 1 frame target, 2 frames ceiling | The card is a presentation-local context switch, not a command (IW-21): no kernel round trip, no tick wait; it appears as a cut on the next render frame (IW-24) |
| `inspect` pressed in the first 13 ticks after focus lands, or straight after a close *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | 17 ms target, 33 ms ceiling (the card, on the next render frame) | 1 frame target, 2 frames ceiling | `PROMPT_SHOW_DEBOUNCE_TICKS` 13 delays drawing the plate only; a press is never dropped, queued or sound-suppressed for it (IW-21, E-IW-16) | *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*
| Card closed by `inspect`, `cancel` or B | 17 ms | 1 frame | Same render frame as the press; no fade, no dwell; no cooldown after a deliberate close (E-IW-52) |
| Card closed because `lit(ref)` fell, the binding was lost, reach was lost or the kernel restored | at most 1 tick after the cause (17 ms) | 1 tick | `lit(ref)` is read each render frame (E-IW-15); the card closes once and never strobes at a pool rim |
| Prompt plate appears on a newly focused lit instrument | `PROMPT_SHOW_DEBOUNCE_TICKS` 13 after focus (about 217 ms at 60 Hz; HUD-owned, IA:892) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | 13 ticks | HUD-drawn, Interaction-owned grammar; avoids flicker as the beam sweeps (IW-21) |
| Prompt plate hides on an instrument that goes unlit | 17 ms | 1 frame | Immediately, not debounced |
| Needle begins to move after the Graph value changes (honest; except a one-quantum reversal, see the dwell row below) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | 0-100 ms | 0-6 ticks | A quantum crossing invalidates the Filter's honest memo (LS-20); the ramp starts in the same render frame the new `displayed_pm` is pulled and runs one Graph step (D.1) |
| A one-quantum reversal of the last accepted move *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | 400 ms (`INSTR_REVERSAL_DWELL_STEPS` 4) | 24 ticks | The needle holds until the reversal persists; a dither across a quantum edge never moves it (IW-11 (b)) |
| Needle completes an honest slow change | 100 ms after the sample | 6 ticks | Then rests; the whole path lags the true value by at most one Graph step plus the quantum (IW-11) |
| Honest card numeral shows a changed digit | 0-1000 ms (0-500 ms unless the HUD skips a boundary, HS-47) | 0-60 ticks (`HONEST_TICK_MS` 500, HUD-owned) | Mean 250 ms; at most `1000 / HONEST_TICK_MS` transitions per rolling second, 2 at 500 ms (D.3; the floor is the HUD's `honest_tick_floor_ms`); the numeral never shows a value the room did not have |
| Tremor appears after a physical stress begins | at most 250 ms | at most 100 ms predicate refresh plus 150 ms onset | The predicate is refreshed once per Graph step (IW-5); `TREMOR_ONSET_MS` 150 |
| Gleam, wander and sound appear after a lie begins | 0 frames added by Instruments | 0 | The Filter owns onset and the sound starts no later than the glide (HR-5); Instruments only draw what `perceived()` returns in the same frame |
| Camera cut, zoom step, resize | 1 frame | 1 | Every placement and the card side are re-chosen by a cut (E-IW-26); no settle fires |
| Pause | 1 frame | 1 | `present_time` freezes, the needle freezes mid-motion; an open card closes on the context change and stays closed on resume (E-IW-25, E-IW-60) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2)* |
| Movement input while the card is open *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | the card closes and the avatar moves in the same frame | 1 frame | No press is swallowed (IW-25, E-IW-29, OQ-IW-17) |

### Animation Feel Targets

Frame data. "Startup" is the delay between the cause and the first visible change; "active" is the visibly moving span; "recovery" is the span in which the thing is still committed (here: none, because every instrument motion ends exactly at rest). Frames are 60 Hz.

| Animation | Startup frames | Active frames | Recovery frames | Feel goal | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Honest detent ramp (a quantum crossing) | 0 | 6 (100 ms) | 0 | A mechanical click: step, then rest | Linear through `tick_alpha` (the one PR-8 interpolator); at most 1.7 px on DIAL_L; no overshoot, no extrapolation (D.1) |
| Honest fast ramp (decompression) | 0 | continuous | 0 | A needle falling with the room | A quantum crossed every step reads as a sweep; no settle fires because `settle_eligible` is false for atmosphere quantities (and one quantum is under `INSTR_SETTLE_JUMP_PM` 40 in any case) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| Settle (`load` dial only; 40 pm or more accumulated within 400 ms of leaving rest) | 0 | 24 (400 ms), overshoot peak at frame 13 (220 ms), 2 % at frame 18 (299 ms, exact) | 0 | Heavy, damped, one soft bounce, then exactly still | Overshoot 4.6 % of the jump at the defaults, at most 5 %; reaches 1 px of overshoot only for a jump of 216 pm (DIAL_L) or 288 pm (DIAL_S) or more (D.4); reduced motion: a 1-frame step |
| Re-based settle (a change during a settle) | 0 | restarts a 24-frame window from the drawn value | 0 | A needle nudged again, no jump | One triple, velocity discarded, a small kink accepted (IW-11 (d)) |
| Tremor onset | 0 | 9 (150 ms) ramp to full | n/a | A hull shuddering, the gauge feeling it | Two incommensurate sines in 6-10 Hz, gain 1.5, offset at most plus or minus `A_eff` (2 art px at `k` 1, else 1) |
| Tremor decay | 0 | 36 (600 ms) | 0 | The shake dying away, resting on a pixel | `TREMOR_DECAY_MS` 600; continuous across a re-rise |
| Lie slew and wander (Filter) | 0 | continuous, never at rest | n/a | A needle that will not settle, with nothing causing it | 0.5 px/s slew floor plus 2.2 px wander at 0.40-0.45 Hz (PF D.2, D.4); at least one tip-pixel flip in every 1 s window; the Filter's, drawn here | *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*
| Card open | 0 | 0 (a cut) | 0 | Blunt and immediate | No scale, fade, slide or pop (HR-13); the needle is already at its value and phase |
| Card close | 0 | 0 (a cut) | 0 | Blunt and immediate | Same frame as the cause |
| Card numeral grid boundary | 0 | 1 (a glyph-cell substitution) | 0 | A digit changing, not counting | One luminance event however many digits change; never a count-up or a tween |
| Alarm mark pulse | 0 | period 120-180 frames (2-3 s), about 50 % duty | 0 | A slow beacon, not a strobe | Never faster than 3 Hz; solid under reduced motion |
| Pip on / off, glyph, machine-frame swap | 0 | 1 (a frame swap) | 0 | A relay clicking | No cross-fade (ART 7.2) |
| Dead frame | 0 | 1 | 0 | A gauge that is simply broken | Static; no motion of any kind |

### Impact Moments

There is no hit-stop, no screen shake, no camera impact, no controller rumble and no time-scale change in this system, by decision: HR-13 forbids HUD shake and luminance flashing above 3 Hz, a hit-stop on a reading would be a state change with no world cause (P1), and the instrument is read in a held moment of stillness. The punctuation is carried by the needle and by silence.

| Impact type | Duration | Effect description | Configurable? |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hit-stop (freeze frames) | none | not used | n/a |
| Screen shake | none | not used; no HUD element moves under camera jolt, `world_to_hud` reads the unjolted pose (HR-13; PIPE C11) | n/a |
| Camera impact / rumble / time-scale | none | not used | n/a |
| Settle overshoot | 220 ms to peak, 400 ms total | The one "impact" of an honest instrument, drawn on the bus-load dial only (`settle_eligible`): a breaker closing, a module installed or a SCRAM lands as a needle that overshoots by under 5 % and comes to rest, as a visible pixel only for a step of 216 pm or more (smaller steps draw a slower ease-out). It says "the ship changed; the instrument caught up" (ART 7.3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | `INSTR_SETTLE_ZETA`, `INSTR_SETTLE_OMEGA`, `INSTR_SETTLE_MS` inside the joint lint (D.4) |
| First tremor | 150 ms onset | The first sign that the hull is really under stress; the instrument's share of the 10-30 s tell (P2) | `TREMOR_ONSET_MS`, `TREMOR_GAIN`, `TREMOR_AMP_PX` |
| The lie's first frame | 0 added | The needle starts its never-resting slew from where it stood, the violet fringe fades up and the dry instrument sound begins; there is no announcement | the Filter's onset, not tunable here |
| Alarm mark appearing | 1 frame, then a 2-3 s pulse | A sparse red mark on the housing, the only red on a face-bearing prop; never the first warning | `ALARM_MARK_WORLD_PX` 16 |
| Card cut and latch sound | 0 transition; a short UI-bus latch | A blunt mechanical click in the Foundry dialect, no pitch change | Audio's mix; the visual is not configurable |

### Weight and Responsiveness Profile

* **Weight.** Heavy and deliberate for the object, light for the lie. The housing, bezel and card plate are solid, chamfered and static (ART 3.4); the needle has mass (one damped bounce, then rest). The lie is thin: it has none of that mass, which is what the player learns to feel. The card is a plate the player holds up to a light, not a window that slides in.
* **Player control.** High and continuous. The card runs in real time and is never a pause: the player can close it at any moment at no cost, the sim keeps ticking, the HUD critical strip stays live and a breach is felt through the consequence channel (IW-25). The only constraint is that the avatar stands still while the card is open: the first step closes the card and moves in the same frame (IW-25), so reading spends light and stillness (P3) without ever eating an input *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; OQ-IW-17 records the ratified Controller amendment (D94) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
* **Snap quality.** The interface is crisp and binary (cuts: the card opens and closes, the pip, glyph and frames swap, the DEAD frame appears); the needle is smooth and analogue but rounded to whole art pixels, so its motion is a series of firm one-pixel steps rather than a blur. A numeral changes a whole digit at a time on a fixed grid.
* **Acceleration model.** Simulation feel, not arcade: a jump is a damped second-order response (instant start, soft arrival), a slow change is a detent, a stress is an envelope that ramps in over 150 ms and out over 600 ms. Nothing starts from zero and nothing eases for decoration.
* **Failure texture.** Fair and quiet. An instrument cannot be "failed" by the player; the failure mode is trust. A player who reads one lying gauge and acts on it is hurt by the consequence channel (a drip, a draught, a hiss, a breach), never by a game-over card, and the read on why is always available: the violet edge, the needle that would not rest, the dry sound, the second instrument that disagrees. The game never explains; it only makes the explanation findable.

### Feel Acceptance Criteria

Specific and checkable by a playtester without instruments; each is traced to a criterion in Acceptance Criteria (group J).

- [ ] Unprompted, a first-session player describes an honest needle as "settling", "stepping" or "catching up" and a lying one as "wandering", "drifting" or "won't settle", and a tremor as "shaking" (AC-IW-52, AC-IW-90).
- [ ] Opening and closing the card never feels like a menu: no tester uses the words "slow", "laggy", "floaty" or "unresponsive" about it, and none reports a press that "did nothing" (AC-IW-91, measured from the input log).
- [ ] A tester shown a settle (a closed breaker, 40 pm or more) and a quantum crossing side by side can say which one is the jump; neither is described as "rubbery" (AC-IW-92).
- [ ] No tester reports the tremor as a lie, or a lie as a tremor, after a 10-minute session (AC-IW-93).
- [ ] The card follows its instrument under camera drift with a plate offset of 0 HUD px per frame (no lag, slide or detach), and never covers the instrument, the avatar or the beam's origin (AC-IW-94).
- [ ] A capture at the rim of the beam shows the card closing once, with 0 frames visible after the light falls and 0 reopen events (AC-IW-95).
- [ ] The honest numeral never feels like a flicker: a breach that changes it faster than the grid shows the then-current value at most 1000 / `HONEST_TICK_MS` times per rolling second (2 at 500 ms; never above `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` 3; fewer when the HUD skips a boundary; lag at most `2 x HONEST_TICK_MS`) (AC-IW-96).
- [ ] With reduced motion on, a tester can still tell a trembling honest gauge from a lying one by the held notch and the lie's motion (AC-IW-97).

## UI Requirements

*This system has real UI, and it has it in two places: the instrument nodes on the walls and machines (layer 0, lit, part of the picture) and the inspect card (layer 2, HUD-hosted). It has no menu, no screen, no overlay and no panel of its own. Everything the player sees is a reading of one specific instrument, shown where that instrument is. The tables use the template's columns (Information, Display Location, Update Frequency, Condition) and add the owner and the source.*

### UI-1. What is displayed, where and when

| Information | Display location | Update frequency | Condition | Owner and source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A gauge reading (needle or meniscus against fixed zone bands) | the wall or panel node of the instrument, layer 0, art px x `k` | every render frame (`perceived` pulled once; motion by `present_time`) | readable only where the lit renderer lights the face (beam, fixture, lantern pool, Chemlight); not gated in code (IW-3) | Instruments; value from `perceived(ref, local PlayerId)` (IW-5) |
| The same reading, precisely | the inspect card: label, the same dial or glass, one numeral with its unit | needle every frame; numeral on the HUD hold grid `HONEST_TICK_MS` (honest) or `T_tick` (lying) | a lit, focused, resolvable INSTRUMENT, after `inspect`, until a close cause (IW-24) | Instruments (content); HUD (frame, `Theme`, gleam helper) |
| The inspect prompt ("R . Inspect", RT on gamepad) | a standard HUD plate at `world_to_hud(anchor_px)` | per render frame while focused | the focused INSTRUMENT is lit (`PROMPT_SHOW_DEBOUNCE_TICKS` 13 to show, HUD-owned, hides at once) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | Interaction (grammar), HUD (drawing); the verb key `verb_inspect` is requested (IW-21) |
| The module's state glyph (run / stop) | the module housing, layer 0 | on a Graph step (state read once per `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6) | stage-2 lies swap it; always shown on a lit lie-capable module | Instruments (Presenter, IW-17) |
| The module's own state frames and wear (`running` / `stopped`, `whole` / `cracked`, scuffs, patch plate) | the module sprite, layer 0 | on a Graph step | always; the lit renderer decides readability | Instruments selects, Item DB owns the art (IW-17, IW-18) |
| The housing pip (a running loop) | the instrument's fitting plate, emissive layer | on a Graph step (`served_pm` crosses 0) | the loop serves; absent for the hull loop | Instruments (IW-4) |
| The alarm mark | the instrument housing (DIAL_L), a machine or console panel, a tank prop, only where the alarm-eligible subject itself is | on a Graph step | `alarm_eligible` on the bound subject (LS-13); never on a DIAL_S, an instrument whose `mount_room` differs from its `subject_room` (REMOTE) or the card (IW-19 decision table) | Instruments (IW-19) |
| The DEAD frame | the instrument's wall node | on a binding change | the bound node is DESTROYED, SALVAGED or ABSENT, or the ref is unresolvable | Instruments (IW-30) |
| The stress notch | the bezel (world) or above the dial (card) | on a Graph step | reduced motion on, the IW-12 predicate holds and the reading is HONEST | Instruments (IW-27) |
| The gleam (a lie's violet edge) | glass edge, needle edge, housing edge, digit-well edges | every render frame | the Filter returned a leak (`leak_amplitude_pm` above 0) for the ref | Instruments draw, the Filter supplies amplitude and phase (IW-16) |

### UI-2. Surface inventory and ownership

| Surface | Layer | Owned by | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Wall and panel dials, level sight-glasses, glyphs, pips, marks, wear, DEAD frames, machine-frame selection | 0 (world, lit, y-sorted) | Instruments | wall gauges take the host face's sort origin (PIPE:129, :137) |
| The inspect card | 2 (HUD root viewport, `CanvasLayer`, group `hud`) | contents Instruments; frame register HUD | one per client (one seat per client); seat-local, never replicated |
| The card's frame, `Theme`, tabular-numeral label and gleam-on-glass helper | 2 | HUD & Suit Layer | HS-57; bit-identical with the Filter on or off; never gleams |
| The prompt plate | 2 | Interaction (grammar) and HUD (drawing) | Instruments supply the focus and the verb only |
| The critical strip, vitals panel, tool rail, field-check reading | 2 | HUD & Suit Layer | **not** Instruments: the field-check / scanner reading is a hand-tool reading (IW-35, K-9); Instruments supply only the honest-digit mechanism as a reusable component |
| Sound captions of a lying instrument (Alpha) | 2 | Adaptive Audio / HUD | text built from `Perceived.displayed_value` only, never `true_value` (IW-27) |

### UI-3. Forbidden surfaces (the scope guard as a UI list)

None of the following exists anywhere in this system, under any setting, difficulty or accessibility option: a schematic, overlay, minimap, ship-status screen, loop-summary screen or "lit schematic console" (rejected, R16, LSG:51 anti-pillar 3); a numeral, digit or text on a world gauge; a closed ring or progress ring (curves are reserved for dial arcs and needles, ART 3.4); a condition word (OK, FAILED, DEGRADED, WORN, LOW, CRITICAL), bar, rating or loot value on an instrument or the card; a weld-preview number or seam readout (`surge[]` is never printed); a sanity meter, lie counter, "lies spotted" statistic, stage name or sanity-correlated icon, colour or number; an Attention, threat, proximity or direction indicator, and a reading of a crewmate or a horror (AT-17; HE:377); a reason string for a refusal; the true value beside a lying one outside the Debug Overlay; a "last known value" ghost; a navigation control between cards; a pod dash. A lint over the scene and a retained-screenshot sweep enforce this (AC-IW-1).

### UI-4. Input, focus and gamepad rules

* **Dual focus (Godot 4.6, `docs/engine-reference/godot/modules/ui.md`).** Mouse and touch focus is separate from keyboard and gamepad focus. Every instrument and card `Control` is therefore mouse-ignoring (`mouse_filter` IGNORE) and non-focusable (`focus_mode` NONE), recursively, in `gameplay` and in `inspect_card`; the in-world focus model is the Controller's (PC-6: aim-weighted proximity, `REACH_PX` 72, `FOCUS_STICKY_MARGIN` 0.10) and never calls `Control` focus. A focusable card could steal gamepad focus from a menu or from the chat; hovering the card changes nothing (AC-IW-53).
* **One action, no navigation.** `inspect` (`R` / RMB, RT on gamepad, the Controller's binding) opens the card on the focused lit INSTRUMENT; `inspect`, `cancel` and B close it. There is no cycle, next or previous control and no cursor-picked instrument (HR-14 is met by proximity focus plus this one contextual action). `interact` on an instrument does nothing.
* **Context.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* In `inspect_card` movement intent is zeroed on entry and any movement past the dead-zone closes the card and moves the avatar in the same frame (IW-25; provisional on the Controller's context-matrix amendment), zoom stays reachable, pause and `chart_table` close the card (E-IW-60) and a resume does not restore it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2)*, the sim keeps ticking and the HUD critical strip stays live; `set_aim` stays live (PC-16, closed). The Adapter, not Instruments, owns the context (K-1).
* **Cursor.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* The OS cursor is hidden and inert in `inspect_card` (HS-52 and PC-16, both refreshed 2026-10-27): the card has no clickable element, and with `set_aim` live on a mouse a visible cursor would be a second pointer over the beam. OQ-IW-21 is closed.
* **Gamepad.** The prompt shows the gamepad glyph token for the bound device (Controller's token, HUD's drawing); no radial, no cursor; the card is closed with B.

### UI-5. Display rules that bind the layout

* **Placement.** The card is one opaque Ground plate at HUD scale `H_card` (D.6), 72x72 HUD art px at `CARD_MAG` 1, snapped to the HUD art-px grid, edge-clamped inside the 5 % safe margin, beside its instrument through `world_to_hud` (the unjolted pose), never covering the instrument it magnifies, the avatar (the chest disc, `CARD_KEEPOUT_CHEST_R`) or the beam corridor (`CARD_KEEPOUT_BEAM_HALFW`); it follows its anchor per frame under camera drift and re-chooses its side only on a cut (IW-23).
* **Size.** `H_card` is capped so the plate is at most `CARD_MAX_FRAC_H` 0.5 of the frame height (D.6): 216 px at 1080p `H` 3, 432 px (40 %) at `H` 6; 360 px (50 %) on 720p at `H` 6; 216 px (27 %) on the Steam Deck at its default. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Accepted, not capped further:** the cap already exists (`CARD_MAX_FRAC_H`), and a plate that covers up to half the frame height at the moment the player stands still in a horror game is the large-scale case; it is documented here and carried as a playtest question (OQ-IW-45: at `H` 6 on 720p does the player feel exposed or blind while the card is open, and does a lower `CARD_MAX_FRAC_H` of 0.4, the bottom of its range, fix it). The card is a cut, closes on the first step and costs nothing to close (IW-25), which is the mitigation.
* **Text tier.** Numeral cap 7 art px (21 screen px at `H_card` 3) and label cap 5 art px (15 screen px at `H_card` 3) meet the HR-12 floors; text sits on opaque Ground only (HR-2); labels wrap to a second row and are never truncated (IW-37).
* **HUD division.** The card is not counted in the HUD's at-rest non-Ground cap (HS-56 measures "at rest" with no prompt, mark or notice; the card exists only while a lit instrument is inspected); its own share (about 0.67 % of a 1080p frame at `H` 3) is stated here so the HUD's UX spec can record the exclusion. The card's honest numeral rides the HUD's shared grid boundary and adds no event (HS-47).
* **Reduced motion and accessibility.** Settle becomes a step; tremor becomes the held notch; the alarm mark holds solid; the Filter scales glide, wander and tick (PF-39). No setting removes the gleam, a lying motion mode or the card numeral, and none adds an Attention or sanity surface. The screen-reader text for a card (Alpha) is built from `Perceived.displayed_value` only; the pinned engine's accessibility live-region maturity is `[UNV]` (Accessibility Settings undesigned).
* **Colour.** Nothing on the card or a face depends on hue alone: zone bands are coded by pattern, the loop by a silhouette, the lie by motion and sound as well as the violet edge (ART 4.7; HR-5).
* **Locale.** All strings are `tr()` StringName keys (the label and the unit); the bitmap instrument face covers the shipped Latin set and a script it lacks degrades the label and unit only, never the card (IW-37, OQ-IW-14) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

### UI-6. Screens and spec documents owed

| Screen / surface | Spec | Evidence gate |
|---|---|---|
| Instrument family (dial, sight-glass, glyph, pip, mark, DEAD, wear) at every zoom rung | `design/ux/instruments.md` (owed before epics, LSG:1003) | retained screenshots in `production/qa/evidence/` (AC-IW-48) |
| The inspect card, all variants (dial, linear, wrapped label), at `H` 3 and `H` 6 on each display | `design/ux/instruments.md` | retained screenshots incl. the Steam Deck case and the 720p capped card (AC-IW-48) |
| The lying-dial scene (wall and card side by side, colour and greyscale) | `design/ux/instruments.md` | retained screenshots (AC-IW-25, AC-IW-48) |
| Pattern library entries (zone band patterns, the gleam, the digit cell, the stress notch) | `design/ux/interaction-patterns.md` (shared with HUD) | review |

📌 **UX Flag — In-World Instruments & Inspect View**: This system has UI requirements. In Phase 4 (Pre-Production), run `/ux-design` to create a UX spec for the instrument family and the inspect card **before** writing epics: `design/ux/instruments.md` (the DIAL_L and DIAL_S faces and mounts, the LINEAR sight-glass, the state glyph, pip, alarm mark, DEAD and wear frames, the three zone patterns, and the inspect card in every variant: label, dial or glass, numeral, wrapped label, gleam, stress notch, placement and keep-outs at every `H` and display), with the shared components (Ground plate, frame register, keycap, digit cell, gleam helper) in `design/ux/interaction-patterns.md`, defined by the HUD & Suit Layer's UX spec (`design/ux/hud.md`), which owns the frame register, the Ground plate, the keycap, the focus bracket, the tabular digit cell and the gleam-on-glass helper (HS-48); `design/ux/instruments.md` consumes them and owns only the card's content layout *(decided 2026-10-27, closing the 'whichever comes first' circularity)*. Stories that reference UI should cite `design/ux/instruments.md`, not the GDD directly. Retained screenshots of each surface, at sanity stage 0 and at forced stage 3 for a lying dial, in `production/qa/evidence/` are the evidence gate (AC-IW-25, AC-IW-48).

### X-6. ADR candidates named (none written; no ADR exists)

1. Instrument scene and node architecture: the single-scene-two-scales rule; a presenter manager versus per-node `_process`. 2. Closed-form settle versus an integrated spring, and the PR-8 exemption (K-2). 3. `quantity` enumeration and `true_value` kinds (with the Graph and the Filter). 4. Gleam transport and compositing (per-instance parameter, hue held fixed under coloured light; shared with the Filter's spike S-1 and OQ-PF-6). 5. Instrument data format `assets/data/instruments/`, its lints and the build tool that generates faces and zone arcs from LS-7 band names. 6. The Adapter-to-Instruments card-context signal (with the Controller). 7. Needle rendering: pre-rendered frames indexed by tip pixel (baseline decided 2026-10-27, IW-10) versus a rotated sprite under NEAREST (fallback), and the one-`InstrumentPresenter`-per-instrument rule (IW-5). 8. Digit mechanism: glyph-cell substitution versus a font. 9. A presentation-local hash shared with the Filter (PF-37 precedent) for tremor. 10. The rest-in-time, jump-window and reversal-dwell scalars as presentation-local state (IW-11), so the PIPE owner can acknowledge them with K-2 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.
